# SLICE-001 Implementation Plan

> **Status:** IN PROGRESS. Written on 28 October 2026 after [the readiness check](SLICE-001-READINESS-CHECK.md). The Product Owner decided D-01 to D-14 in the question dialogs of 29 October 2026 and authorized the build order recorded in [§11](#11-decisions-recorded); [§10](#10-owner-resolutions--the-status-of-every-item) holds the status of each, and [§1.2](#12-current-position) says where the work stands.
> **Repository path:** `delivery/planning/SLICE-001-IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md`
> **Owns:** the order, bounds, dependencies and evidence of the work that builds [SLICE-001](../slices/SLICE-001.md), and the register of the checks that work will owe.
> **Does not own:** any product rule. The slice, the [features](../../features/README.md), the [contracts](../../contracts/CLAUDE.md) and the [architecture](../../architecture/SECURITY_DESIGN.md) own those, and a task links to them and never restates them.
> **Companions:** [the readiness check](SLICE-001-READINESS-CHECK.md) · [the SLICE-000 plan](SLICE-000-IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md), whose rules, structure and mechanisms this plan reuses · [Development Execution Plan](../DEVELOPMENT_EXECUTION_PLAN.md) · [Bootstrap B0 Runbook](BOOTSTRAP_B0_RUNBOOK.md) (the handoff format and stop conditions this plan extends)

## 1. How to use this plan

Work on **one task at a time**, in the order of [§4](#4-stages-gates-and-the-order-of-work). Hand a coding agent one block of [§5](#5-the-tasks), never the slice:

```text
Implement task S1.NN of delivery/planning/SLICE-001-IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md.
Read the block, the documents it names and nothing else.
Change only the files it lists; add no operation, table or dependency it does not name.
Write the failing tests first, test as the SLICE-000 plan's §3 testing cadence says, and report the actual results.
Stop at its stop conditions. Do not start another task.
```

Avoid *Implement SLICE-001*, *Build the pickup flow* and *Finish the backend*. A coding agent executes a task; it does not decide Melarc product policy, and a value this plan calls provisional is a measurement or an owner decision, not an agent's choice.

**What a task block is.** Each block names what the task needs, what it builds on that already exists and is tested, what it newly builds, the files, the smallest meaningful validation, the evidence state it reaches, the obligations it carries, what it does not do and when it must stop. A task that lists D, W, E, L, A or J is not done on `pnpm test` alone.

**What this plan is not.** It is not an approval, a schedule, a claim that any check has run, or a replacement for the criteria. A criterion is met by integrated behaviour ([definition of done](../../standards/definition-of-done.md)); a task, a fixture or a green unit test is not.

- **S1.22 (2 November 2026, not committed): the rider starts the run it was given, arrives at its stops and reads a stop with its `ETag`; built and verified in the API, reviewed by an independent reader, and corrected on the Product Owner's review of the same day.** Migration `0065` (the first policies, grants and guards that let a rider's context write: the moment of the start on a manifest and the moment of the arrival on a stop, each held by a guard for every writer; the ledger's rider arm taking `pickup.manifest.started` and `pickup.stop.arrived`; and, from the correction, the rules below), the contract `5.84.0-slice-001-run-execution-correction` with the regenerated client, `startRun` (`If-Match`, the day rule of OB-S1-31 on the hub's clock, one row written), `arriveAtPickupStop` (`Idempotency-Key`, any order of the stops, one row written) and `getPickupStop` (the stop of the manifest read and its `ETag`), in `pickup-run-execution.service.ts`, `pickup-manifests.service.ts` and three controllers. A rider who acts on work that is not its own is told `NOT_ASSIGNED_RIDER`, the same for a colleague's run, a draft and an id that matches nothing; the reads say `NOT_FOUND`. **The correction (the Product Owner's review, applied the same day):** (1) **OB-S1-38 is decided and closed:** the cancellation of a request stays immediate and atomic, and the database holds the invariant for every writer: a `CANCELLED` request and a stop that holds it and is `PENDING` or `ARRIVED` never coexist, checked at the end of the transaction on both tables, so the arrival judges the stop alone; an arrival that commits first is kept by the cancellation that follows it, and one that meets a cancellation that committed first is refused (both orders tested deterministically). (2) **The key of the cancellation is held to the skip** by the stop's guard (place, claim and arrival kept). (3) **A manifest is not completed while a stop is to be worked,** before any completion path exists. (4) **OB-S1-39 is decided and closed:** `startRun` refuses a run with nothing left to work with the declared `MANIFEST_EMPTY` (`422`), the run staying dispatched, with no event. (5) **`getPickupStop` is built** (staff by the hubs of the grant with an audited crossing, a rider through its assigned and dispatched run, a probe policy and a record-scope entry for the stop, a vendor `PERMISSION_DENIED` until OB-S1-23). **Checked:** `contract:check` (66 operations, no findings), `api-client:check`, `db:check`, build, typecheck, lint and format (`precheck` 8 of 8); the unit tests of the areas reached (109 files, 3,737 passed); 51 database files (1,865 passed) and five alone files (317 passed); the Ops Portal's wire-shape guards (1,235 passed); the root document tests (1,478 passed, 0 failed); 34 break checks of the correction, 33 killed and 1 survivor that is one layer of a pair (the first build's 58 are in the block). **Findings for the Product Owner** ([S1.22's block](#s122--start-the-run-and-arrive-at-a-stop)): the empty start answers the catalogue's `MANIFEST_EMPTY` (say if you prefer `STATE_CONFLICT` with a rule); the vendor's read of its own stop, which the contract's text promises, is not built (OB-S1-23); the end-of-transaction rule checks what a transaction writes and not what is already there (a read-only query to look first is in MIGRATION_AND_SEEDING.md); and the stop-lock approach is not extended to the collection and custody acts.
- **S1.23 (2 November 2026, not committed): Ops skips a stop, the system completes a run in progress with nothing collected, and the Vendor is told; built and verified in the API.** Migration `0066`, contract `5.85.0-slice-001-stop-skip` and the regenerated client. `skipPickupStop` (`pickup.stop.skip`, `If-Match` = the stop's `ETag`, an `Idempotency-Key`): an active `PICKUP_STOP_SKIP` code with its note where the code requires one; the stop is `SKIPPED` with its place, its arrival and its claim kept and the request untouched (no attempt consumed, no release of the claim); the note is Ops's own and `STOP_COLUMNS` hides it from every non-staff principal, and so from every `ETag` a rider holds. `completeRunIfSettled` (`src/pickup/pickup-run-completion.ts`) completes a run in progress at the moment its last stop turns terminal with nothing collected, in the transaction of the act (the skip, the cancellation of a request; S1.24 adds the two resolutions of a handover), with that act's actor, `detail.completed_by` `SYSTEM` and the cause, and **the database holds it for every writer** (the keys that may, the columns, nothing collected, no handover pending). `startRun` now locks the stops after the manifest, so a run is never put into progress past a pickup that is being resolved, and the order of locks that makes the completion safe is written in [state-machines.md §4](../../contracts/state-machines.md#4-pickupmanifest). The Vendor's notice is an envelope in the transaction of the skip (`PICKUP_SKIPPED_VENDOR_FEED`, a feed entry; the new closed `notification.parameters` carries the reason and the requested action as tokens, and the lane copies only the members the message declares) and `vendor.pickup_skip.notified`; its wording is a draft until the Product Owner approves it (OB-S1-40) and an individual sender's pickup has no Vendor and no notice (OB-S1-41). The master specification's §31.2 gained the row for it (the event was signed by `MSC-DEC-412`). **Checked:** against a real PostgreSQL and the real application, `pickup-stop-skip` (20), `pickup-stop-skip-policies` (29), `pickup-run-completion` (14: the completion once under two skips at the same moment, a skip against a cancellation, a start against a skip, the ordering of a manifest's stops against a skip, a second start of a run in progress that never waits, and a deterministic test that the stops are counted after the manifest's lock), `notification-parameters` (25); the unit tests of the vocabulary, the message list and the declaration of a record scope; **break checks: 19 changes to the migration and the services, 11 killed**, and each of the 8 that survive is one layer of a pair or a triple that refuse the same statements (the stop's read policy carries the hub; the stop's `skip_facts` and `moment_facts` constraints and the snapshot rule carry the state; the generic transition table, the branch rule and the settle policy carry the manifest's state), shown by removing both layers where that was cheap and by the survivors' own refusals otherwise. **Not built here, by the Product Owner's decision of 2 November 2026:** the Vendor's read of its own stop (OB-S1-23).
- **S1.24 (2 November 2026, not committed): the run custody handover; built and verified in the API.** Migration `0067` (the table `run_custody_handover`, its policies, guard and holder function, the receiving rider's visibility of the run under the accepting key, the manifest's guard refusing a completion over a pending handover, and the audit ledger's rider arm for `fleet.custody.accepted` and the completion it causes), contract `5.86.0-slice-001-run-custody-handover` and the regenerated client, and the new module `src/fleet/` (the `fleet` permission domain, an existing one, as the placement rule puts the acts there). `initiateRunCustodyHandover`, `acceptRunCustodyHandover`, `failRunCustodyHandover`, `listRunCustodyHandovers` (with `manifest_id` and `state` filters) and **`getRunCustodyHandover`, added here: the contract says the slice that builds an act adds the read whose `ETag` it takes**. One open handover per run (an index); the origin is the run's custody holder (`melarc.run_custody_holder`), read and never sent; opening moves nothing; acceptance makes the receiver the holder, records a count that seeds nothing, keeps the run's rider and the stops worked before; Ops accepts only a handover to the hub; a rider is told `NOT_ASSIGNED_RIDER` in one answer for any custody that is not its own (a new `record.surfaces` option of the platform lets `acceptRunCustodyHandover` ask staff which hub and leave the rider to the operation). A resolution can complete a run with nothing collected, in the receiving rider's own transaction too. **The locks this task takes are reviewed and stated** (the run, then the handover; the completion takes the run after the stop; [state-machines.md §4](../../contracts/state-machines.md#4-pickupmanifest)) and tested against the acts they meet. **Checked:** `run-custody-handover` (28: the whole flow of AC-67 and AC-68, the completion on a resolution in either order and at the same moment, the reads), `run-custody-handover-policies` (48: every rule the database holds for every writer, the ledger arm included), the negative-access sweep (237, with the custody operations and an arranged handover), the architecture test that pins that `fleet` and `pickup` import nothing of each other and meet through two ports; **break checks: 23 changes to the migration and the services, 22 killed** (two survivors of the first pass were weak tests, now strengthened and killed), the remaining survivor (the fail key's column rule) a layer of a pair with the checks that tie every other column to the state. **The adversarial review of S1.23 and S1.24 (3 November 2026, eight findings) and what became of each:** the ordering of a manifest's stops racing a skip answered 500 (fixed: it locks the stops after the manifest, and the lock is never taken for a run in progress; a test for each); a parcel count above the column's range answered 500 (fixed: a maximum in the contract and the schema, and a check); the receiving rider's reach was wider than documented (fixed: `melarc.receiver_stands`, a rider stands for a run only while a handover to it is pending or its handover is the latest completed one; five changes to it, all killed); the pending-handover guard holds only for writers that can read the custody table, and three clauses wait for `submitHubHandover` (documented, OB-S1-44); `fleet` imports `pickup` (OB-S1-42); a reason code over 64 characters is dropped from the parameters (judged moot, the validator caps codes at 64); the refusal-order sweep now pins the exact set a rider is served with a body. **Also built here, from the owner's decision on the open stops:** `pnpm --filter @melarc/api run db:open-stops`, the preflight of migration `0065` (read-only, as the cluster administrator, exit 0 clear, 2 found, 1 failed), nine tests and six changes killed; run against `melarc_dev` on 3 November 2026 it found **no** cancelled request holding a stop still to be worked. **The wider database set that the changes reach** (every pickup, custody, notification, audit, migration, policy-structure, registry and rider file, and the sweep): 61 files and 2,143 tests, all passed, on 3 November 2026 (the custody, pickup, notification, registry-grant, policy-structure, migration, refusal-order, audit, commitment-upgrade, slice, reason, rider, negative-access and open-stop files). The API unit tests of the areas reached: 137 files, 4,662 passed and 5 skipped (platform-only skips that were already there), in the routes, OpenAPI, contract, notification, authorization, audit, database, http, tools, architecture, pickup, fleet and root-module areas.
- **S1.25 (3 November 2026, not committed): the Ops screens for manifests and runs, built and verified against the contract; Gate 4 is the Product Owner's.** `/manifests` (the list, with the two filters `state` and `service_date` that OB-S1-25 owed, kept in the address as the pickup queue's are, and recognised by the server alone), `/manifests/new` (the pool, with row selection), `/manifests/{id}` (the stops and their reorder; assign and dispatch as separate acts; cancel before the start; the custody of the run and its handovers) and `/manifests/{id}/stops/{stopId}/skip`. New components: row selection in the queue table, a stop-reordering control, a rider picker. They were built test first by a delegated agent from the contract's generated client and the S1.19 pattern, and **verified here by running the files myself, not taken from its report**: 22 files and 628 tests of the manifest screens, their platform code and their wording; 164 files and 4,102 tests of the guards in test/ and src/platform (the ones against hand-written wire types and role names among them); the ops-web typecheck and ESLint clean; and Chromium on the built app and the dev server (Playwright's own tally for the six projects of the manifests, pickup and messages specs, two workers): **454 ran, 452 passed, 2 failed, 0 skipped, 0 flaky, 0 interrupted**. **The 2 failures are the two instances (beside a table, beside cards) of one test**, `pickup.spec.ts:316` "opens a request in a new tab with a modifier-click, and in place with a plain click", **in `pickup-dev-chromium` only**, both at line 332: the new tab's heading was not visible within 5000 ms. They ran at the same moment, one on each worker. The same test then passed twice alone and passed in a rerun of its whole project (111 of 111). Their traces were lost (the next run clears `test-results`); the run's log is kept. **The cause is not established** (OB-S1-49), and it is not called environmental. **The browser specs run on a stand-in API that `test/manifest-contract.test.ts` holds to the contract, and are not evidence of the integration. The integration is the journey of the integrated harness (`e2e/harness/journeys/manifest-journeys.ts`, in the shared list): the screens, the real API, a real PostgreSQL and the real worker, with synthetic data and the capture sink only: 31 tests of the harness pass in one run of 9.3 minutes on plain HTTP Chromium, the journey of S1.25 among them (14 s), with the stack as separate processes, a real PostgreSQL and the real worker.** The pool of confirmed requests is an arrangement, not a booking, because the harness's database cannot take one (no zone aliases, no saved pickup location, a 10:00 cutoff: OB-S1-02, OB-S1-57). Not run: Firefox and WebKit; any browser spec against the real API; the custody half of the screens against the real API; the gate, the affected run and CI. **Not built:** the Vendor's read of its own stop (OB-S1-23, kept open and excluded from this acceptance by the Product Owner), and any rider screen. **Found and fixed on the way:** the S1.19 queue's recovery from a refused page looked for the name `page_token`, and the real API names it `/page_token` (the service judges the opaque position itself and spells it so); the route tests of both lists now pin what the wire says, and the queue accepts both.
- **S1.26 (3 November 2026, not committed): `recordCollection`, the rider's count at the door; built and verified in the API.** Migration `0068` (the table `collection_record`, its policies, guard and version trigger; the stop kept while a record stands; the rider's key given the lock of its own stop, the short-count reasons, the request of the stop it has arrived at and the individual sender's promotion; the ledger's rider arm), contract 5.86 (the operation as built) and the regenerated client; `src/pickup/collection.service.ts`, `.controller.ts`, `collection-schemas.ts`, `collection-record-view.ts`, `collection-record.schema.ts`. **Written for D-04 option 1: the count makes the record and the stop stays `ARRIVED`; the handshake (S1.27) closes it.** The actual count (never the declared one), `MATCH` or `SHORT` on every collection, the reason of a short one from the `PICKUP_SHORT_COUNT` catalogue (the note it asks for; a photo it asks for is `REASON_REQUIRED` until evidence exists), the office flag raised in the same transaction, a count of zero `ZERO_COLLECTION_IS_FAILURE` and nothing made, a provisional individual sender promoted, replay-safe. **A stop with a record is never skipped, failed or cancelled away** (the plan's own prerequisite, decided here: `STATE_CONFLICT` `collection_recorded` for Ops's skip and the cancellation of the request, in both orders of a race, and the database refuses every other writer). **Checked:** 30 database tests of the act (`pickup-collection.db.test.ts`), 46 of the policies, the guard, the two operations, the promotion's event and the races (`collection-record-policies.db.test.ts`), 24 unit tests of the pure parts; the sweeps (the negative-access and the refusal-order sweep, `recordCollection` in both) and 69 database files (2,552 tests) of the wide regression after the review's corrections, green (the whole API unit project, 261 files and 7,722 tests; the process project, 9 files and 113 tests; the integrated harness, 31 tests with the S1.25 journey among them); 61 mutants of the service, the two acts that skip a stop, the migration, the moment parser and the view: 52 killed at the first run, 6 of the 9 survivors killed by a strengthened test, 2 (Q09, Q15) are a layered pair that the other layer answers, and 1 (U2, the year 0) was retired when the rule moved into the calendar check, which `calendar-day.test.ts` holds. **Found on the way:** the repository's own structural check refused my first form of a policy (a `WITH CHECK (false)` is not a supported form), and the negative-access sweep had grown past the 120 requests a minute that one session is allowed (a code-less 500), so its existence-rule describe takes sessions of its own now. **Also found, and corrected:** three process tests (`test/process.test.ts`, two of `test/contract-check.process.test.ts`) still pinned the 42 operations and 40 paths of the end of SLICE-000, so `pnpm test` failed on them from the first operation of SLICE-001 on; the loop runs the process tests only when start-up code changes, so no task met them. They now read the expectation from the contract and the implemented scope (`test/support/contract-operations.ts`), so they need no edit when an operation is implemented, and they pass on the rebuilt `dist/`. **Corrected on the independent review of 4 November 2026 (three findings in the database, one in the journey):** (1) the record's guard now takes the stop's lock before it judges the stop, so a direct insert and a skip or a failure meet in either order of two real runtime transactions (three tests: a skip waiting for an insert, an insert waiting for a skip, an insert waiting for a statement that failed the stop; each failed first with the second writer never waiting); (2) arrival alone no longer promotes a sender: only `melarc.promote_collected_sender`, for a stop the calling rider has counted, promotes it, and the runtime login holds no `UPDATE` on a sender (tests: a direct update before and after the count is refused, the operation before the count is refused, after it promotes once, for the counting rider only; the record, the promotion and the first event are undone together when the event of the promotion cannot be written, and made together on the retry); (3) the key reads no request and no sender as rows at any time (OB-S1-53 closed: tests of the key's own queries before and after the count, of the direction for the stops it may and may not ask about, and of the grants: the two operations are executable by the API's runtime alone); (4) the custody journey now accepts a count of zero on a run on which nothing was collected, and the positive-count case is pinned apart (OB-S1-59). **Mutation check of the corrections:** 19 mutants of the migration and the service, 16 killed; 3 survive because another layer answers the same break (N04, the owner binding of the sender’s update policy, behind the login’s missing UPDATE privilege and the sender’s owner-bound read policy; N06, the explicit rider test inside the promotion, behind the read policy of the record; N11, the explicit PROVISIONAL test of the promotion’s update, behind the update policy’s USING). **Not built, and why:** the handshake (S1.27); the evidence a photo needs (S1.28); the failure report (S1.28); any rider screen; what the review recommends on OB-S1-43, -48, -50, -51, -52, -54 and -59 (recorded beside each, awaiting the Product Owner). Everything that needs the Product Owner is OB-S1-43, -48, -50 to -52, -54, -55, -59 and -60.
- **Independent review of 4 November 2026 (of the archive `...-s1-26-and-follow-up.zip`; the reviewer ran no application, database or browser, and verified the archive's hashes).** **Accepted within the demonstrated scope:** the S1.25 port refactor (OB-S1-42), the manifest return-to-place (OB-S1-46) and the real-API journey (the screen shows `Completed` but not who completed it or who holds custody after an acceptance, which is OB-S1-56 and stays open). **Held:** S1.26 API acceptance and Gate 4, for three database findings and one product finding. The three database findings are corrected and tested (see the S1.26 bullet above); the product finding is OB-S1-59, awaiting the Product Owner. The review's recommendations on OB-S1-43, -48, -50, -51, -52, -54 and -59 are recorded beside each obligation; none is built.
- **Second independent review of 4 November 2026 (of the archive `...-s1-26-review-corrections.zip`, SHA-256 verified by the reviewer, who ran no database or application).** **Kept:** the acceptance of the S1.25 port refactor, list continuity and the real-API journey. **Held:** S1.26 API acceptance, for two database findings that must be corrected before S1.27 builds the handshake on these records; the first review's three database findings were found corrected, and OB-S1-59 (a custody count against an empty completion) open for operational acceptance. **Corrected (migration `0068`, edited in place; history checked first, below):** (1) **the promotion's event could be manufactured by the correct rider and key**: the ledger's arm trusted a transaction setting (`melarc.promoted_sender`) that any statement of the runtime login can set, and did not bind the cited stop to the promoted sender; now `melarc.promote_collected_sender` writes the event in the change it records (the sender that stop's request names, the stop it was asked about, its hub, the rider and the correlation id of the context) and the ledger admits it to a statement that runs as the owner and to no other, so the service no longer writes it; (2) **a direct insert could store the wrong handshake direction**: the record's guard now asks `melarc.collection_handshake_type` and refuses a record made under the counting key with the other direction (the rider's key still reads no request and no sender). **Proof, test first (six of the new tests failed on the migration as reviewed, each for the reason the review gave; the owner-run test of the arm's conditions and the concurrency test passed at once, and mutants show they can fail):** the marker, a second counted stop and a second event are each refused to the counting key with a genuine count; the event the operation writes equals, field by field, what the application's writer records for the same change; the arm's own conditions are shown under a statement run as the owner (the key, the rider that counted, the stop cited, its hub, the states, the object, the tier); the promotion and its event are one change (a ledger that refuses the event leaves the sender provisional, and the retry makes both; the route-level rollback and retry of the act is unchanged); two counted stops that name one provisional sender, promoted by two transactions at once, write one event; both wrong pairings of direction are refused and both right ones made, as the real runtime rider. **Mutation check:** 19 mutants: 15 of the migration, 3 of the service and 1 race mutant of the promotion; 17 killed. Two survive and are not forced: Q11, the recording rider inside the arm’s own EXISTS (behind the read policy of the record, which shows the owner under a rider’s context only that rider’s records), and Q15, a dead marker setting that changes no behaviour. The race mutant (the promotion made a check then an update, with the update policy’s PROVISIONAL test loosened) is killed by the concurrency test alone, so the sequential tests do not stand in for it. **Checks on the final tree:** the policy file (46 tests, the concurrency test among them), 65 related database files (2,086 tests: the act and its route-level rollback and retry, the run completion, the ledger, the manifests, the notification lane, the fleet and rider files) and the 2 alone files that hold the definer list and the catalogue (108 tests), all passing; the whole API unit project (261 files, 7,722 tests, 7 skipped on this system as before); lint and typecheck clean. Not re-run: the process project and the integrated harness (no start-up, contract or journey code changed, and no journey reaches the count), the whole database suite, the gate and the browsers. **Migration history checked before editing `0068` again:** `melarc_dev` holds 64 migrations (up to 0063; 0064 to 0068 are pending) and its journal agrees with the folder; the four leftover disposable `melarc_test_*` databases stop at 0041 (three) and 0057 (one); one unrelated database (`flock`) is not readable by the migration login and is not a Melarc database; no Melarc database on this machine holds 0068; and migrations 0042 to 0068 are all untracked in git, so no commit, push or CI run can carry it. The check read each database in a read-only transaction through the repository’s own `checkAgainstApplied`, applied nothing and printed no connection value. What it cannot establish is a machine of the Product Owner’s that this one cannot see; `runMigrations` refuses an applied migration whose text was edited, so a database that held the earlier text would stop at its next `db:migrate`, and the change would then be made as a forward migration `0069`. **Recommendations recorded, not built:** OB-S1-59 (the reconciliation rule), the confirmation of the refusal of a counted stop's skip, failure and cancellation, OB-S1-54 (the count's own event, before S1.27) and OB-S1-43, -48, -55 (authority after a hand-over, before the handshake's authorization): each is beside its obligation and awaits the Product Owner. **Found while checking the same class, not changed:** OB-S1-60 (a direct writer under the counting key can still store a reason snapshot, a flag time and a recording time of its own; shown by a probe, not kept as a test).
- **S1.26 closeout (4 November 2026, not committed): the database holds the facts of a collection record, and the count has its own audit event.** *Approved by the Product Owner on 4 November 2026:* OB-S1-60, OB-S1-54, counted-stop protection (a stop with a positive record is never skipped, failed or cancelled away) and the counted-stop authority of S1.27 (an S1.27 requirement, in [the feature, §5.4](../../features/pickup/pickup-collection.md#54-a-stop-that-has-been-counted)); OB-S1-59 stays an operational-release blocker. **Migration `0069`, forward (not an edit of `0068`).** The record's guard holds the reason of a short count to the `PICKUP_SHORT_COUNT` catalogue at the insert (active; its label and attribution as the catalogue holds them; a note where it asks for one; no photo asked for until S1.28 stores evidence; no member of the writer's own) and refuses with `REASON_NOT_ACTIVE` or `REASON_REQUIRED`, which the act answers as the contract's errors: a catalogue that changed between the act's reading and its insert is a controlled refusal that undoes the act and frees the key, and the same request is then a retry. `recorded_at` and a short count's `office_notified_at` are the database's own instants, taken once the stop's lock is held (a writer that waited for the lock is stamped after it); a flag on a clean count is refused by the table's own check; the device's `captured_at` is kept apart. The ledger's arm for the count admits one event, **`pickup.collection.recorded`** (the same for a clean count and a short one: its object the record; the stop, its run and request, the rider and the hub named; the counts, the variance, whether the flag was raised and the direction of the handshake; `ARRIVED` before and after; no note), with every member bound to the stored record and its stop and every other free-form member absent, and admits `pickup.stop.collected` and `pickup.stop.partially_collected` to the counting key never: S1.27 writes them when the handshake closes the stop. The catalogue is 200 codes (its prose counts of the tiers, 78 and 111, were stale and are corrected to 79, 5 and 116). The act writes the count's event, then the promotion (whose event the database writes), then completes the idempotency record, in one transaction. **The migration history was read first**: every database of the local server was looked at read-only (a read-only transaction each, through the repository's own `checkAgainstApplied`, nothing applied). The owner's `melarc_dev` holds `0000` to `0063` (`0064` to `0068` pending, no history problem), four leftover test databases stop at `0041` or `0057`, one unrelated database is unreadable by the migration login, and **no database holds `0068`**; the forward migration rests on the text of `0068` having been given to two reviewers in archives, whose use cannot be established from here. `melarc_dev` and the running stack were not touched. **Evidence.** The probe of 4 November 2026 is now permanent: every case is the runtime login as the assigned rider under the counting key at an arrived stop of its own run, each refusal is shown to be the guard's stated reason, and the same writer is then accepted on the same stop with the genuine facts. They cover an invented, retired and wrong-catalogue reason, a substituted label or attribution, a fact of the writer's own, a note or photo requirement unmet, a snapshot that is not an object, a label that is the text of a number, a catalogue that changed between the reading and the insert (as a database fact and through the real operation, held between the two by a lock), a reason kept intact when the catalogue is edited later, past and future instants given for the recording and the flag, the instants taken after a real lock wait, and the device's moment kept apart. The ledger is tested member by member for a clean and a short count (each member changed on its own with the rest right, the genuine event then accepted by the same writer), for the writer, the key and the other kinds of caller, for the free-form members, for the correlation id, for a stop that has left `ARRIVED`, and for the two events of the close. The route tests cover the events, the note kept out, a ledger that refuses either of the two events (the whole act, the promotion included, is undone, the same key then makes it all, and a replay after that makes nothing more). **Break checks of the closeout.** The first pass over the migration (36 mutants) killed 30; of the 6 that survived, two were checks the table already makes (removed), three were gaps in my tests (closed) and one is a layered pair. The final run over the migration (35 mutants) killed 34, and its one survivor is that layered pair: the recording rider inside the arm's own `EXISTS`, behind the record's read policy, which shows a rider only its own records and has its own test (the same pair as Q11 of the second review). The 4 mutants of the service and the 3 of the reason mapping were all killed. **Not changed, recorded as OB-S1-61:** the ledger holds no uniqueness for the count's event (a rider with a real record could write the same true event again from direct SQL), and `occurred_at` and `recorded_at` of `audit_event` are writer-supplied for every arm of the insert policy. **Checks on the final tree.** `pnpm run precheck`: 8 of 8. The two collection files (78 tests of the policies, the ledger and the instants, and 32 of the real operation), a wider set of 79 database files (2,792 tests: the pickup, custody, notification, audit, migration, policy, registry, rider, vendor, sweep and slice files) and 4 files that run alone (168 tests: the catalogue invariants, the seed's initialization, the runtime role, provisioning), all passed. The API unit and process projects: 271 files, 7,846 passed, 13 skipped (the platform skips that were already there), none failed. The root document and tooling tests: 1,481, with 1,478 passed, 3 skipped and none failed. The first unit run found one failure that was mine, the security matrix's coverage table (it counts its rows, and the new row `GBT-D20` made Family D 20 and the total 305); it is fixed and the file passes. **Not run:** the rest of the database suite, the browsers, the integrated journeys (none reaches the count's event), the gate and `pnpm run test:affected` (whose selection is the whole repository while its baselines are old: the owner's). **Not built, by the Product Owner's order:** the reconciliation of OB-S1-59.
- **S1.27 (4 November 2026, not committed): the collection handshake; built and verified in the API, with the lane's texts.** Migration `0070` (the table `collection_handshake`; the five handshake settings; five `SECURITY DEFINER` operations of the owner that only the API's runtime executes: `handshake_issue`, `handshake_verify`, `handshake_sms_fallback`, `handshake_override` and `handshake_resolve`, and the one the notification lane executes, `handshake_mark_delivered`; the table's guard; the stop's close by the handshake; the policies and the ledger's arms; the owner-run arm of the outbox for the one request for a text), contract `5.87.0-slice-001-collection-handshake` (the three rider-facing operations declare the rider host, the error lists are those the guards and services raise, and each operation has an "as built" paragraph) and the regenerated client. The API: `initiateHandshake`, `verifyHandshake`, `requestSmsHandshakeFallback`, `authoriseHandshakeOverride` and `resolveHandshake` (`src/pickup/collection-handshake.service.ts`, `.controller.ts`, `handshake-view.ts`, `handshake-schemas.ts`). The lane: `HANDSHAKE_CODE_SENDER` and `HANDSHAKE_CODE_VENDOR_NUMBER` are wired (message list, two draft wordings, the reader's four new reads, the code made at the send, the handshake told that the text was accepted). **The code is derived (stop, generation, `credential_delivery` key) and never stored**; the database keeps a keyed digest. The S1.26 closeout's requirements are met: the events of the close are the handshake's, the counted-stop authority is the database's, and a custody transfer gives the receiving rider no handshake authority. *Findings for the Product Owner are the rows OB-S1-62 to OB-S1-65.* *Checks:* see the S1.27 block.
- **S1.29 (4 November 2026, not committed): the collection dispute; built and verified in the API.** Migration `0071` (the table `collection_dispute`, its policies, guard and version trigger, the one `SECURITY DEFINER` operation `melarc.dispute_collection`, and the ledger's vendor arm taking `pickup.handshake.disputed`), contract `5.88.0-slice-001-collection-dispute` and the regenerated client; `src/pickup/collection-dispute.service.ts`, `.controller.ts`, `collection-dispute-schemas.ts`, `collection-dispute.schema.ts`. `disputeCollection` (the vendor whose pickup it is; `201`): the item (`OPEN`), the handshake `DISPUTED` and one event with the vendor's note, in one change; **the stop stays `COLLECTED` or `PARTIALLY_COLLECTED` and the manifest does not wait** (a test completes the run after the dispute). The plan block said "Operations: none"; the contract has had the operation since the reconciliation, so it is built as named, not provisional. No operation resolves an item in `SLICE-001`.

- **S1.28 (4 November 2026, not committed): the failure report; built and verified in the API, in part.** Migration `0072` (the grant, the rider's policies on the stop, the reasons of a failure, the system completion of a run and the reads of its own count and of the custody table; the branch of the stop's guard that judges a failure for every writer, the reason held to the `PICKUP_FAILURE` catalogue and the moment the database's own; the manifest guard's completion key; the ledger's two arms), the operation `reportPickupFailure` (`src/pickup/pickup-failure.*`; contract **5.89.0**, the thirteenth operation of the Rider host; the client regenerated), the cause `LAST_STOP_FAILED` of the system completion, and the rule that a refused collection is not rescheduled by ordinary action and is opened by a Senior Ops extension (`attempt-ceiling.ts`, the reschedule and `extendPickupAttempts`). **Not built, and why: [OB-S1-66](#7-deferred-obligations--what-is-owed-and-not-yet-run)**: the backstop (the stop condition is met: its cadence and process are the owner's, OB-S1-07), the evidence photo (S1.11 is not built; two reasons that ask for one cannot be used), the narrowing of the escalation, and the messages of a failure. A real defect was found by the tests: the rider's failure key could not read its own count, so neither the trigger nor the application found it and a counted stop could be failed (the migration restates the read policy with the key). A break-check of the migration (71 mutants: 55 killed; the 16 that survive are checks that stand behind a policy or another check — the stop policy's assigned-rider and state terms and the guard's run terms, held by the policy's check and the rider's read policy; the guard's state move, held by the state machine; the label type, held by the comparison with the catalogue; four checks of a contact attempt that cover one another; the domain filter of the catalogue lookup, held by the reasons policy; the not-found term; the ledger arm's rider term and the completion arm's, held by what a rider can read) and of the code (36 mutants: 33 killed; the 3 that survive are the category list, held by the schema, the attempt bound, held by the schema's maxItems, and the principal check, held by the route's guard). Eleven mutants that the first run found alive each have a test that kills them now.
- **Review closeout of 5 November 2026 (not committed): the independent review of S1.27 to S1.29 is answered. Migration `0073` (a forward migration: `0070` to `0072` were given to the reviewer and are not edited), contract `5.90.0-slice-001-review-closeout`, the client regenerated.** *The reviewer held S1.27's acceptance, kept S1.28 partial and asked for the deeper review of S1.29's database operation (done: below).* **Fixed:** (1) **A code is texted at the length it was made with.** `collection_handshake.code_length` is kept per generation and frozen with it by the guard; `handshake_issue` and `handshake_sms_fallback` take the length the application used and refuse `CODE_LENGTH_CHANGED` (409 `code_length_changed`) if the setting moved between the read and the write; the lane reads the stored length and no longer reads the setting (its policy arm and its grant are gone). The handshakes that existed took the setting of the migration's day (4 where it cannot be used), which a test proves by running the migration's own block over handshakes in every state and over eight settings; a handshake established with no code records 4, so the officer's override no longer depends on a setting. (2) **The provider-operation ceiling is enforced** (OB-S1-65, closed): the five operations that write a notification envelope declare `providerInitiating: true` (`confirmPickupRequest`, `dispatchManifest`, `skipPickupStop`, `initiateHandshake`, `requestSmsHandshakeFallback`); the authentication step admits the counter right after the session's bucket, on the session, in the limiter's own transaction (never inside the request's); it is the seventh name of the limiter's closed set and not a bucket. A request counts when it is admitted and stays counted whatever the operation answers (a missing token, a validation failure, a refusal for state and the replay of an idempotent request each take a place); one the ceiling refuses takes none and changes nothing. A structural test reads the TypeScript writer of the outbox as a syntax tree and the one SQL function that writes it, and requires the operations they name to be exactly the ones that declare; the routes are proven through the real application, with a burst of fourteen concurrent skips of which exactly the limit are done. (3) **The registered number is the phone of the vendor's credential** (OB-S1-62): owned by the credential, verified by possession, changed by no operation in this slice, and bound to the delivery (the lane is given it by a function of the notification capability, for the request its envelope names and while an unexpired fallback code is live; the owner-run reads of the account and the credential are bound to the organisation concerned; the message log keeps no hint of it). (4) **The door wait is enforced for a vendor-unavailable failure and the contact attempt carries a method and an optional note** (OB-S1-69, OB-S1-67); (5) **an extension says what it did** (OB-S1-68); (6) **`session-issue.db.test.ts` and `authority.db.test.ts` no longer write the fixed policy's revision into a test**: they raised the floor to a number the seed had already reached; they raise it from the stored revision, and the policy the seed has not written (`NOT_SEEDED`), which no test reached, is tested on both paths. *Found by this closeout's own full run of the database project (9 files, 39 tests), and fixed:* the route suites that make dozens of ceiling-covered requests from one session lift the ceiling for their own database (one helper; the ceiling is proven by its own tests), two lists that pin who may execute and read what were completed, and **two tests that could not pass any more for reasons older than the closeout**: `configuration-reader` read at a fixed day before the seed's own timestamp and `mfa-factor-ordinal` seeded an old history with the whole current seed. *Three independent read-only reviews of this closeout (the migration; the application and its tests; the specification), every finding verified before it was acted on.* **Fixed from them:** the arrival the door wait is measured from is the database's own (a writer under the arrival key chose it); the refusal of the door wait is no longer made by asking a transaction the server has aborted (the rider met a server error where it should have met the wait); the override no longer depends on the code-length setting; the backfill no longer stops on a setting that is not a whole number; the number is not given for a code past its life; blank text is judged by any white space; the handshake's foreign key to the request is dropped (it made the first handshake of a stop take a key-share lock on the request while holding the stop, against the cancellation, which takes the request and then the stop: a deadlock); the dispute locks only a stop of the calling vendor and holds the note to the ledger's screening before the database writes it; the message log keeps no hint of the registered number (a first version kept a digit-free hint, which the Ops Portal's strict reader would have refused: the delta review caught it); the stale statements of the documents that the reviewers listed. *Recorded for the owner and not changed:* OB-S1-70 to OB-S1-74 (no operation reads a dispute; the credential messages are outside the ceiling; a text that cannot be sent is not told to the handshake; the pairing of a reason with a category is checked nowhere; two proofs that say less than their criteria). **Break-checks:** the text of migration `0073` that answers the reviews (13 mutants, all killed; one survived as an equivalent mutant and was remade), the guard and the declarations of the ceiling (5, all killed), the session floor and its judge (6, all killed).

- **Checks run for S1.27 to S1.29 on the final tree (4 November 2026):** `pnpm run precheck` 8 of 8 (the cached run; `contract:check` 80 operations, no findings; `api-client:check` current; `db:check`; the audit). `pnpm run test:root`: 1,481 tests, 1,478 passed, 3 skipped. The whole API unit and process projects: 272 files, 7,872 passed, 13 skipped, 0 failed; that run found two things the focused runs had not, both fixed: the registry held two rows for `CollectionDispute` (S1.29 added one beside the existing row; now one row, and the HUB section states 53 tables), and a test that counts the Rider host's operations (now 13). The client's own tests: 9 files, 250 passed. The database tests of the areas the three tasks reach: 91 files (every `pickup-*`, `collection-*`, `run-*`, `notification-*`, `rider-*`, `vendor-*`, `session-*`, `audit-*` and `reason-*` file, the two sweeps, the registry, policy, migration, bootstrap and scope-probe files, `two-builds`, `open-stop-upgrade`, `seed` and `reference-*`), 3,312 tests, 90 files passing. **`session-issue.db.test.ts` fails, and failed before these tasks**: it raises the Vendor fixed policy to revision 2, the build has been at revision 2 since S1.8 (`BUILD_FIXED_POLICY`), and the guard of the table refuses a floor that does not rise. The test is to be moved up by one (floor 3, then revision 4); it is an earlier task's, and this closeout did not change it. The pin of the columns the runtime login may update on a stop (`pickup-manifest-claim.db.test.ts`) was widened by the two failure columns. **Not run:** `pnpm run test:affected` (a migration is in the change, so it selects every project, which is the owner's gate), the gate, the browser, e2e and verifier tests, the ops-web tests (no ops-web file changed; its typecheck passed in the precheck), and a Linux run.
- **Checks run for the review closeout (5 November 2026, the owner's order of that day: fix the review, review adversarially, run everything, commit and push only if everything is green).** *The database project, run whole twice* (the first run found 9 files and 39 tests that could not pass: the ceiling in the route suites, two pinned lists, two time bombs and one extension audit expectation; the second, after every fix: 156 files, 4,981 tests passed, 1 skipped) *and the `database-alone` project* (26 files; it found two more stale pins of the allow-lists that predate this closeout, fixed). *`pnpm run precheck`* 8 of 8 on the final tree (`contract:check` 80 operations, no findings; `api-client:check` current; the lint script now gives ESLint a 6 GB heap, because the type-aware lint of the whole repository needs about 5 GB and Node's default 4 GB ended it with an out-of-memory error, which is a finding about the repository's growth and not about this closeout alone). *Break-checks:* the new text of migration `0073` (13 mutants, all killed; one survived as an equivalent mutant and was remade), the possession condition, the release read, the extension check and the failing write (all killed), the ceiling's guard and declarations (5, all killed), the session floor and its judge (6, all killed). *Four independent read-only reviews* (the migration; the application and its tests; the specification; and the fixes made after the first three), every finding verified before it was acted on; the fourth found a regression the fixes had made (the hint in the message log, which the Ops Portal's strict reader would have refused) and two pinned lists still stale. *The first full gate (`review-closeout-1`, 85 minutes, tree of 2,120 files unchanged while it ran):* format, types, build, contract, lint, the whole unit stage (root 1,481 tests, 1,478 passed and 3 skipped; client 250; harness 546 passed and 3 skipped; Ops Portal 4,189; API unit and process 7,896 passed and 13 skipped), the verifier, `test:db` (181 files, 5,765 tests passed, 7 skipped), the end-to-end journeys (31) and the audit passed; **the browser stage failed on 1 of 1,058 tests** (`messages.spec.ts`, 'tell a filter that hides everything', in the dev-server project): two filter choices made one after the other before the page had taken the first, a race of the test's own that a loaded machine loses; it passed alone three times in three and the choices now wait for the first filter's address. A gate of the final tree follows: its result is in the hand-over and not in this document, because writing it here would change the tree it describes. *Left to CI:* the HTTPS half of the end-to-end journeys (it makes a certificate authority and refuses to run on a developer machine).
- **Next, after S1.19 (1 November 2026):** the pickup queue's presentation is redrawn (six grouped columns, and cards for a narrow room: [COMPONENT_PATTERNS §22f](../../design/COMPONENT_PATTERNS.md#22f-queuetable)); its manual refresh (with the time of the page's last successful check) and its return to the same page, position and request after a visit to a request are built the same day, in memory only ([§22f](../../design/COMPONENT_PATTERNS.md#22f-queuetable)); **the Overview is not the next build.** Its accepted direction is preserved as a reference ([the brief](../../design/OVERVIEW_DASHBOARD_DESIGN.md), [the visual reference](../../design/reference/MELARC_OVERVIEW_REFERENCE_2026-11-01.html)) and builds nothing: no route, operation, placeholder or sample figure. Each domain defines its measures in the reporting data dictionary as it is built, a section is connected only by a bounded task the owner names, and the final integration and acceptance are `SLICE-012`'s ([the plan](../IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md#3-sequence)).
- **Not built, and why:** S1.9 (not needed: D-02 chose the seed). **S1.10b** (the fleet fixtures) is **not built, by the owner's decision of 29 October 2026**: the check that a rider has a serviceable motorcycle (AC-15, `NO_SERVICEABLE_MOTORCYCLE`) moves to the fleet slice, SLICE-011, and until then a rider whose motorcycle is broken can be assigned a run. **S1.11** (evidence) is built with its first user, S1.28: the store is Cloudflare R2, with an in-process stand-in for development and CI, and the mapping from an owner to its permission (`x-permission: owner` has no access kind in the guard) is its first design question. **S1.12** (the notification service) is built, parts a to e (29 and 30 October 2026, below): the outbox and the lane, the message list and its draft wording, the Hubtel client, adapters and stand-in, the controlled window and the smoke command, the receipts, the limits as platform settings, the suppression and the kill switch, the metrics, and the Ops message log (`listNotifications` and its screen `/messages`). An independent review of 30 October found four important and ten minor points; the four and nine of the ten are fixed or recorded, and one is left (below). **Part f (the handshake code store) is not built**: it needs the stop table of S1.20 and the scope of the handshake settings, both named below ([§5](#s112--the-notification-service-outbox-sms-through-hubtel-receipts-and-the-ops-log-gate-2)). **Migrations `0055` to `0057` are applied to the owner's `melarc_dev`**: the owner restarts the API and the worker to load the build, and signs out and in to carry `notification.read` and the home hub. The plan's own graph lets Gate 3 stand without Gate 2; Stage 4 needs S1.12 from S1.21.
- **Values chosen to keep going, now confirmed by the owner (OB-S1-15):** a forced extension permits one further attempt, once per request; the replacement of a failed attempt is dated the next operating day after the later of the source's day and today; a missing or invalid attempt cap blocks nothing and the audit event records that it was not checked; an escalation is a marker, not a state, until S1.28.
- **Open, the owner's:** the SMS wording (`OQ-037`) before any real send; the Hubtel credentials, the window's settings and the owner's run of the smoke command; the legal basis for recipient numbers (`OQ-028`) before the heads-up SMS reaches a real recipient; the real alias list (OB-S1-02); the launch values of the lock idle time and the evidence limits; the R2 account and bucket (OB-S1-05) ([§10](#10-owner-resolutions--the-status-of-every-item)). After the review closeout of 5 November 2026: the confirmation of the field of the registered number (OB-S1-62), of the methods of a contact attempt (OB-S1-67), of the reach of an extension (OB-S1-68) and of the door wait (OB-S1-69); the trust boundary of the code's key (OB-S1-63) and the wording of the two handshake texts (OB-S1-64), both before any real send; and the decisions of OB-S1-70 to OB-S1-74.
- **Checks run:** targeted only, as the owner asked. `pnpm run precheck` (8 of 8 cheap checks), the API unit tests of the pickup, contract, route, audit and database-tooling areas, 15 pickup and schema database test files and the whole-schema database suites that run alone, the root document and bundle tests, the Ops Portal unit tests of the pickup screens and the navigation, and the pickup browser spec on Chromium against the production build and the development server. **Not run:** the full gate (the owner's), `pnpm run test:affected` (its selection is the whole database suite, because the migrations changed), Firefox and WebKit, the hosted run, the Linux run.
- **Checks run for S1.20 and its closeout (1 November 2026):** `pnpm run precheck` 8 of 8, `contract:check` included (no findings). The whole API unit project: 7,486 tests, 7,479 passed, 0 failed, 7 skipped, in its second full run. The first full run failed one test, `window-counter.test.ts` (an `EPERM` on the `mkdir` lock in its temp folder): **it passed in isolation, 3 of 3, and in the second full run; the failure in the first full run is unresolved.** 715 database tests in 18 files (every pickup file, the registry, the migration history and the sweep), the catalogue invariants (54), the client’s tests (250) and the root document and tooling tests (1,478 passed, 0 failed). The Ops Portal unit tests: 3,631 passed, 12 skipped, **one file failed**: the production-build test timed out in its 20-second hook inside a full run of 462 seconds. **It passed alone, twice, in 9 to 10 seconds; the failure in the full run is unresolved** (the machine’s CPU was at 81 % with nothing of ours running, which does not establish the cause). **Not run:** `pnpm run test:affected`, whose dry run chooses the whole repository (250 API unit files, 156 database files, 109 Ops Portal unit files, 12 browser specs, the verifier suite) because each target’s last green record is days old: that is a whole-suite run, the owner’s to start; the full gate; the browser specs; Firefox and WebKit.

## 2. What exists today, and what is future behaviour

Read from the repository on 28 October 2026 (HEAD `6ce206b`), not from the documents. **This section is the baseline of that date and is not updated as tasks land: what exists now is in [§1.2](#12-current-position) and in the carried-obligation table of [§7](#7-deferred-obligations--what-is-owed-and-not-yet-run).** A task in [§5](#5-the-tasks) says which of 2.1 it reuses and which of 2.2 it creates.

### 2.1 Existing and tested — reuse it, do not rebuild it

| Area | What exists | Where |
|---|---|---|
| Sessions and principals | Opaque sessions for staff, vendor and rider; the issuer protocol under policy locks; the fixed Vendor and Rider policies compiled into the build (`BUILD_FIXED_POLICY`) | `src/platform/auth/sessions/` |
| Authorization | Declared access on every route, the guard chain, `@RequiresPermission`, tier rules, record scope and the existence-rule probe (`RECORD_ENTITIES`), `OperationRunner.read` and `.mutate` | `src/platform/authorization/`, `src/platform/routes/` |
| Idempotency, audit, limiter | The principal-, operation- and key-bound idempotency store; the audit writer holding each event to its tier; the PostgreSQL limiter and bounded hash admission | `src/platform/idempotency/`, `audit/`, `limiter/` |
| Settings and reasons | A settings reader with no default (`SETTING_MISSING`) for company and platform settings; the reason validator (`REASON_REQUIRED`, `REASON_NOT_ACTIVE`) | `src/platform/settings/` |
| Delivery | A durable credential-delivery queue and a separate worker that only captures, with a recipient allow-list | `src/platform/delivery/`, `src/delivery-worker-runtime.ts` |
| Rider device protocol | Enrolment, the signed-challenge sign-in, the private attestation verifier, the substitute | `src/platform/auth/rider/`, `apps/attestation-verifier/` |
| Contract machinery | `@ContractOperation`, `contract:check`, the response and request validation, `declared-refusals.ts`, the generated client | `src/platform/contract/`, `packages/api-client/` |
| Reference data | The permission catalogue and six bundles, the `hub` table, company and platform settings, identity reasons, one-shot seed | `src/tools/seed/`, `src/platform/database/schema/reference-data.ts` |
| Ops Portal | The shell, route registry and session boundary; 27 components (`Table`, `ReadState`, `Dialog`, `ReasonConfirmationDialog`, `Select`, `DatePicker`, `StatusBadge` and others); 20 SLICE-000 routes | `apps/ops-web/src/` |
| Tests and harness | The database test kit (`startIntegrationKit`, `admin()`, `observe()`), the integrated harness with its closed lists, the security matrix, the two-build proof, the concurrency harness, the citation inventory | `apps/api/test/`, `e2e/`, `scripts/` |

### 2.2 Does not exist — future behaviour

Confirmed by search of the code on 28 October 2026, not assumed.

| Capability | State | Built by |
|---|---|---|
| Any pickup, manifest, stop, collection, handshake, intake, order, delivery-commitment or evidence table | None; the 24 tables are identity, delivery, audit, idempotency, limiter and reference data | S1.13, S1.20, S1.24, S1.26 to S1.31, S1.36, S1.11 |
| Zones and hub-scoped, versioned settings | None; `hub` is `id` and `name`, `setting` has company and platform scopes | S1.4, S1.5 |
| Reason domains for the slice and the workflow seeds | None; the domain set is closed by a `CHECK` | S1.7 |
| The slice's keys in the role bundles | **Built at S1.8 (29 October 2026):** `tools/seed/bundles.ts` holds SLICE-000's fifteen keys and the slice's thirty-three; migration `0043` gives a database seeded earlier the same, as an addition; the build declares fixed-policy revision 2 | S1.8 |
| Vendor locations, allowance and standing; motorcycles and breakdowns | None; fixtures only, by decision D-14 | S1.10 |
| An evidence store, an outbox and a notification relay, a linkless SMS kind, a scheduler lane | None; the task-capability list holds six | S1.11, S1.12, S1.28 |
| The Vendor PWA and the Rider Android app | None | SLICE-015 and SLICE-016 ([D-03](#11-decisions-recorded)); S1.41 to S1.48 are their starting inventory |
| Ops screens for pickup, manifest, intake or itemization | None | S1.19, S1.25, S1.30, S1.35, S1.40 |
| Operations for hub administration, fleet, price correction, payer edit, dispute, cancel-a-manifest and others | None in the contract | S1.3 adds those D-07 and D-02 choose |

### 2.3 Statements a reader may take as true and are not

Surfaced for the owner; none is edited here (S1.1 edits them with the owner's authority). The master specification is described as the controlling baseline and holds none of the later pricing, intent and handover decisions ([readiness check F-01](SLICE-001-READINESS-CHECK.md#51-the-sources-disagree)). The feature files' pointer sections say operations are missing that exist, and the reverse (F-12). [SLICE-001.md](../slices/SLICE-001.md) §3 says readiness was assessed on 17 August 2026 and records no verdict. The settings document says "nine" fee keys and lists ten. The domain model catalogues a withdrawn `ZonePairRate`.

### 2.4 Traps a task author meets

- **Adding rows after the first seed.** The one-shot initializer refuses unless its seven tables are empty; a later row (a zone, a setting, a reason) needs the additive definer function S1.4 builds, not a second `db:seed`.
- **A hub-scoped table gets its policy, its probe policy, its `RECORD_ENTITIES` row and its registry row together**, in its own migration; a vendor's boundary is its organisation and never a hub ([apps/api/CLAUDE.md](../../apps/api/CLAUDE.md)).
- **`OperationRunner.read` is refused for a state-changing request; `mutate` takes the shared policy locks first** and compares `If-Match` against the version recomputed after the lock.
- **A new capability** enters `TASK_CAPABILITIES`, `CAPABILITY_ACCESS` and the registry in one change; **an adapter** is a `*.adapter.ts` imported only by the composition-root files `src/architecture.test.ts` lists.
- **Outside production an undeclared refusal is a `500`** (`declared-refusals.ts`): declare a code on the operation before a test provokes it.
- **Money is integer pesewas with a `_minor` suffix; the client never sends a price, a margin, a surcharge, a total or a zone.**
- **The cutoff and every service-day rule read the database clock in `Africa/Accra`**, never the host's.
- **Contracts keep the line endings Git stores; edit them with a tool that preserves each file's.** `.prettierignore` keeps `/delivery/` and `/contracts/` out of formatting.
- **The process tests' waits are the owner's** and `VITEST_MAX_WORKERS` is never set ([SLICE-000 §7.48](SLICE-000-IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md#748-the-testing-loop-27-october-2026)).

## 3. Rules every task keeps

The rules of [the SLICE-000 plan's §3](SLICE-000-IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md#3-rules-every-task-keeps) hold for every block without being repeated: one bounded task and a failing test first; contract-first, with the generated client regenerated and never edited; tables in the first task that reads or writes them, forward migrations only, forced row-level security and a registry row; authorize on held permissions and never on a role name; negative-access tests at the five surfaces with each operation; secrets never in a log, event, label, cache or `package:source`; persisted expiries on one fresh database time after the locks; engineering parameters qualified at their first consumer; the five evidence states; the run codes; the testing cadence of the owner's decision of 27 October 2026 ([the development loop](../../DEVELOPMENT.md#the-development-loop-and-the-gate)); local services started by the developer. This plan adds:

- **Precedence.** Where the master specification and a later-dated feature, contract, setting, domain model or state machine disagree, S1.1 records which governs (D-01); until then no task relies on a contested statement.
- **Server-owned values.** A price, a margin, a surcharge, a total, a zone, a declared count before the count, a code's value and a permission decision are never accepted from or echoed to a client where the schema omits them; the omission is the guard, tested as an absence.
- **The blind count is structural.** `HubIntakePreCount` has no declaration property; every read, queue, notification, export, log, event and metric of an unreceived intake is tested for its absence.
- **The existence rule.** A vendor or a rider asking for another party's record is answered exactly as for one that does not exist; staff outside the hubs of a record are `HUB_SCOPE_VIOLATION`; each id-addressed operation ships its equality test.
- **Hub settings.** A hub-scoped value is read for that hub at an instant with its setting key, hub and version, snapshotted on every charge; a missing value fails visibly (`SETTING_MISSING`) in pricing and configuration paths and is **recorded as an absence** in operational paths (a cancellation charge is `UNAVAILABLE`, a cutoff unset does not block a booking); never another hub's value, never a default.
- **Reasons come from the catalogue.** A requirement a reason carries (a note, a photo, a contact attempt) is read from the reason, never hard-coded; the four pickup-failure categories are a fixed enum.
- **Offline.** Only the hub handover is queueable; every other act is online-only and idempotent for connection resilience.
- **Evidence.** A `PENDING_UPLOAD` record cannot be referenced; a signed URL is in no log, event or metric.
- **Notifications are transactional intent.** They are written in the act's transaction, captured in development and CI, sent by no real provider (G3), and a failed notification rolls nothing back.
- **Fixtures are not product.** They are `FIXTURE_`-named, refused in production, changed by closed arrangements and never by a database edit, and a criterion that depends on one is *fixture verified* until the owning slice exists.
- **Run codes.** B, D, W, E, L, J and M as in SLICE-000; **A** is the Android build and tests (Gradle on the pinned JDK, instrumented tests on an emulator).

## 4. Stages, gates and the order of work

Nine stages and nine gates: Stages 0 to 7 and 10, because Stages 8 and 9 (the two clients) moved out of this plan on 29 October 2026. **A gate is the recorded exit check of a stage's last mandatory task, and that task's Needs reach every other mandatory task of the stage**; no task waits for its own gate. One item is conditional: **S1.9** runs only if D-02 is option A. The staging is the readiness check's F-38: the slice is built in milestones and is **complete only when every required criterion is met**.

| Milestone | Gate | What is then true |
|---|---|---|
| M0 Reconciliation | Gate 0 (S1.3) | The specifications, the contract and the client agree with the owner's decisions |
| M1 Foundations | Gates 1 and 2 (S1.8, S1.12) | The hub's zones and fees are read from versioned rows, pricing is a tested pure calculation, the slice's keys are granted, and the fixtures, evidence store and notifications exist |
| M2 API and Ops | Gates 3 to 7 (S1.19, S1.25, S1.30, S1.35, S1.40) | A parcel goes from booking to a closed, priced intake through the API and the Ops Portal |
| M5 Close | Gate 10 (S1.56) | The journeys, sweeps, races, measurement, documents and the owner's walk are recorded |

M3 (the Vendor PWA) and M4 (the Rider app) are `SLICE-015` and `SLICE-016`, not milestones of this plan: their Stages 8 and 9 stay in [§5](#5-the-tasks) as the starting inventory of those slices ([D-03](#11-decisions-recorded)).

**Graph check (generated by a script kept outside the repository, run on the day this section was written).** The graph has 56 parent tasks and 4 suffix subtasks and 109 dependency edges between tasks (the owner's decisions are inputs, not edges). It has no cycle and no reference to an unknown task. Every task of a stage is an ancestor of that stage's gate task, apart from S1.9 and S1.48. The 39 operations this plan places and the 81 criteria (80 and one added) each appear exactly once in [§6](#6-traceability). **These are the counts of that run, which was not repeated:** the table below carries six subtasks of S1.12 that the count of suffix subtasks does not include, and Stages 8 and 9 (S1.41 to S1.48) left the plan on 29 October 2026.
