## 5. The tasks

Each block gives: **Needs** (what must be done first: tasks, and the owner's decisions of the readiness check), **Run** (the suites that prove it), **Operations** and **Criteria** (where real-operation evidence completes), a **Subtasks** table where the task is too large for one hand-off, then what the task **builds on** that exists and is tested, what it **newly builds**, the **files**, the **evidence** it reaches, the **obligations carried**, what it **does not** do and when it must **stop**. A criterion marked † is one S1.1 proposes.

### Stage 0 — Reconciliation of the specifications, the contract and the client

No product code. The specifications, the contract and the generated client are brought into line with the decisions of the readiness check, as SLICE-000's Stage R did. The stage ends at **Gate 0** (S1.3): the documents and the contract agree and the checks pass.

#### S1.1 — Master specification and feature reconciliation

| | |
|---|---|
| **Needs**| [D-01](SLICE-001-READINESS-CHECK.md#d-01-which-text-governs-where-the-master-specification-and-the-later-documents-disagree), [D-08](SLICE-001-READINESS-CHECK.md#d-08-authorize-stage-0-the-reconciliation) |
| **Run**| B |
| **Operations**| none |
| **Criteria**| none |

**Builds on.** The six feature files, the master specification (version 1.15), the contracts' prose and [SLICE-001.md](../slices/SLICE-001.md); the readiness check's findings F-01, F-05, F-06, F-07, F-08, F-12 and F-37.
**Newly builds.** The master specification's §21 to §23 and §33 to §36 amended to the decisions the features cite (flat fees in place of the zone-pair matrix, three size classes, the booking estimate, the three pickup intents, the handover row per collected request). The sentences that wait for a decision (the high-value surcharge, D-06; Amasaman's batch days, D-13; the Rider's technology, D-03) are amended when that decision is made (§11), not in this task. The stale statements of F-12 corrected where they stand (including the status of the handshake parameters, and the screen-inventory row of overview.md, F-34). The criteria repaired: governing line, surface and level for `AC-SLICE-001-69` to `-72`; a retained section for `-05`, `-24`, `-25`, `-73`; the code for `-12`, `-18`, `-33` and `-64`; the catalogue codes for `-69` to `-71`; the expected codes of `-42` and `-76` (a missing key is `PERMISSION_DENIED`, errors-and-enums.md line 182; F-14); `-58` to the behaviour the schema allows (F-14). **Criteria the readiness check found missing** (the cap acknowledgement at booking) is added under the next identifier, `AC-SLICE-001-81`, never renumbering; the same-area correction that the check also found is not a criterion of this slice, because controlled repricing is deferred (D-07). SLICE-001.md records the readiness verdict and the staging of this plan; [IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md](../IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md) §3 and §4a record D-02 once it is made (§11).
**Files.** `PROJECT_MASTER_SPECIFICATION.md`, `features/pickup/*.md`, `features/hub/*.md`, `contracts/domain-model.md` and `contracts/settings.md` (prose only), `architecture/MIGRATION_AND_SEEDING.md`, `architecture/SOLUTION_ARCHITECTURE.md` and `SECURITY_DESIGN.md` (the Rider technology), `surfaces/*.md` (the Rider technology and the pull-only statement), `delivery/slices/SLICE-001.md`, `delivery/IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`.
**Evidence.** `pnpm run precheck`, then `pnpm run test:affected`: a document change reaches the root tests, which read every document (links, the `CLAUDE.md` budgets, the seed lists that equal the contracts). Every changed statement is read against the decision that licenses it and the decision is named in the change. *Evidence state: document change only; no criterion moves.*
**Obligations carried.** The count of criteria in SLICE-001.md §1 is derived afterwards (80 becomes 81).
**Does not.** Change a product value that is not already decided; edit `contracts/openapi.yaml` (S1.2, S1.3); renumber or reuse a criterion identifier; decide any open question of section 7 of the readiness check.
**Stop if.** A document must record a decision that is not among D-01 to D-14; a contradiction not in F-01 is found (add it to the check and surface it).

#### S1.2 — Contract reconciliation, part 1: fields, codes and ETag reads

| | |
|---|---|
| **Needs**| [S1.1](#s11--master-specification-and-feature-reconciliation) |
| **Run**| B |
| **Operations**| none |
| **Criteria**| none |

**Builds on.** `contracts/openapi.yaml` 5.77 with `x-error-codes` and `ETag` declarations, the generator `pnpm run api-client:generate`, `contract:check`, and the pinned contract tests (`identity-decisions`, `contract-consistency`, `real-contract`, `compare-real-operation`).
**Newly builds.** The fields of F-13 that need no product decision: `cutoff_exception_state`, the saved location a request selects, the fixed failure category on the failure request and on `PickupStop`, `is_serialized_goods` and `serial_imei`, the evidence references of `OrderItemize`, `delivery_date_override`, the zone authorization, the cancellation charge's snapshot, `PickupStop.arrived_at` and the failure snapshot, `PickupManifest.completed_at`, the per-parcel declared value, requested delivery date and service level, a typed `payer_override`, the request's primary and secondary phone, and the rider-visible projection of a stop (vendor, packages, location note). The codes of F-14 declared on the operations that raise them (and a `400` on `itemizeOrder`; `DESTINATION_UNRESOLVED` and `SENDER_IDENTITY_AMBIGUOUS` on `createPickupRequest`; `SIZE_CLASS_REQUIRED` made reachable, either by an optional `size_class` or by amending AC-58 to the `400`, as D-08 authorizes), `ALREADY_MANIFESTED` added to the catalogue, the withdrawn code struck from prose, the choice of F-07. **The manifest read, the stop and the evidence responses set an `ETag`** (F-16). The client regenerated and never edited. New pins for the slice beside the old ones, which change deliberately and in this task only.
**Files.** `contracts/openapi.yaml`, `contracts/errors-and-enums.md`, `packages/api-client/src/generated/*` (generated), `apps/api/src/platform/contract/*.test.ts`, `apps/api/src/platform/contract/declared-refusals.ts` (its `AWAITING_DECLARATION` list is unchanged unless the owner decides finding 1 of [SLICE-000 §7.49](SLICE-000-IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md#749-stage-8-s062-to-s070-27-and-28-october-2026)).
**Evidence.** `pnpm run contract:check`, `pnpm run api-client:check`, the contract tests. A deliberate break (a code removed from an operation, an `ETag` header removed) is caught by the sweep that reads each criterion's code against its operation (S1.52 builds the full sweep; this task adds the screen used here). *Document and contract change; no criterion moves.*
**Obligations carried.** None.
**Does not.** Add or remove an operation (S1.3); rename an identifier; change a header or a status the owner has not decided.
**Stop if.** `contract:check` or `api-client:check` fails for a reason the edit does not explain.

#### S1.3 — Contract reconciliation, part 2: operations, states, reasons, permissions and registry (Gate 0)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Needs**| [S1.2](#s12--contract-reconciliation-part-1-fields-codes-and-etag-reads), [D-02](SLICE-001-READINESS-CHECK.md#d-02-how-the-hubs-zones-fees-and-operational-settings-reach-the-system), [D-04](SLICE-001-READINESS-CHECK.md#d-04-the-order-of-the-count-and-the-handshake-and-the-ad-hoc-direction), [D-05](SLICE-001-READINESS-CHECK.md#d-05-the-reason-catalogue-for-the-slice), [D-07](SLICE-001-READINESS-CHECK.md#d-07-operations-the-specifications-require-and-the-contract-lacks), [D-09](SLICE-001-READINESS-CHECK.md#d-09-operations-no-feature-names-and-where-evidence-code-lives), [D-14](SLICE-001-READINESS-CHECK.md#d-14-records-from-other-slices-and-the-slices-grants) |
| **Run**| B |
| **Operations**| none |
| **Criteria**| none |

**Subtasks.**

| Subtask | Content | Also needs |
|---|---|---|
| S1.3a | The operations and schemas D-07 and D-09 choose, with `If-Match`, `Idempotency-Key`, permission, session kinds and `x-error-codes` each | S1.2 |
| S1.3b | The state machines, domain model, permissions (holders of new keys; the explicit grant of D-14), audit codes (with tiers), errors and settings (the `HubSetting` model D-02 chooses) | S1.3a |
| S1.3c | The data-scope registry rows of F-20, each a table or an embedded value as decided; the reason domains and codes D-05 chooses in domain-model §3.9 | S1.3b |
| S1.3d | Verification: regenerate the client, run the checks, extend the pinned tests and the document-reading tests, record the gate | S1.3c |

**Builds on.** The contract documents and the checks of S1.2.
**Newly builds.** First what the decisions settle: the booking estimate (D-01); the one-package recipient capture and the way a one-package request comes to exist (D-07); the handshake state and channel names of F-03, and the wording of `AC-SLICE-001-21`, `-27`, `-28` and the second "never" rule of pickup-collection.md §5.3 (D-04). Then the operations of the readiness check's D-07 table that you decide to build, under the names fixed in [§6.1](#61-operations): `getPickupStop` (the stop's `ETag` read), `resolveCutoffException`, `amendPickupRequest` (the origin and the payer arrangement before the freeze), `cancelPickupManifest`, `failRunCustodyHandover`, `resolveHandshake` (key `pickup.handshake.resolve`, new), `disputeCollection`, `recordIntakeCondition`, `getIntakeSummary`, `getOrder`, `requestPayerCorrection`, `approvePayerCorrection`, `findAdHocSender` and `listVendorPickupLocations`, the exception body of `closeHubIntake`, and a `manifested` filter on `listPickupRequests` for the pool; if D-02 is option A, the hub-configuration operations of S1.9. The handshake, custody and dispute states of the state machines; the reason domains of D-05; the permission holders of every new key; the audit events of every new act; the registry rows. The slice's operation inventory in §6.1 and its counts are re-derived afterwards.
**Files.** `contracts/openapi.yaml`, `contracts/state-machines.md`, `contracts/domain-model.md`, `contracts/permissions.md`, `contracts/audit.md`, `contracts/errors-and-enums.md`, `contracts/settings.md`, `contracts/data-scope-registry.md`, `packages/api-client/src/generated/*` (generated), the pinned tests of S1.2.
**Evidence.** **Gate 0:** `pnpm run contract:check`, `pnpm run api-client:check` and the contract and document-reading tests pass; no criterion's operation, field or code is missing from the contract; the reading of section 6 of the readiness check is re-run: no criterion remains *Amend*, and none remains *Decide* for a decision this task needs (D-01, D-02, D-04, D-05, D-07, D-09, D-14); each *Decide* that remains is listed with its decision and the task that closes it (D-13 at S1.5, S1.11 and S1.32; D-06 at S1.6; D-10 and D-11 at S1.12 and S1.28; D-12 at S1.37; D-03 at S1.31, and in SLICE-016 for the offline rule). *Document and contract change; no criterion moves.*
**Obligations carried.** The counts in this plan (operations, criteria) are re-derived; OB-S1-01.
**Does not.** Implement an operation (`IMPLEMENTED_OPERATIONS` is unchanged); add a table or a migration.
**Stop if.** An operation or field cannot be specified without a product value (surface it; never default it).

### Stage 1 — Hub, zone and pricing foundation (SLICE-014, bounded)

The hub configuration the slice cannot price without, in the shape D-02 chooses (written for option B; S1.9 holds the additions of option A). The stage ends at **Gate 1** (S1.8): the hub's zones and fees are read from versioned rows, pricing is a tested pure calculation, and the slice's keys are granted.

#### S1.4 — Zone and hub-setting store, with the hub-aware settings reader

| | |
|---|---|
| **Needs**| [S1.3](#s13--contract-reconciliation-part-2-operations-states-reasons-permissions-and-registry-gate-0) |
| **Run**| B D |
| **Operations**| none |
| **Criteria**| none |

**Builds on.** The `hub` table and the seed path (`melarc.initialize_reference_data`, `db:seed`), the settings reader (`src/platform/settings/settings-reader.ts`: no default, `SETTING_MISSING`), the registry and its grants test (`registry-grants.db.test.ts`), the existence-rule probe (`RECORD_ENTITIES`).
**Newly builds.** One migration creating `service_zone` (hub, name, service-area class, serviceable days, active) and `hub_setting` (hub, key, value, unit, version, effective from, status) as reference data owned by `melarc_owner`, forced row-level security, a staff read policy in hub scope, a `SYSTEM` arm under the `seed` capability, no write for the runtime, registry rows and `SEED_ONLY_TABLES` entries. `SettingsReader` gains a read for a hub at an instant that returns the value **with the setting key, hub and version it came from** (the snapshot every charge carries), refuses with `SETTING_MISSING` when none is effective and **never** falls back to another hub or a default. A way to add rows after the one-shot initializer has run (it refuses unless the seven tables are empty): an additive, idempotent definer function executable by the provisioning login, as `melarc.add_reason_fixtures` is for fixtures.
**Files.** `apps/api/src/platform/database/schema/reference-data.ts`, one migration, `apps/api/src/platform/settings/settings-reader.ts`, `apps/api/src/tools/seed/seed-runtime.ts`, `apps/api/src/test-support/registry-grants.ts`, `contracts/data-scope-registry.md` (already holds the `ServiceZone` and `HubSetting` rows), tests in `apps/api/test/database/` and beside the reader.
**Evidence.** Database tests as the runtime login: the policies admit the staff of the hub and no vendor or rider; no write grant; a missing key is `SETTING_MISSING` for that hub while another hub has it; a value changed at a later effective date does not alter an earlier snapshot; the additive function refuses a non-provisioning caller. *Fixture/platform verified.*
**Obligations carried.** The zone and `HubSetting` fields are provisional and minimal; SLICE-014 owns the full design (settings.md §7.2). Recorded in the migration's header.
**Does not.** Write a proposal, an approval or a change history (SLICE-014); add an operation; seed values (S1.5).
**Stop if.** Adding rows after the first seed needs a mechanism the documents do not state and the additive function does not cover.

|---|---|

**Newly builds.** In `src/pricing/`, pure functions with no I/O: the fee for a destination (in-area doorstep, corridor on its batch day or off it, station drop, outside the service area as carrier cost plus margin plus the size surcharge), the size surcharge **only** in the third-party mode, the high-value surcharge as D-06 decides, the price components with their hub, setting key, version and value, and the **booking estimate** (base fee times packages for a standard request; the destination-aware form for a one-package exception; absent for a known outside-Accra destination). Every refusal is a typed error that carries its code (`SETTING_MISSING`, `MARGIN_NOT_CONFIGURED`, `SIZE_CLASS_REQUIRED`, `CARRIER_COST_REQUIRED`).

**Does not.** Read a setting (the caller passes the snapshot in); accept a price, margin, surcharge, total or zone from a client.
**Stop if.** A fee row of the table or a component has no stated rule.

A label is the code in sentence case until he rewords it, and a failure reason is not bound to a category in the table. A mutation run of nine breaks (a changed attribution, requirement, code, domain, or a migration that also writes into an unseeded database) failed a test each time.

### Stage 2 — Platform capabilities the slice needs

Fixtures, the evidence store and the notification mechanism, each built once and used by the stages after. The stage ends at **Gate 2** (S1.12).

#### S1.11 — Evidence: tables, the object-store port and the three operations

| | |
|---|---|
| **Needs**| [S1.8](#s18--bundle-and-fixed-policy-additions-for-the-slices-keys-gate-1), [D-09](SLICE-001-READINESS-CHECK.md#d-09-operations-no-feature-names-and-where-evidence-code-lives), [D-13](SLICE-001-READINESS-CHECK.md#d-13-values) |
| **Run**| B D |
| **Operations**| `createEvidence`, `completeEvidenceUpload`, `createEvidenceRetrievalAuthorization` |
| **Criteria**| none |

**Builds on.** The idempotency store and audit writer, the security matrix Families M, N, U and X, the port pattern of `src/platform/auth/ports/` (an interface, a token, a descriptor; adapters only in the composition root), `TASK_CAPABILITIES` and `CAPABILITY_ACCESS`.
**Newly builds.** The `evidence` table (owner type and id, purpose, state `PENDING_UPLOAD`, `STORED`, `EXPIRED`, an owner-aware hub, no hub for a vendor owner); an `EvidenceStore` port with two adapters: **Cloudflare R2** (the owner's choice of 29 October 2026; its S3-compatible API, with single-object presigned upload and retrieval of the 15 and 5 minute lifetimes of the settings, the signature made by hand with `node:crypto` and proved against the published Signature Version 4 examples of AWS, so no dependency is added) and an in-process stand-in for development and CI (refused in production), which never reaches a bucket; the owner's R2 credentials are held as Hubtel's are (the git-ignored `apps/api/.env`, never in a chat, a log, a test or a commit); the three operations (create with a size, content type and SHA-256; complete, validating size, checksum and signature; retrieval authorization, refused with `EVIDENCE_NOT_STORED` until stored); a validation lane under a new `evidence_validation` capability; the sweep that expires `PENDING_UPLOAD` records and keeps them; no signed URL in any log, metric or audit event.
**Files.** `apps/api/src/platform/auth/ports/evidence-store.port.ts` (or the port the owner places, D-09), `apps/api/src/platform/evidence/*`, `apps/api/src/platform/database/task-capabilities.ts`, `capability-access.ts`, one migration, `apps/api/src/app.module.ts` (the adapter binding), tests.
**Evidence.** Route tests per operation; matrix Families M, N (including `GBT-N6`, `N8`, `N11`, `N12`) and U8 as far as the development adapter lets them run; a `PENDING_UPLOAD` record cannot be referenced; the production composition refuses the development adapter. *Fixture/platform verified; the real bucket and the malware check are open (OB-S1-05).*
**Obligations carried.** OB-S1-05: the store is decided (Cloudflare R2), and so are the ceiling and the types (10 MB; JPEG, PNG or PDF; development values, the launch values stay the owner's); the account and bucket, the malicious-content mechanism (a release gate) and the retention period are still the owner's; G3.
**Does not.** Add a dependency; serve a byte through the API; attach evidence to an owner (S1.28, S1.34, S1.36 do); touch a real bucket other than a non-production one the owner provides with a restricted token.
**Stop if.** A check can only be given by a real bucket (ask the owner for a non-production bucket; never use a production one), or the mapping from an owner to its permission cannot be made without a new access kind in the guard (surface it).

#### S1.12 — The notification service: outbox, SMS through Hubtel, receipts and the Ops log (Gate 2)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Needs**| [S1.8](#s18--bundle-and-fixed-policy-additions-for-the-slices-keys-gate-1), [S1.10](#s110--non-production-fixtures-and-test-kit-arrangements), [D-10](SLICE-001-READINESS-CHECK.md#d-10-the-recipient-channel-bar-and-the-heads-up-sms), [D-11](SLICE-001-READINESS-CHECK.md#d-11-notifications-the-outbox-the-scheduler-and-what-notified-means) |
| **Run**| B D L |
| **Operations**| `listNotifications` |
| **Criteria**| none |

**Decided 29 October 2026 ([§11](#11-decisions-recorded)).** The full notification service now: the mechanism and its monitoring, and not SLICE-010's administration (editable templates, per-vendor preferences). **Who is texted:** the ad-hoc sender (pickup confirmed or declined, the handshake code, a pickup failure without private details, the intake completed with its order reference; no link in any of them), the registered vendor's registered number for the handshake fallback only, and the rider (one text when a run is dispatched and again when it is reassigned). A registered vendor's other milestones stay in the app feed. **The provider:** Hubtel, under the sender ID `Melarc`; its credentials are the owner's alone, held in the git-ignored `apps/api/.env.sms` (which only the worker and the smoke command load) and never in a chat, a log, a test or a commit. **Real messages** are proved in a controlled local window (S1.12d). **The heads-up SMS** is built, once per parcel, and its confirmed value stays On. **Limits:** 10 messages per number per day, 500 per day, 20 in the window. English only.

**Subtasks.**

| Subtask | Content | Also needs |
|---|---|---|
| S1.12a | The `outbox` envelope written in the business transaction, the `notification` record, and the relay lane of the existing delivery worker, which reads the outbox under a capability of its own | |
| S1.12b | The list of messages (event, audience, channel, template and version) held to the documents by a test; the versioned English templates, whose wording the owner approves (`OQ-037`); the audience rules; the kill switches | S1.12a |
| S1.12c | The Hubtel client and the adapter behind a message port that carries text and no link; a local stand-in that speaks Hubtel's HTTP contract; the outcome mapping (a timeout, a 429 and a 5xx are `UNKNOWN`; a refusal is terminal) | S1.12a |
| S1.12d | The controlled local window: the deployment rule amended with its guards, the window's settings, and the `sms:smoke` command the owner runs | S1.12c |
| S1.12e | Receipts polled from Hubtel's status check, the limits and the suppression, the Ops message log and the Ops feed (API and screens), the alerts | S1.12b, S1.12c |
| S1.12f | The handshake code store (hashed, never logged) and the linkless SMS kind for the ad-hoc sender and the registered number, with the cap of 3 and the cooldown of 60 seconds held by the database | S1.12b |

**Builds on.** The credential-delivery queue and worker (`src/platform/delivery/`, `src/delivery-worker-runtime.ts`), the capture adapter and the recipient allow-list, [BACKGROUND_JOBS_AND_EVENTS.md](../../architecture/BACKGROUND_JOBS_AND_EVENTS.md) §3 and §4, the worker-capability table of SECURITY_DESIGN §14, the settings reader and the audit writer.
**Newly builds.** The `outbox` and `notification` tables, every row forced-RLS and classified in the registry. A notification is written in the transaction of the act and is sent by the worker with no transaction open; a failed send rolls nothing back (BJE §3). Receipts: the worker polls Hubtel's status check by message id on a bounded schedule (no webhook is documented), and a notification ends `SENT`, `DELIVERED`, `FAILED`, `SUPPRESSED` or `EXPIRED` (a limit, a kill switch, the allow-list, wording nobody approved, a request that changed first; a destination Hubtel refuses is `FAILED` with its closed word until the provider's refusals can be told apart). One port for text messages with a capture adapter (the default in a local run and in CI), the Hubtel adapter and the stand-in; the credential-delivery adapter for SMS uses the same Hubtel client behind its existing port. The notification types of F-25, each held to its document. The Ops message log (a read of the notifications with their status, no destination in clear and no code) and the Ops feed.
**Files.** `apps/api/src/platform/notifications/*` (not `platform/delivery`: the code-placement rule of F-32), the ports under `apps/api/src/platform/auth/ports/`, `apps/api/src/delivery-worker-runtime.ts`, `task-capabilities.ts`, `capability-access.ts`, `apps/api/src/platform/config/` (the Hubtel keys `HUBTEL_SMS_CLIENT_ID`, `HUBTEL_SMS_CLIENT_SECRET` and `HUBTEL_SMS_SENDER_ID`, and the window's settings), `apps/api/.env.example` (names only), the migrations, the registry rows, the two platform limits in settings.md, the Ops screens, tests.
**Evidence.** A notification is written in the same transaction as the act and rolls back with it; a failed send rolls nothing back; the limits hold across two worker processes; a code appears in no log, event, metric or file of the message log; the production composition refuses the capturing lane; the Hubtel adapter's outcomes (accepted, refused, `UNKNOWN` on a timeout, a 429 or a 5xx) against the stand-in; the controlled window refuses every send without all its guards and after its end. **Gate 2.** *Fixture/platform verified; the live proof is the owner's smoke run (OB-S1-06).*
**Obligations carried.** OB-S1-06: the SMS wording (`OQ-037`) is the owner's to approve before any real send; the Hubtel credentials, the window's settings and the smoke run are the owner's; the legal basis for recipient numbers (`OQ-028`) is a go-live gate for the heads-up SMS; OB-24 style Linux evidence for the worker's new lane.
**Does not.** Send a real message before every guard of the window is met; build SLICE-010's administration; add a broker or a second process; build the Vendor's feed screens (SLICE-015); read, print or store a credential.
**Stop if.** The technical-role design cannot give the lane its own capability without another login or process (surface it); Hubtel's real contract differs from the one stated (the stand-in and the adapter are corrected together).

**Built (29 and 30 October 2026): S1.12a to S1.12e.** Part f is not built (below). Migrations `0055` to `0057` were rehearsed on a restored copy of the owner's database (20 checks: nothing changed but the new tables, three settings and three grants; no staff row, session or audit event moved) and **applied to the owner's `melarc_dev` on 30 October 2026** (`db:bootstrap`, then `db:migrate`: 58 migrations; verified read-only afterwards).
- **The tables** (migration `0055`): `outbox`, the envelope a business act writes in its own transaction (its scope is the aggregate's: the insert guard compares it with the row in the writer's own transaction, so a caller cannot choose it; the relay's bookkeeping is the only part that ever changes), and `notification`, one message to one audience (no destination, no text and no code: a masked hint and a count digest). Forced row-level security, owned by `melarc_owner`, guards for both state machines, the accessors `melarc.outbox_id()`, `outbox_hub_id()` and `outbox_aggregate_id()`, and two read policies that show the lane the one request its leased envelope names and the one sender of it. **Staff read the log only under the key `notification.read`** (the policy tests the authorization key beside the hub scope, as the pickup tables do). The lane has a grant set of its own, `melarc_worker_cap_notification`, that provisioning puts in front of the worker's login beside the credential-delivery one (**the owner runs `db:bootstrap` before `db:migrate`**).
- **The lane** (`platform/notifications/`) runs inside the delivery worker as a periodic task. One tick: it relays envelopes (the context is built from the stored row, the aggregate is reloaded under it, a scope that no longer matches is a dead letter and a security signal and sends nothing), sends the texts that are due with **no transaction open** (the destination is read in a transaction that ends before the provider is asked), and asks the provider for the network's word. Every claim is a lease and every outcome is fenced by its version; a retry is the Class 1 schedule (six attempts, the provider's own wait honoured), a refusal is final, a guard's refusal and a limit hold the message back as `SUPPRESSED` with a reason. `confirmPickupRequest` writes its envelope.
- **The messages:** two are wired (the confirmation, by text to the ad-hoc sender and as a feed entry for a registered vendor) and thirteen are planned with their documents (a test holds the list to §31.2 of the specification, row by row). **A wording is a draft until the owner approves it (`OQ-037`)**, and an approval is of the exact words (a digest of them): a real provider is never asked to send words nobody approved (`WORDING_NOT_APPROVED`); the capture adapter sends any. **The owner approved the two wordings built so far, as written, on 30 October 2026**; each later message's wording is a draft until they approve it.
- **The provider:** the Hubtel client and adapter are built from the best known form of its contract, **marked unverified in `hubtel-sms-client.ts`**; the capture adapter and a stand-in on a real socket serve a local run, CI and the tests. The deduplication identity is not forwarded to Hubtel (the contract as known has no place for it), so a retry after an unknown outcome can send twice until its reference field is confirmed; the day's limits now count such a message (R69).
- **The window:** the guard adapter (end, allow-list and cap, enforced before any provider call), the window's counter (a file in the capture folder, safe across restarts and processes, failing closed), `.env.sms.example`, and `sms:smoke`, the command the owner runs; both are composed by `src/notification-sender.ts`, as the worker is.
- **The switch and the limits** (migration `0056`): the platform settings `sms_enabled` (On), `sms_per_number_daily_limit` (10) and `sms_daily_limit` (500), read by the lane through its capability (three rows, two columns) and kept for a few seconds; a value that is missing or not what the key takes holds the texts back and the log names the key. The window's own cap (20) is the worker's environment setting.
- **The metrics:** the counters of the relay, the sends, the held-back texts and the receipts, and two gauges on the worker's private endpoint: the age of the oldest work that is due and not done, and the number of envelopes in the dead letter. **Four alert rules** (`alert-rules.ts`, the owner's thresholds of 30 October 2026, held to the *Text messages* rows of OBSERVABILITY_AND_RECOVERY.md §3 and §3.2 by a test): the oldest due work above five minutes for fifteen minutes (SEV-3); a new dead letter, `UNKNOWN_EVENT` or `SCHEDULE_EXHAUSTED` (SEV-3); a relay ending in `SCOPE_MISMATCH` (SEV-2, a security signal); refusals, unanswered attempts and exhausted schedules above a tenth of at least twenty sends in fifteen minutes (SEV-3). The gauge of dead envelopes is for the eye and is not an alert (R74).
- **The Ops message log** (migration `0057`, operation `listNotifications`, `notification.read`): the messages of the hubs of the grant, newest first, filtered by state, audience, channel and the record they are about; the Ops feed is the same list at `channel=FEED&audience=OPS_FEED`. An entry holds no destination, no text and no code (the closed list of its fields is held to the contract by a test, and the API's login cannot select the digest, the provider's reference or the lease). The screen is `/messages` (surfaces/ops-portal.md §7), built by a delegated agent and verified by me: a page with the nine interface states and filters kept in the address (including the Ops feed and one request's messages, which the pickup request page links to for a session that holds `notification.read`); a shape guard that refuses the whole page on any entry whose "hint" is not a masked number, or that carries a field the contract does not name; a menu entry gated by the permission and never by a role; 728 unit and structural tests; 47 browser tests in Chromium against the production build and 47 against the development server (WCAG 2.2 A/AA with axe at 1280, 768 and 320 px, 200% text, forced colours, reduced motion); 24 deliberate breaks caught. **Not run:** Firefox and WebKit, and nothing against the real API and database (the screen is proved against mocked answers; OB-S1-20). The words of the screen are provisional and kept in `message-presentation.ts`.
- **The independent review of 30 October** (a read-only reviewer, whose claims were each checked against the code): no Critical; four Important, all fixed with a test that fails without the fix: **I-1** the day's limits could not see a text whose answer was lost (R69); **I-2** a message's life ran from the relay and not from the act (R68); **I-3** six of the eight lease fences had no test (now each has one); **I-4** nothing proved that the worker composes the lane and the wording gate (two tests now start the built worker: one relays, sends to the capture folder and serves the gauges, one puts a real provider, with the stand-in, behind unapproved wording and sees it asked nothing). Minor and fixed: the masked hint and the digest are pinned (m1); a count file that vanishes under a running worker stops the sending (m2, R72); an approval is bound to the exact words (m3, R71); a send starts only with time left in its lease (m4, R70); a dead letter names its envelope in the log and is counted by a gauge (m5); the receipts' looks are told as they are (m9, R60); the day's edge and a dead lane's lease are tested (m10). The rest are recorded: R73 (m7), the envelope's idempotency linkage (m8, in BACKGROUND_JOBS_AND_EVENTS.md §3.7), and the settings cache on the wall clock (m6, left: a backward step of the clock keeps a stale switch for as long as the step).
- **Evidence:** 102 database tests of the two tables, their policies and guards; 59 of the lane against a real PostgreSQL with two lanes on two database pools; 28 route tests of `listNotifications` and 15 of the grants (0043 and 0057); 2 tests of the built worker process; the unit tests of the log, the message list, the hint and digest, the guards, the counter and the composition; mutation runs: 66 changes to the migration, the store, the lane, the guards and the wording (54 killed at once; the 12 survivors were answered by tests and killed), 46 to the log, its policy and its grants, 20 to the settings (migration `0056` and its reader), 36 to the fixes of the review, 6 to the wording approval and the worker's composition, and 17 to the four alert rules: all killed, one of the review's after a test was sharpened to the edge of the day that it had missed, and two of the alerts' after a test was added for the length of the window.

**Rulings made while building S1.12 (provisional until the owner confirms them).**
- **R56.** The relay is the notification lane of the delivery worker and not a process of its own: no broker, no `melarc_outbox_relay_runtime` login (registry §4.6 says so). The lane leases the envelopes it relays.
- **R57.** The lane stores no destination. It reads the number from the aggregate in the transaction that precedes the send, so a number corrected or a request cancelled between the act and the send is seen; the row keeps a masked hint and a SHA-256 of the namespaced number to count by (a key to count with, not a protection: the number is in the business record).
- **R58.** Two stages. The relay writes the notifications and ends the envelope in one transaction; the send leases each notification on its own. A retry of a send never relays again.
- **R59.** A text stays worth sending for six hours (a confirmation after an outage is noise); a feed entry stays for ninety days. Both provisional.
- **R60.** The network's word is polled (Hubtel documents no webhook): a first look thirty seconds after the send, then eleven more after waits that grow from thirty seconds to twelve hours (twelve looks over about twenty-six hours), then the message is left as it was sent.
- **R61.** A day is the UTC day, which is Accra's (no daylight time). The two limits count what was sent that day and what another lane is holding, under one advisory lock of class `NOTIFICATION_LIMITS`, so two processes cannot both take the last place.
- **R62.** A guard's refusal (the window ended, the cap spent, a number off the list) is a message held back, not a failure of the provider, and shows so in the log.
- **R63.** The worker's pool grows by one connection for the lane. The lane's tick is bounded by a batch and by a budget sized for the whole batch, because a budget that ends in the middle of a send would lose the outcome of a message the provider took.
- **R64.** The audience `RECIPIENT` and the suppression reasons `WORDING_NOT_APPROVED` and `OVERTAKEN` were added to the first migration: it was not yet applied anywhere.
- **R65.** The staff read of `notification` requires the authorization key `notification.read` and not only a hub scope, so that a staff operation under another key (a pickup grant) cannot read the log. Cost if wrong: one policy line; migration `0055` was not yet applied anywhere.
- **R66.** `notification.read` is granted by its own list and migration (`0057`, an addition that ends no session) to Ops Staff and Senior Ops at their own hub and, since task P1 (migration `0075`, 6 November 2026), to Platform Admin at every hub. A session open when the migration is applied does not carry it: sign out and in. A Platform Admin without a primary hub read no log before P1 (the finding F-S1.8-01) and reads the log of every hub now (`platform-admin-every-hub.db.test.ts`).
- **R67.** The Ops feed is the log filtered to its audience and channel. No read marker (unread, acknowledged) is built, because no Ops feed message is raised before the failure escalation (S1.28) and the hub handover (S1.34); whether a feed is per person or per hub, and whether it replaces or points into the queues, is the owner's when the first one exists (OB-S1-18).
- **R68.** A message stays worth sending for its time to live **from the act** (the envelope's creation), not from the relay. One already out of date when its envelope is relayed is made with an end one second away and not due before it, so that the first step of the next tick ends it `EXPIRED` and the log shows it was too late. Cost if wrong: one query.
- **R69.** The day's limits count what may have left, not only what the provider said it took: a message counts from the moment it reserved its place (`reserved_at`, just before the provider is first asked) unless it is known not to have left (held back, or refused). A lost answer, a lane that stopped mid-send and a message that gave up after answers it could not read are counted; up to six duplicate texts of one message remain possible when the provider takes a text and its answer is lost, and the limits now see that message once.
- **R70.** A send starts only while the provider's whole time-out and five seconds to record the outcome are left in the lease; otherwise the message is left for the next claim, so that a lane that stalled before the call does not send under a lease another lane may take.
- **R71.** The approval of a wording holds for exactly those words: the template records who, when and the digest of its kind, version, title, wording and placeholders, and words edited afterwards are a draft again whether or not the version was raised.
- **R72.** A count file that has never been written is a window that has sent nothing; one that this counter has seen and now misses, or cannot read, stops the sending. The owner clearing the capture folder between two runs starts the count again, and renewing a window is done with a new approval reference.
- **R73.** `DESTINATION_REFUSED` stays in the vocabulary, the table's check and the contract as **reserved**: the lane cannot yet tell a refused destination from another refusal (Hubtel's error vocabulary is unverified), so every refusal is `FAILED` with the provider's closed word; the first real answer shows whether it can. `provider_outcome_class` may also carry the lane's own words `UNKNOWN_MESSAGE` and `TEMPLATE_UNUSABLE`.
- **R74.** The alerts on the notification series are the owner's proposals of OBSERVABILITY_AND_RECOVERY.md §3, accepted as written on 30 October 2026 and coded in `alert-rules.ts`, with two readings of mine. (a) "Any dead envelope" is read as **a new dead letter in fifteen minutes** (the counters `UNKNOWN_EVENT` and `SCHEDULE_EXHAUSTED`), not as the gauge `notification_dead_envelopes` above zero: a dead row is immutable and no operation retires it, so the gauge could never return to zero and the alert would fire for ever after the first. `SCOPE_MISMATCH` is its own rule, SEV-2. (b) A `RETRY` (an answer that could not be read) counts as a failed attempt beside `REJECTED` and `SCHEDULE_EXHAUSTED`, so that an outage shows when it starts and not hours later when the schedules end; one message retried six times counts six. Which tool evaluates the rules and where a page goes stays release gate G3's. Cost if wrong: a number or a word in one rule and its row.
- **R75.** The kill switch's active state is visible to an operator per message (a held-back text says `KILL_SWITCH`); a screen that shows the switch itself waits for the settings screens of SLICE-010 (§33.3), and until then the switch is changed in the database.
- **R76.** The sub-ceiling `provider_initiating_operation_ceiling_per_minute` (20 a minute per session, settings.md §7.7) is seeded and enforced nowhere, and `confirmPickupRequest` already causes a text. The owner decided on 30 October 2026 that it is built with S1.27, as this plan says; the pilot limits (10 a number a day, 500 a day, 20 in the window) bound the spend until then. Cost if wrong: one new bucket in the limiter and a declaration on the operation. **Built on 5 November 2026 (OB-S1-65, closed):** not a bucket but the seventh name of the limiter's closed set, declared by the five operations that write a notification envelope and admitted by the authentication step after the session's bucket.

**Part f (the handshake code store and the linkless SMS kind) is not built (30 October 2026), and why.** (1) The handshake is bound to a stop (`PickupStop`), which does not exist until S1.20; the cap of three and the sixty seconds between sends are enforced "per stop" in the database, and an outbox envelope's aggregate must exist in the writer's transaction (`outbox_insert_guard`), so there is no honest way to write and test them before the stop table. (2) The handshake settings of settings.md §7.4 (`handshake_code_length`, `handshake_code_ttl_minutes`, `handshake_max_wrong_attempts`, `sms_fallback_max_per_pickup`, `sms_fallback_cooldown_seconds`) have no stated scope, hub or platform, which the seed's header already gives as the reason they are not seeded; seeding them would invent one (OB-S1-19). **Design recorded for the builder (S1.26 and S1.27):** the code is generated by the system when the collection is recorded; it is stored hashed for verification (a keyed hash, compared in constant time) and **sealed to its stop** (as the credential payload is: `delivery-payload.ts`) for the one send, then erased when the notification ends; the lane opens the sealed code only under the `notification` capability and only from the envelope's own aggregate; the envelope's aggregate type `PICKUP_STOP` and the reader of it are added in the migration that creates the stop (the check list and the insert guard grow there); the cap and the cooldown are a trigger on `outbox` insert for the event `pickup.handshake.fallback_requested` per aggregate (`SMS_FALLBACK_LIMIT_REACHED`), so no process can exceed them; the two messages (`HANDSHAKE_CODE_SENDER`, `HANDSHAKE_CODE_VENDOR_NUMBER`) are already in the list as planned, and carry no link. A hub reassignment between an act and its relay raises `SCOPE_MISMATCH` and the security signal (a legitimate case once hubs can be reassigned: that task adjusts it deliberately), and an Ops feed entry meant for Senior Ops alone would be readable by Ops Staff under `notification.read` (A S P): the first such message decides whether it needs its own key.

### Stage 3 — Pickup requests (API and Ops screens)

The front door of the parcel journey: booking, confirming, the one-package exception, cancelling and the charge, rescheduling. The stage ends at **Gate 3** (S1.19).

#### S1.13 — Pickup request tables and the two reads

| | |
|---|---|
| **Needs**| [S1.8](#s18--bundle-and-fixed-policy-additions-for-the-slices-keys-gate-1), [S1.10](#s110--non-production-fixtures-and-test-kit-arrangements) |
| **Run**| B D |
| **Operations**| `listPickupRequests`, `getPickupRequest` |
| **Criteria**| none |

**Builds on.** The reference tables and fixtures (S1.10), `OperationRunner.read`, `@RequiresPermission`, the existence-rule probe (`existingRecordHub`, `RECORD_ENTITIES`) and the ETag and page-token helpers modelled on `staff-identity-etag.ts` and `staff-identity-page-token.ts`.
**Newly builds.** One migration for `pickup_request` (the fields of [domain-model.md](../../contracts/domain-model.md) §6.2 and the intent columns) and `ad_hoc_sender` (`PROVISIONAL` or `REUSABLE`), each owned by `melarc_owner` with forced row-level security: a staff context by hub, a vendor by its own organisation and **never by hub**, a rider none, `SYSTEM` only under named capabilities, with registry rows and a `RECORD_ENTITIES` row. The `src/pickup/` module with the two reads, paged, filtered by state and service date. The existence rule: a vendor asking for another vendor's request is answered exactly as for one that does not exist; an Ops user outside the hubs of the request is `HUB_SCOPE_VIOLATION`, and for a request that does not exist `NOT_FOUND`. The read sets the record's `ETag`.
**Files.** `apps/api/src/pickup/pickup.module.ts`, `pickup-request.schema.ts`, `ad-hoc-sender.schema.ts`, `pickup-requests.controller.ts`, `pickup-requests.service.ts`, `pickup-requests-providers.ts`, `pickup-request-view.ts`, `pickup-request-etag.ts`, `pickup-request-page-token.ts`, one migration, `apps/api/src/app.module.ts` (the module), `implemented-scope.ts`, `route-inventory.test.ts`, `apps/api/test/database/pickup-request-reads-routes.db.test.ts`.
**Evidence.** Route tests as each principal; the equality test for the existence rule (status, code, body and every header, the request id and `Date` apart); matrix Family C rows C4 to C8 against the new table; the list is scoped for a vendor and for a hub. *Real-operation verified for the two reads; AC-79 completes at S1.17.*
**Obligations carried.** The table is empty until S1.14; no fixture creates a request.
**Does not.** Create or change a request; put an estimate in a read.
**Stop if.** A rider read of a request is needed (the registry gives riders none).

#### S1.14 — `createPickupRequest`, the ad-hoc lookup and the saved-location read

| | |
|---|---|
| **Needs**| [S1.13](#s113--pickup-request-tables-and-the-two-reads), [S1.5](#s15--launch-seed-for-the-accra-hub-and-the-address-to-zone-resolver), [S1.6](#s16--pricing-calculation-and-the-booking-estimate-pure), [S1.7](#s17--reason-domains-seeds-and-the-fixed-pickup-failure-categories), [S1.10](#s110--non-production-fixtures-and-test-kit-arrangements) |
| **Run**| B D |
| **Operations**| `createPickupRequest` |
| **Criteria**| 02, 08, 09, 69 to 72, 81† |

**Builds on.** S1.13; the idempotency store (principal, operation, key, fingerprint); `OperationRunner.mutate`; the vendor session and the fixed Vendor policy; the zone resolver and the estimate (S1.5, S1.6); the company settings `value_declaration_threshold` and `liability_cap_amount`.
**Newly builds.** `createPickupRequest` with `Idempotency-Key`, by Ops or by a vendor for itself, entering `PENDING`. The three intents: `OWN_PACKAGES` reuses the vendor and its selected or default saved location and refuses a collection point or item as mis-declared; `COLLECT_FOR_VENDOR` needs the collection point, reference and item and a destination (or the vendor's default, refused if it has none), and sends the rider to the collection point; `ADHOC_SENDER` takes exactly one of an existing sender and a new one, creates the reusable profile and the request as one idempotent act, never promotes it to a vendor, and requires today's goods every time. `resolved_pickup_location` is derived by the server. The minimum of two packages (`PICKUP_MINIMUM_NOT_MET`); Monday to Saturday and the zone's days (`SERVICE_DATE_NOT_PERMITTED`, `ZONE_NOT_SERVICED_ON_DATE`, `CORRIDOR_DAY_NOT_PERMITTED`); a suspended sender refused (`SENDER_SUSPENDED`) and its non-terminal requests held as D-07 decides; a declared value above the threshold needs the cap acknowledgement and snapshots the cap in force (`VALUE_DECLARATION_REQUIRED`); an override never rewrites a saved default. The response carries the indicative estimate, labelled provisional and stored as no price. The two reads D-07 adds: a returning ad-hoc sender by verified phone, and a vendor's saved locations.
**Files.** `apps/api/src/pickup/pickup-request-creation.service.ts`, `pickup-intents.ts`, `ad-hoc-senders.service.ts`, `pickup-requests.controller.ts` (extended), `apps/api/src/vendor/vendor-pickup-locations.*` (the read), tests in `apps/api/test/database/`.
**Evidence.** AC-02, 08, 09, 69 to 72 and 81 on the real route as a vendor and as Ops; the same booking entered by Ops and by a vendor yields an identical record; the same key with another body is `IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_CONFLICT`; matrix Families R and SB for the ad-hoc sender where they apply. *Real-operation verified; AC-08's suspension is made by the test kit (SLICE-008 owns the act), so AC-08 is fixture verified until then.*
**Obligations carried.** OB-S1-04 (fixtures stand for SLICE-008).
**Does not.** Confirm; calculate an authoritative price; store the estimate; create a charge or reserve credit (§36.11).
**Stop if.** The representation of a held request is not decided (D-07), or a vendor's goods profile is needed (it is defined nowhere).

#### S1.15 — `confirmPickupRequest`

| | |
|---|---|
| **Needs**| [S1.14](#s114--createpickuprequest-the-ad-hoc-lookup-and-the-saved-location-read) |
| **Run**| B D |
| **Operations**| `confirmPickupRequest`, `resolveCutoffException` (D-07) |
| **Criteria**| 01, 04, 10 |

**Builds on.** S1.14; `OperationRunner.mutate` with the lock-then-compare-`If-Match` pattern of `approveStaffIdentity`; the hub settings of S1.4.
**Newly builds.** `PENDING → CONFIRMED` under `If-Match`, with the guards revalidated after the lock (minimum, service date, zone day, sender, attempt cap). The response carries the indicative estimate (base fee times packages for a standard request; destination-aware for a one-package exception; none outside Accra), presented as provisional. No credit is reserved and no charge created. A request confirmed after the hub's `booking_cutoff_time` (read on the database clock in `Africa/Accra`, with the grace window for a vendor that has settled) confirms and sets `cutoff_exception_state` to `PENDING_OPS_REVIEW`; it is never an error; an unset cutoff records the condition and the confirmation proceeds. The request joins the unmanifested pool. `resolveCutoffException` (built under D-07; `pickup.request.confirm`, a mandatory note, `Idempotency-Key` and `If-Match`) decides a request waiting in `PENDING_OPS_REVIEW`: `APPROVE_SAME_DAY` settles it as `SAME_DAY_APPROVED`; `RESCHEDULE_NEXT_DAY` is the cancel-plus-clone in one transaction (the source `CANCELLED` by Ops, `RESCHEDULED`, carrying `replacement_request_id`; a `PENDING` clone for the next Monday-to-Saturday day after the later of the service date and today on the hub's clock). The clone is the same booking (`pickup-request-clone.ts` classifies every column of the table as carried, set by the act or fresh, and a test fails when a column is in none) and keeps the source's attempt number: nothing was attempted, so the cap cannot refuse it. A decline is not built: the table's guard refuses the move to `DECLINED`.
**Decided in the build** (rulings of 29 October 2026). The cutoff is judged on the database's clock read on `Africa/Accra`; the cutoff itself is on time and the first instant after it is late; the grace window is minutes after the cutoff, given to a registered vendor with no `OVERDUE` standing and to nobody else. A cutoff or an attempt cap that is unset or invalid decides nothing and blocks nothing: the audit event records `cutoff_check` / `attempt_cap_check` (`SETTING_MISSING`, `SETTING_INVALID`). A blank note is `REASON_REQUIRED` (422), judged before the audit's screening of the note (400). One enhanced event, `pickup.request.cutoff_exception_resolved`, records the act (decision, actor, note, a before-and-after of the exception, the replacement link); the clone gets its own `pickup.request.created`; the source gets no separate `pickup.request.cancelled`. The answer is the source request.
**Files.** `apps/api/src/pickup/{cutoff,pickup-request-loader,pickup-request-confirmation.service,pickup-request-confirmation.controller,cutoff-exception.service,cutoff-exception.controller,cutoff-exception-schemas,pickup-request-clone,pickup-headers}.ts`, `booking-rules.ts` (`nextOperatingDay`), `estimate-view.ts` (`estimateOf`), migration `0050`, tests.
**Evidence.** AC-01 (including the identical record for a request entered by Ops from WhatsApp), AC-04, AC-10 (two Ops users read one `PENDING` request and both confirm: one succeeds, the other `STATE_CONFLICT`, and a held-row race that proves the version is compared under the lock); an unset cutoff does not block; the audit `pickup.request.confirmed`; AC-06 for the reschedule (the source and the clone are never both active). *Real-operation verified.*
**Obligations carried.** Whether a `CONFIRMED` request still in `PENDING_OPS_REVIEW` is manifest-eligible is stated nowhere: S1.20 decides, and the safe reading is that it is not claimable until it is decided. S1.16 replaces the minimum guard's "an exception id is present" with "an approved exception", and gives a one-package request its destination-aware estimate (`estimateOf` returns none for a single package until then).
**Does not.** Reserve credit; create a charge; persist the estimate; reject a late booking.
**Stop if.** The estimate's existence or persistence is unresolved (F-06, D-01).

#### S1.16 — The one-package exception

| | |
|---|---|
| **Needs**| [S1.15](#s115--confirmpickuprequest) |
| **Run**| B D |
| **Operations**| `requestOnePackageException`, `approveOnePackageException` |
| **Criteria**| 03 |

**Builds on.** S1.15; the maker-checker rule and the trigger pattern of migration `0032` (`SELF_APPROVAL_FORBIDDEN` is the last refusal and does not apply at tier P).
**Newly builds.** The `one_package_exception` table (migration `0051`; registry `OnePackageException`) and the uniform `Approval` it answers. `requestOnePackageException` (Ops, `pickup.exception.request`; a `PENDING` request of one package with no live exception, the recipient's name, phone and delivery address and a note); `approveOnePackageException` by **any holder of `pickup.exception.approve`**, never the requester unless the grant that authorizes it is a Platform Admin's (`SELF_APPROVAL_FORBIDDEN` is the last refusal, after the permission, the hub and the state; a trigger is the backstop). The approval links the exception to the request, which is what makes it confirmable (the confirmation's minimum guard asks for an approved exception), and snapshots the `single_package_pickup_fee` as **due, not collected**. A request and the approver's queue (`one_package_exception_state`) show the exception, in the representation and so in the ETag.
**Decided in the build** (rulings of 29 October 2026). The exception is a record of its own and not a generic `approval` table: the contract's `Approval` is the shape both operations answer. **The approval travels with the booking**: a clone (the reschedule of a late booking, S1.18's failure reschedule) carries `one_package_exception_id`, so the same trip needs no second approval and owes no second fee; the key from the request is therefore a plain key, and a trigger holds that a request names only an approved exception raised for it (migration `0053` made it so) or the one the request it replaces carries. A vendor is refused `PERMISSION_DENIED` (the contract; the plan's `EXCEPTION_NOT_AVAILABLE_TO_SELF_SERVICE` is the deferred code of state-machines.md and is not used). A fee that cannot be read never blocks an approval: the exception shows none and the audit event records `fee_check`. `pickup.request.single_package_fee_posted` is not emitted: posting to the vendor's statement or gating on prepayment is `SLICE-011`'s. Two enhanced events record an approval (`pickup.exception.approved`: which actor and which key and tier; `pickup.request.single_package_approved`: the request's timeline) and `pickup.exception.requested` the ask, all against the `PickupRequest` object with the exception in `relatedIds`. The estimate of a single package with an approved exception is the fee that applies at the recipient's address (resolved to a zone by the hub's own aliases) plus the single-package pickup fee, and **none while the address belongs to no known zone**: the alias data is the Product Owner's to supply and none is seeded. The request key can lock a request in its hubs through a policy whose check is false: it can never change one. `INSUFFICIENT_AUTHORITY` is no longer declared for the approval: any holder approves, with no tier requirement.
**Files.** `apps/api/src/pickup/{one-package-exception.service,one-package-exception.controller,one-package-exception-schemas,one-package-exception-view,pickup-note}.ts`, the table in `pickup-request.schema.ts`, `pickup-request-loader.ts` and `pickup-requests.service.ts` (the exception in the representation and the queue), `estimate-view.ts` (`estimateOf` for a single package), migration `0051`, tests.
**Evidence.** AC-03: a user without the key is `PERMISSION_DENIED`; any holder approves; the requester is `SELF_APPROVAL_FORBIDDEN` and a Platform Admin may; the recipient fields are mandatory; the fee is due; confirming before the approval is `PICKUP_MINIMUM_NOT_MET`. Two held-row races prove the request and the exception are judged under their locks, and a third that a clone carries the approval. The "explicitly granted" path is tested with a bundle that carries the key (D-14). *Real-operation verified.*
**What a vendor reads** (the owner's decision of 29 October 2026). A Vendor reads the outcome of its own exception only: the state, when it was asked for and decided, the recipient and the fee that became due; `requested_by`, `approved_by` and `note` are staff's and are absent from every read that is not staff's (the contract states the two audiences as two views, `OnePackageException` for staff, which requires the three, and `OnePackageExceptionOutcome` for every other reader, which is closed and has none of them, so that each guarantee is a rule a validator enforces: corrected on 1 November 2026, when the portal's guard and the contract's one shared shape, which made the three optional, disagreed; the registry row says the same). The audience is derived from the principal and fails closed: only a staff principal reads the whole exception. The ETag digests the outcome alone, so every audience holds one token for one request and a vendor's `If-Match` is the token Ops holds (`pickup-request-view.ts`, `pickup-request-loader.ts`; tests in `pickup-one-package-exception-routes.db.test.ts` and `pickup-request-etag.test.ts`).
**Found in the review of 29 October 2026 and fixed:** approving the exception of a request that had been cancelled since (its exception stays `REQUESTED`, so the approver's queue still lists it) ended in a server error from the request's guard; the approval now needs a `PENDING` request and answers `STATE_CONFLICT`. **Since task C1.1 (6 November 2026, Product Owner decision D4 of 5 November) the approver's queue no longer lists such a request:** a request that is not pending (a cancelled one, for example) is out of the list filter `one_package_exception_state=REQUESTED` and out of the count (`AC-QSUM-12`, the reporting data dictionary; the Product Owner's decision of 6 November 2026 made it every state but `PENDING`). A page that was read before the cancellation can still show one, and the Ops Portal offers it no approval and says so.
**Obligations carried.** OQ-047: per-user overrides stay open. Nothing refuses an exception (declining a request is deferred, D-07): `REJECTED` is in the machine and no operation of the slice produces it, so an exception that is asked for and never approved leaves its request `PENDING` until the request is cancelled (S1.17). The address-to-zone alias data (OB-S1-02) is what lets a single package show an estimate.
**Does not.** Collect or post the fee; refuse an exception; invent where the recipient details live (D-07 placed them on the exception).
**Stop if.** D-07 has not placed the recipient capture.

#### S1.17 — Cancellation and the cancellation charge

| | |
|---|---|
| **Needs**| [S1.15](#s115--confirmpickuprequest), [S1.7](#s17--reason-domains-seeds-and-the-fixed-pickup-failure-categories) |
| **Run**| B D |
| **Operations**| `cancelPickupRequest`, `waiveCancellationCharge` |
| **Criteria**| 05, 60 to 64, 79 |

**Builds on.** S1.15, S1.13; the reason validator (S1.7); the hub setting `cancellation_charge_amount` and its snapshot.
**Newly builds.** `cancelPickupRequest` with `Idempotency-Key` and `If-Match`. A vendor may cancel only its own `PENDING` request (`PERMISSION_DENIED` otherwise); Ops at any point, with no charge before rider assignment. After assignment `apply_charge` and a charge reason are required, and the charge is **discretionary**: `NOT_APPLIED` when Ops elects none, `UNAVAILABLE` when the hub's amount is unset (never `SETTING_MISSING`), `EXEMPT` whenever the reason's attribution is `MELARC`, whatever the caller sent. The charge is snapshotted with its setting key and version. `waiveCancellationCharge` (Senior Ops): total, never partial; refused with `NO_CHARGE_APPLIED` for an outcome of `UNAVAILABLE`, `NOT_APPLIED` or `EXEMPT`, and then no waiver event is written.
**Decided in the build** (29 October 2026). The request is read, then locked, and `If-Match` is compared under the lock; a vendor that could not lock a request that has since been confirmed is told `STATE_CONFLICT`, and one that asks for a confirmed request is told `PERMISSION_DENIED` (the contract), another vendor's request is `NOT_FOUND`. The reason is validated against `PICKUP_CANCELLATION` and snapshotted with its label; the catalogue is the office's, so migration `0052` lets a vendor read the reasons of that domain, and of none other, while it runs `pickup.request.cancel`. A cancellation of a request no rider holds records no outcome, and a decision offered for it is `VALIDATION_FAILED`. After assignment the election and its reason are mandatory (`REASON_REQUIRED`); electing needs `pickup.request.cancel_chargeable`, and a vendor that sends either member is `PERMISSION_DENIED`. The charge reason is a reason of the same domain (assumed: the contract says only "a code of the `PICKUP_CANCELLATION` reason domain"). `pickup.request.cancelled` records the free cancellation and `pickup.request.cancelled_chargeable` every post-assignment one, with the outcome and the charge reason. The waiver needs a note (`REASON_REQUIRED`), a cancelled request (`STATE_CONFLICT`) and an applied charge (`NO_CHARGE_APPLIED`), is made under the request's lock and version, and writes one enhanced event with the before and after; `INSUFFICIENT_AUTHORITY` is no longer declared for it (only a Senior Ops or Platform Admin grant carries the key).
**Files.** `apps/api/src/pickup/{pickup-request-cancellation.service,pickup-request-cancellation.controller,cancellation-schemas}.ts` (the charge decision lives in the service), migration `0052`, tests (`pickup-cancellation-routes`, `reason-code-vendor-read`).
**Evidence.** AC-05, 60 to 64 and 79 (a vendor asking for another's request, or cancelling it, is answered as for an id that matches nothing; the vendor's list holds only its own; an Ops user outside the hub is `HUB_SCOPE_VIOLATION`); audit `pickup.request.cancelled`, `pickup.request.cancelled_chargeable`, `pickup.cancellation_charge.waived` (enhanced); held-row races prove the version is compared under the lock for the cancellation and for the waiver. *Real-operation verified.*
**Obligations carried.** D-05: **the codes of the `PICKUP_CANCELLATION` domain are the Product Owner's and none is seeded**, so these tests prove the mechanism with fixture codes of their own; a production cancellation cannot be accepted until the codes exist.
**Does not.** Create a replacement (S1.18); collect a charge.
**Stop if.** The cancellation reason domain has no decided codes (D-05).

#### S1.18 — The reschedule chain, the attempt cap and escalation

| | |
|---|---|
| **Needs**| [S1.17](#s117--cancellation-and-the-cancellation-charge) |
| **Run**| B D |
| **Operations**| `extendPickupAttempts`, `escalatePickupRequest` |
| **Criteria**| 06, 07 |

**Builds on.** S1.17; the hub setting `MELARC_MAX_PICKUP_ATTEMPTS`.
**Newly builds.** `create_replacement` on a cancellation: in the same transaction the source is `CANCELLED` with its reason preserved and a new `PENDING` request carries `attempt_number + 1` and `replaces_request_id`; the two are never active together (`SOURCE_STILL_ACTIVE`, `REPLACEMENT_REQUIRED`). The cap is refused at the operation the contract names after S1.2 (`PICKUP_ATTEMPT_LIMIT_REACHED`). `extendPickupAttempts` (Senior Ops and Platform Admin; the reason required by schema; before and after audited) raises the ceiling for one request. `escalatePickupRequest` is the terminal Ops resolution; a `REFUSED_COLLECTION` failure or an exhausted chain leaves only by escalation or extension.
**Decided in the build** (29 October 2026). **The reschedule** is Ops's and of a `CONFIRMED` request (a vendor is `PERMISSION_DENIED`; a `PENDING` request was never attempted, `STATE_CONFLICT`). The clone is the same booking (`pickup-request-clone.ts` says which columns it carries) at the next attempt, carrying the one-package approval and the extension of its source, for **the next operating day after the later of the source's day and today** (**provisional**: the contract names a day only for a late booking, `replaceRequest` is the one place to change it). The charge of the cancellation is decided as for any cancellation. The next attempt past the ceiling is `PICKUP_ATTEMPT_LIMIT_REACHED` and nothing is written; **a cap that is unset or invalid blocks nothing** and the event records `attempt_cap_check`. The cutoff reschedule (S1.15) and this one share the clone and its timeline (`pickup-request-replacement.ts`). **Never active together** is also held by the database where the transaction ends (a deferred constraint trigger). **The extension** is a record of its own (`pickup_attempt_extension`, migration `0053`; registry `PickupAttemptExtension`; never changed or removed): actor, reason, moment, and the ceiling before and after. **It permits one further attempt** (**provisional**: "exact extension ceilings remain domain-design follow-up", section 21.6; `attempt-ceiling.ts` is the one place to change it): the replacement that attempt makes carries it so that it can be confirmed, and a further reschedule needs a further extension. It needs a `CONFIRMED` request that cannot go on without it; a request that can, one in any other state, a hub with no cap (no ceiling applies) and one already extended for this attempt are `STATE_CONFLICT`. The confirmation reads the same ceiling. **The escalation** is a marker and a judgement, not a state: `escalated_at` is set once on a `CONFIRMED` request under a mandatory note (the actor, the trigger `OPS` and the note are in the enhanced audit event), nothing else changes, and the request can still be cancelled. **Contract changes** (deliberate): `PickupRequest.escalated_at`; the error lists and the `400` and `404` answers of both operations; `INSUFFICIENT_AUTHORITY` is no longer declared for the extension; the descriptions.
**Stated, from the review of 29 October 2026.** `REPLACEMENT_REQUIRED` and `SOURCE_STILL_ACTIVE` are declared on `cancelPickupRequest` and **nothing emits them**: a reschedule is one command, and the database holds "never active together" at commit (a violation there is a server error, a defect and not a case). A cancellation of a request still waiting for its cutoff review leaves `cutoff_exception_state` as `PENDING_OPS_REVIEW` on the cancelled row, and a reschedule of such a never-attempted late booking spends a cap attempt (the right path for it is `resolveCutoffException`, which keeps the attempt): nothing yet knows that a confirmed request was never tried, and S1.28 does. A vendor reads the one-package exception of its own request, with the Ops note and the staff ids on it (the registry says so): the owner confirms. `apply_charge: true` without `pickup.request.cancel_chargeable` is `PERMISSION_DENIED` even where the reason is attributed to Melarc (the contract says an election is "ignored" there and also that the key is required: the key is read first).
**Files.** `apps/api/src/pickup/{pickup-request-chain.service,pickup-request-chain.controller,chain-schemas,attempt-ceiling,pickup-request-replacement}.ts`, the cancellation service and the clone, migration `0053`, tests (`pickup-chain-routes`, `pickup-attempt-extension-table`, the replacement tests of `pickup-cancellation-routes`).
**Evidence.** AC-06 (service level and route), AC-07 (Ops is refused at the cap; Senior Ops extends with a reason; the extension records actor, reason, before and after and an audit event; the reschedule then succeeds and its replacement can be confirmed, and a further one is refused again); audit `pickup.attempt.extended` and `pickup.request.escalated` (both enhanced). Held-row races prove the version is compared under the lock for the reschedule, the extension and the escalation. *Real-operation verified; AC-33 completes at S1.28.*
**Obligations carried.** **S1.28** narrows the escalation to a request whose stop failed and adds the backstop (`trigger: BACKSTOP`), makes a `REFUSED_COLLECTION` failure refuse an ordinary reschedule, and the extension then opens it; **until then a confirmed request need not have failed to be escalated**. Nothing yet says what resolves an escalated request (the senior decision queue is S1.30's).
**Does not.** Record a failure (S1.28).
**Stop if.** The operation that refuses at the cap is still undecided after S1.2.

#### S1.19 — Ops screens: pickup requests (Gate 3)

| | |
|---|---|
| **Needs**| [S1.18](#s118--the-reschedule-chain-the-attempt-cap-and-escalation), [S1.16](#s116--the-one-package-exception) |
| **Run**| B W E |
| **Operations**| none |
| **Criteria**| none |

**Builds on.** The Ops Portal shell, the route registry (`navigation/route-registry.tsx`), `Table`, `ReadState`, `Dialog`, `ReasonConfirmationDialog`, `Select`, `DatePicker`; the generated client and the session boundary; [ops-portal.md](../../surfaces/ops-portal.md) lines 82 to 86.
**Newly builds.** The routes `/pickup-requests`, `/pickup-requests/new`, `/pickup-requests/{id}`, `/pickup-requests/{id}/exception` and `/approvals`, each behind its permission key. The booking form for the three intents with the declared value and cap acknowledgement and the split-payer fields; the estimate shown as an estimate; confirm, cancel with the charge decision, waive, extend, escalate and reschedule, each sending the version it read; the approver's queue naming the permission and never a role. New components, each built for its screen and added to the showcase: a filterable, paged queue table, a searchable picker (vendor, sender), a radio group, a money display, an attempt-chain timeline.
**Decided in the build** (29 October 2026). The screens compute no price, total, fee or zone: money is an integer of pesewas from the wire to `MoneyAmount`, the estimate is the server's and is told as indicative and provisional, and a fee is drawn only as the server snapshotted it (the browser stand-in plants a price in answers that never carry one, and a test looks for it). A cancellation takes its reason from the `PICKUP_CANCELLATION` catalogue and no code is written in a page: **with no codes seeded (D-05) the dialog says so, naming `settings.reason.manage`, and no cancellation can be made from it**. An escalation, an extension, a waiver and a late booking's decision take a mandatory note. The two acts of the exception send neither a version nor a key, as the contract has it; `SELF_APPROVAL_FORBIDDEN` is the server's answer and the page only tells it, naming the permission. `/approvals` is the list of requests whose exception is `REQUESTED` (a real filter of `listPickupRequests`), behind `pickup.exception.approve`. A request that is escalated shows when (`escalated_at`) and is not offered a second escalation. The Ops Portal's copy of the bundles (`BUNDLE_KEY_SETS`) and its comparison with the seed now read the slice's grants, and the navigation tests show each bundle the three pickup entries its keys allow. The components are written up in COMPONENT_PATTERNS `22b` to `22e` with status IMPLEMENTING until the owner accepts the composed screens.
**Files.** `apps/ops-web/src/app/pages/pickup/*`, `apps/ops-web/src/components/ui/{queue-table,picker,radio,money,timeline}/*`, `apps/ops-web/src/platform/pickup/*`, `navigation/route-registry.tsx`, the showcase, component tests and `test/browser/pickup.spec.ts` with its API stand-in.
**Evidence.** **Gate 3, as far as it ran:** Ops Portal unit tests for the pickup screens, the components and the navigation (26 files, 765 tests, and the shell and navigation files, 80 tests); the browser spec on Chromium against the production build and against the development server, 45 and 45 tests, which include the axe check (WCAG 2.2 A and AA) of every screen at 1280, 768 and 320 px with no sideways scrolling, enlarged text, forced colours, reduced motion and the exact headers of each act; no hand-written API type (`test/pickup-contract.test.ts` holds the guards to the contract). *Client evidence for AC-01 to 10 and 60 to 64; journeys at S1.49.* **Not run: Firefox and WebKit (not installed on the development machine), which the gate asks for (OB-S1-16).**
**Obligations carried.** The page-level inventory (`OQ-030`) is satisfied for these routes by this task's description, which S1.55 records in the surface document. The owner's acceptance of the composed screens (the look) is owed.
**Does not.** Show a price as authoritative; decide a state or a total in the client.
**Stop if.** A screen needs a field the contract does not carry.

### Stage 4 — Manifests and runs (API and Ops screens)

From the pool of confirmed requests to a started run: building, assigning, dispatching, starting, skipping and handing custody over. The stage ends at **Gate 4** (S1.25).

#### S1.20 — Manifest and stop tables; create, list and get; the pool

| | |
|---|---|
| **Needs**| [S1.15](#s115--confirmpickuprequest), [S1.10](#s110--non-production-fixtures-and-test-kit-arrangements) |
| **Run**| B D |
| **Operations**| `createPickupManifest`, `listPickupManifests`, `getPickupManifest` |
| **Criteria**| 11 |

**Builds on.** S1.13 to S1.15; the rider fixtures; matrix Families C and D.
**Newly builds.** One migration for `pickup_manifest` and `pickup_stop`, forced row-level security: staff by hub, a vendor **denied both**, a rider by assignment (the stop inherits the manifest's assignment, not the hub). `createPickupManifest` with `Idempotency-Key`: only `CONFIRMED` requests of the manifest's hub that are on no other manifest, each becoming a stop with a manual sequence (`ALREADY_MANIFESTED`, or the code S1.2 fixes). The read of the unmanifested pool: the `manifested` filter of `listPickupRequests` (`state=CONFIRMED&manifested=false`). `listPickupManifests` scoped (a rider only its own, staff by hub, never a vendor). `getPickupManifest` with the stops embedded and **an `ETag`**; a rider asking for a manifest not assigned to it is answered as for one that does not exist; staff outside the hub `HUB_SCOPE_VIOLATION`.
**Files.** `apps/api/src/pickup/pickup-manifest.schema.ts`, `pickup-stop.schema.ts`, `pickup-manifests.controller.ts`, `.service.ts`, `-providers.ts`, `pickup-manifest-etag.ts`, one migration, tests.
**Evidence.** AC-11 (only confirmed, unmanifested requests are eligible; a request on another manifest cannot be added to a second); concurrent overlapping creation (one claim wins, no partial manifest, no orphan stop); mixed authorized and unauthorized input (refused whole, no existence leak, even for a foreign request that is claimed); a service-date mismatch and a request awaiting cutoff review; the claim rule enforced by the database (a skip does not release a claim); matrix C9, D1 to D3 and D9 to D11; the `ETag` changes when a stop's order changes (by fixture until S1.21). *Real-operation verified; AC-80 completes at S1.22.*
**Decided 1 November 2026 ([§11](#11-decisions-recorded)).** The ten calls of the S1.20 row there govern this block where they differ from the text above: the claim (a stop with no `released_at`), the service date (the day the run is worked: on or after every request's promised day, and not in the past), the pool (a request awaiting cutoff review is out of it), and the rider's visibility (from dispatch, while assigned).
**Obligations carried.** [OB-S1-21](#7-deferred-obligations--what-is-owed-and-not-yet-run) (`pickup_summary` before dispatch), OB-S1-22 (the release of a claim), OB-S1-23 (the vendor's read of its own stop), OB-S1-26 (a request cancelled after the claim), OB-S1-27 (carry-over), OB-S1-28 (the size of a manifest). *Since decided, on 2 November 2026 (their rows in [§7](#7-deferred-obligations--what-is-owed-and-not-yet-run)): OB-S1-21 (closed), OB-S1-26, OB-S1-27 and OB-S1-28 (closed).*
**Does not.** Assign, dispatch or start.
**Stop if.** A manifest read must show a vendor another vendor's stop.

#### S1.21 — Order, assign, dispatch and cancel a manifest

| | |
|---|---|
| **Needs**| [S1.20](#s120--manifest-and-stop-tables-create-list-and-get-the-pool), [S1.12](#s112--the-notification-service-outbox-sms-through-hubtel-receipts-and-the-ops-log-gate-2) |
| **Run**| B D |
| **Operations**| `setManifestStopOrder`, `assignRider`, `dispatchManifest` |
| **Criteria**| 12, 13, 15, 19 |

**Builds on.** S1.20; the rider fixtures (S1.10); the notification service (S1.12).
**Newly builds.** `setManifestStopOrder` (manual, `DRAFT` only; no optimisation). `assignRider`: `RIDER_UNAVAILABLE` (`NO_SERVICEABLE_MOTORCYCLE` stays declared on the operation and is not emitted until SLICE-011: the owner decided on 29 October 2026 to skip the check); sets `assigned_rider_id` and `assigned_at`; **this is the moment vendor self-cancellation closes** on every request on the manifest, and office cancellation is unaffected. `dispatchManifest`: at least one stop and a rider (`MANIFEST_EMPTY`); the stops become visible to the rider; the rider's text (D-11: one run-level message, on dispatch and again on reassignment) is written to the outbox in the same transaction; dispatch does not start the run. `cancelPickupManifest` (provisional name): `DRAFT` or `DISPATCHED` only, `RUN_ALREADY_STARTED` after, the stops moving to `SKIPPED`. A rider cannot add or substitute a stop (`PERMISSION_DENIED`).
**Files.** `apps/api/src/pickup/manifest-planning.service.ts`, `manifest-dispatch.service.ts`, controllers, tests.
**Evidence.** AC-12 (assignment records `assigned_at`; a later vendor self-cancel is refused with the code S1.1 fixes; office cancellation succeeds), AC-13, AC-19 (AC-15, a breakdown between assign and dispatch, is not exercised: it moves to SLICE-011); two Ops dispatching at once, one `STATE_CONFLICT`; audit `pickup.manifest.rider_assigned`, `.dispatched`, `.stops_ordered`. *Real-operation verified.*
