**Prerequisites from S1.20 (1 and 2 November 2026).** The cancellation of a request resolves its stop itself, on a draft manifest and on a dispatched one (the second closeout); `skipPickupStop` is S1.23, and **S1.21 depends on nothing of it**. `dispatchManifest` still re-reads the request of every stop still to be worked (`PENDING` or `ARRIVED`, not released) under the manifest's lock and refuses with `STATE_CONFLICT`, naming the stops, if any is not `CONFIRMED`, **as a second safeguard**: a cancelled request is never sent to a rider, and a refused dispatch sends nothing, writes no outbox envelope and leaves the manifest `DRAFT` (a test of its own). `assignRider` sets `rider_assigned` on every request of the manifest (nothing else sets it; the vendor window and the cancellation charge read it), and the cancellation or reassignment of a manifest keeps it true; the cancellation of a manifest releases its claims (OB-S1-22) and moves its stops to `SKIPPED`, leaving a stop already resolved as it is. A stop reaches a rider only with its `pickup_summary` (OB-S1-21). When the office cancels a request while its rider is travelling it contacts the rider (OB-S1-30).
**Obligations carried.** OB-S1-04, OB-S1-22, OB-S1-30, OB-S1-34 (the requirement and the design approved, in [§7.4](#74-the-proposed-record-of-the-offices-contact-with-a-rider-ob-s1-34); the record is not built and is a separate bounded task), OB-S1-37. **Closed by the closeout of 2 November 2026:** OB-S1-06 (the rider’s text approved, wording only), OB-S1-21 (the payload approved), OB-S1-35 (the current rider commitment, built) and OB-S1-36 (the names approved).
**Built (2 November 2026).** `setManifestStopOrder`, `assignRider`, `cancelPickupManifest` and `dispatchManifest` (migrations `0060` and `0063`; `pickup-manifest-planning.service.ts`, `pickup-manifest-dispatch.service.ts` and their controllers, `pickup-manifest-order.ts`, `pickup-manifest-loader.ts`, `pickup-hub-day.ts`), with AC-12, AC-13 (the manifest is `DISPATCHED` and not `IN_PROGRESS`, the rider’s text is queued and each stop to be worked carries its summary; the refusal of `arriveAtPickupStop` is asserted with S1.22, which builds that operation) and AC-19. **Dispatch** is judged under the manifest’s lock, in this order: the `ETag`, a draft, the manifest’s day on the hub’s clock, the rider (still `ACTIVE` and of the manifest’s hub), a stop still to be worked, and the confirmed request of each such stop. It writes the summaries, the state, the event and one envelope, or nothing: a refusal queues no text and leaves the draft as it was. The rider’s text is wired through the notification lane, of which the pickup manifest is now the second aggregate (migration `0062`; `RUN_DISPATCHED_RIDER`, a draft wording until the Product Owner approves it, OB-S1-06). **Reassigning a manifest that has gone out is not built:** assignment is of a draft only (approved), the way out is to cancel the manifest and plan its requests again, the reassignment before the start that a breakdown needs is SLICE-011’s, and `RUN_REASSIGNED_RIDER` stays planned. **`assignRider` gives a manifest only a rider of its own hub** (OB-S1-37), in the service and in the guard of migration `0063`.
**A rider’s read of a dispatched run (a real answer of the real stack, 2 November 2026; `pickup-manifest-dispatch.db.test.ts`, “shows the assigned rider the reference…”).** The stop’s `id`, `pickup_manifest_id`, `pickup_request_id`, `arrived_at`, `failure_category` and `failure` are as the contract already states them and are left out here:

```json
{
  "code": "ACC-MF-Q92422", "state": "DISPATCHED", "service_date": "2026-11-04",
  "assigned_at": "2026-11-02T07:04:43.504Z", "dispatched_at": "2026-11-02T07:04:43.608Z",
  "started_at": null, "completed_at": null,
  "stops": [
    { "sequence": 1, "state": "PENDING", "skipped": null,
      "pickup_summary": {
        "reference": "ACC-PR-CQJKJT",
        "sender_display_name": "Ama Serwaa Fashions",
        "declared_package_count": 3,
        "resolved_pickup_location": {
          "address": "12 Oxford Street, Osu", "landmark": "Opposite the Total filling station",
          "map_link": "https://maps.example/osu-12", "latitude": 5.5571, "longitude": -0.1822 },
        "location_note": null,
        "contact_phone": "+233201234567" } },
    { "sequence": 2, "state": "PENDING", "skipped": null,
      "pickup_summary": {
        "reference": "ACC-PR-ZYU26H", "sender_display_name": "Fixture Sender", "declared_package_count": 3,
        "resolved_pickup_location": { "address": "Fixture shop" },
        "location_note": null, "contact_phone": "+233200000001" } }
  ]
}
```

The request’s second contact (`+233241112222`), its goods (“Six boxes of ankara fabric”), its payer intent and every other member of the request were in the database for that test and appear nowhere in the answer; the test asserts that none of them does. `reference` was the one member beyond the Product Owner’s first list, and **the Product Owner approved the payload, `reference` included, on 2 November 2026 (OB-S1-21 is closed)**. Every value above is a synthetic fixture value, not a person’s data.
**Checked in the third round (2 November 2026, the tree before the closeout, not committed).** Against a real PostgreSQL and the real application: the dispatch (22 tests: the payload and its exact members, with the second contact and the goods absent; a stop whose request was cancelled before the dispatch or after it; the rider’s eligibility at the moment of dispatch, including an officer whose grant reaches both hubs; a day that has passed and the way out of it, and a day that is today; the `ETag`; the permissions; two officers at once; a stop taken while the dispatch waits for it; a dispatch against the cancellation of the manifest), the rider’s text and its lane (8), the claim and policy file (63), the planning file (34) and the reasons (13); the wider set of 51 database files, **1,961 tests, all passing**; the API unit project, **7,518 tests passing and 7 platform skips, none failing**; five files of the alone project (the catalogue, the seed, provisioning, the technical identities, the runtime role), where **one test failed first: a list of the notification role’s grants, pinned in S1.12a, that was stale** (three grants of migrations `0055` and `0056` that it never held, and the eight of `0062`); it is corrected and that file passes (149 tests); the Ops Portal’s typecheck and its message tests (3 files, 250 tests). **Break checks: 73 changes in two passes, 65 killed, 8 survive.** The first pass (57) left 12 survivors; five of them (the rider’s hub for an officer who sees both hubs, the message of a manifest with no rider, and the three arms of the stop’s guard, which raise the same code) showed that the tests could not tell two layers apart, and were killed once the tests were extended. Of the 8 that survive, six are **one layer of a pair that hold the same rule**, and the pair removed together is killed each time (the code’s state filter and the policy’s; the manifest policy’s state and the guard’s; the hub condition of the dispatch policy of a manifest, and of a stop, and of the read policy an update passes through; the filter on a summary that exists, the policy and the guard); the last two are clauses of the summary’s check that the clauses after them make redundant. **Not run:** the gate; `test:affected` (it selects the whole repository while `package.json` and the lockfile are changed); the browser and end-to-end suites; the verifier; the `two-builds` file of the alone project; and the two intermittent failures of the full run (the window counter’s lock, the Ops Portal’s `build.test.ts` hook) remain **unexplained**.
**The current rider commitment (OB-S1-35; the Product Owner’s decision of 2 November 2026; built the same day; migration `0064`, contract 5.82).** *The chosen implementation.* The stored mark `rider_assigned` stays and is **defined as the current commitment**, kept by the two acts that change what it is made of. It is not derived when read, because a read through row-level security cannot be trusted to find the claim (a vendor reads no stop or manifest, and an officer of another hub reads none), and the cancellation of a request reads the request it has locked. `assignRider` raises it, as before, on the confirmed requests the manifest holds. `cancelPickupManifest` lowers it, in the same transaction and after releasing the claims, on the requests that are still confirmed and whose claims it released (those claims carry its instant, so a request another manifest has claimed since is never reached from there); a cancelled request is frozen and keeps its mark and its decision. *The locks* are taken in one order, the manifest, then the requests it holds in the order of their ids, then their stops ([state-machines.md](../../contracts/state-machines.md) §5), which is the order of `cancelPickupRequest` (the request, then its stop): `cancelPickupManifest` now locks its requests before it writes a stop. *The database holds the rule for every writer* (migration `0064`, new: `0060` and `0063` are not edited): a policy admits the key of the manifest’s cancellation to update a confirmed request in a hub of the grant; the guard of a request lets that key do one thing, lower the mark, and only when a stop of the request released its claim and no stop holds it; the assignment key raises the mark only on a held claim of a manifest that has a rider; no other key changes it; each key changes the mark and nothing else. The event of the cancellation counts the commitments it ended (`detail.commitments_ended`). No grant was added and row-level security was not bypassed. The guard reads the stops through row-level security, which is complete while a request and its stops share a hub (OB-S1-33 keeps that true when a request can change hub).
*The proofs, against a real PostgreSQL and the real application* (`pickup-rider-commitment.db.test.ts`, 19 tests; `pickup-manifest-claim.db.test.ts`): the assignment, then the cancellation of a request, sees the commitment (the read says so; the decision is required and recorded); the cancellation of the manifest, then that of the request, sees none (the read says so, its `ETag` moved so that a cancellation on the earlier read is `STATE_CONFLICT`, a decision offered is `VALIDATION_FAILED`, and the cancellation records no charge and the free event); a dispatched manifest’s cancellation ends it too; the cancellation of a manifest leaves a request cancelled earlier exactly as it was, to the last column, an applied charge and an exempt one included; giving a draft another rider keeps it and marks nothing twice; a request claimed by a later manifest and given its own rider keeps its mark when the earlier manifest is cancelled again, replayed or assigned, and the database refuses to lower it while a stop holds it; a refusal (a stale or missing token, a rider not available, a blank note, a run that has started) and a failure to write the event (of the assignment, of the manifest’s cancellation, of the cancellation of a committed request) leave no mark, claim, stop, event or charge half written; the order of the locks is observed (a request is held as its cancellation holds it, the manifest’s cancellation is seen waiting for it before it has written a stop, and the stop is then free); each of the six serial orders of the three acts ends as the model says (four distinct ends); and twelve rounds of the three at once, with seeded start offsets, end in one of those four, with no deadlock, no answer of 500 and a mark that equals the claim for every request still confirmed. **Break checks of the correction: 17 changes, 15 killed, 2 survive, each explained.** K02 (the clearing statement’s own filter on a request that is not confirmed) is one layer of a pair, because the state condition of the release policy hides such a request from the statement: both removed together are killed by the guard’s frozen-request rule. K06 (the assignment’s lock of its requests before it marks them) no test can observe, because `assignRider` writes no stop and so cannot form a circle either way; it stays so that the order is one rule.
*The upgrade check (the reviewer’s finding of 2 November 2026, approved by the Product Owner).* Migration `0064` changes what the database enforces and **repairs nothing**, so a persistent database is checked before it is migrated, and a clean install proves nothing about that path. The migration cannot make the check (its login reads these tables through row-level security and would find nothing, which would pass for the wrong reason), so the check is a command run by the cluster administrator: `pnpm --filter @melarc/api run db:commitment-marks [database]` changes nothing and says which confirmed requests are marked and held by no manifest that has a rider (stale), which are held by one and not marked (missing), and how many requests that are not confirmed carry the mark (history, never touched); `--reconcile` puts right only the confirmed requests that disagree with their current claims, in one transaction that locks the three tables and checks its own result before it commits; the exit code is 0 (consistent), 2 (not) or 1 (failed). The owner stops the application and the worker, builds (`pnpm run build`: the command runs from `dist`), runs it, records the result, reconciles if it found anything, and only then applies `0064` ([MIGRATION_AND_SEEDING.md §5.5](../../architecture/MIGRATION_AND_SEEDING.md#55-an-upgrade-check-data-the-migration-cannot-see)). *Tested from the old behavior, in the order the procedure gives* (`commitment-upgrade.db.test.ts`, 6 tests; the reviewer’s finding of 2 November 2026, the sixth round): a database built through `0063` by the real runner and left what the old code left (a rider’s manifest cancelled with the marks still raised, a request cancelled after assignment and charged, a request claimed by a later manifest that has a rider, a held request never marked, a mark on a manifest with no rider). **The test walks the procedure’s own order, which its first version did not** (it migrated before it reconciled, so it showed that the reconciliation works under the guard of `0064` and nothing about the guard of `0060` that the procedure runs it under): the check finds exactly the stale and the missing and changes nothing by looking; **the reconciliation runs with `0064` not yet applied**, and the test reads the catalogue to show that the guard in force is the old one and that the release policy does not exist; it lowers and raises exactly those, leaves the cancelled request and the requests that agree exactly as they were (every column and the version), moves the version of those it put right and changes no other column; `0064` then applies on the reconciled data and changes no request; the check says the database agrees, and a reconciliation that is asked for does nothing. **The other order is the recovery path and a second test** (a database migrated before it was checked: `0064` repairs nothing, the check finds the same, and the reconciliation works under the guard `0064` installed); a database whose marks already agree is migrated as it is; a database before the manifest tables, and one before the requests, are read for what they are. **The commands are run in the same order, built** (the check exits 2, the reconciliation 0, the migration applies, the check exits 0, and no credential is printed). Unit tests with a scripted connection pin the transaction (the lock, the re-check, the rollback). **The reconciliation needed no change: it acts with no authorization key, which neither guard restricts, so the finding was a gap in the proof and not a defect.** **Break checks of the upgrade: 20 changes, 18 killed**, among them a reconciliation that works only once `0064` is applied (killed by the documented-order test and not by the recovery test, so the first version of the test could not have seen it), one that works only under the old guard (killed by the recovery test alone), a harness that builds the database after `0064` (killed), and a check made as the migration login (killed by the commands); the two survivors are the outer and the inner state filter of the repair, each held by the other (both removed are killed). *The recorded result for `melarc_dev`* (2 November 2026, 13:54 UTC; run read-only against the owner’s database, which holds migrations `0000` to `0063` and one pickup request): **0 confirmed requests looked at, 0 stale, 0 missing, 0 requests that are not confirmed carrying the mark: consistent.** Nothing was reconciled. The check is run again, and recorded here, immediately before `0064` is applied to it, because a result is only as old as the day it was made.

Database files that the change reaches (every pickup file, the notification files, the negative-access and refusal-order sweeps, the registry grants, the slice grants, the reasons, the audit events: 31 files): **1,268 passed**; five files of the alone project (the catalogue, the technical identities, the runtime role, the seed, the provisioning): **317 passed**. (OB-S1-26, a request cancelled after its manifest was dispatched, no longer stops this task: it was decided on 2 November 2026 and is built.)

#### S1.22 — Start the run and arrive at a stop

| | |
|---|---|
| **Needs**| [S1.21](#s121--order-assign-dispatch-and-cancel-a-manifest) |
| **Run**| B D |
| **Operations**| `startRun`, `arriveAtPickupStop` |
| **Criteria**| 14, 16, 80 |

**Builds on.** S1.21; the Rider session, bearer and hosts (`DescribeServers(RIDER_SERVERS)`) of SLICE-000.
**Newly builds.** `startRun` (the assigned rider only, `NOT_ASSIGNED_RIDER`; `DISPATCHED → IN_PROGRESS`, member work to the active context in one change). `arriveAtPickupStop` (`PENDING → ARRIVED`, `MANIFEST_NOT_STARTED` before the start, idempotent, in any order; the recommended next stop is advisory and never enforced). The rider isolation of AC-80: a rider holding a reference to a stop that is not theirs is `NOT_ASSIGNED_RIDER`, never `NOT_FOUND`; a manifest not theirs is `NOT_FOUND`, identical to one that does not exist.
**Files.** `apps/api/src/pickup/run-execution.service.ts`, controllers, tests.
**Evidence.** AC-14, 16, 80; matrix D1 to D3 against the real routes; audit `pickup.manifest.started`, `pickup.stop.arrived`. *Real-operation verified; the Rider app halves complete in SLICE-016.*
**Prerequisites from the S1.20 closeout (2 November 2026).** An act of the rider on a stop locks the request first and the stop second (the order of the cancellation, and of [the one order of locks](../../contracts/state-machines.md#5-pickupstop)) and judges what it finds: a request that is `CANCELLED` and a stop already resolved are refused (`STATE_CONFLICT`), never accepted from an outdated screen, and no notification is relied on. **Superseded in part by what S1.22 built, and settled by the Product Owner’s review of the same day (OB-S1-38, below):** the rider’s act locks the stop alone, because a rider’s context can neither read nor lock the request; a stop skipped by the cancellation is refused by its own state, not by a read of the request, and the database refuses at the end of a transaction a cancelled request that a stop still to be worked holds. The same locking is **not** extended to the collection and the custody acts without a review of their own (S1.24, S1.26).
**Prerequisites from the S1.21 closeout (2 November 2026; documentation only, nothing of `startRun` is built): dispatch is publication and the start is the day** (OB-S1-31; the Product Owner, 2 November 2026; [state-machines.md](../../contracts/state-machines.md) §4). A manifest for a later day may be dispatched in advance, and that only shows its stops to the assigned rider. `startRun` is permitted **on the manifest’s `service_date` on the hub’s clock**, subject to its other rules (the assigned rider, a dispatched manifest), and is refused **before** that day and **after** it has passed, each as `STATE_CONFLICT` told apart by `details.rule` (`service_date_not_reached`, `service_date_passed`; fixed when the operation is contracted). A run already started goes on past midnight: it keeps its original `service_date`, preserves its actual moments (`started_at`, the arrival and resolution moments of its stops), attributes each pickup’s outcome to the local date of its own event, and is not cancelled, completed or redated by the system at midnight. The task builds the refusals and tests each side of the day on the hub’s clock, and a run that crosses midnight.
**Obligations carried.** OB-S1-31 (closed by this task), OB-S1-38 and OB-S1-39 (opened by it, decided by the Product Owner’s review of the same day, built in its correction and closed, below).
**Built (2 November 2026).** `startRun` and `arriveAtPickupStop` (migration `0065`; `pickup-run-execution.service.ts`, `pickup-run-start.controller.ts`, `pickup-stop-arrival.controller.ts`, `pickup-stop-etag.ts`; contract 5.83, now 5.84 after the correction below, and the regenerated client). **`startRun`** is judged under the manifest’s lock, in this order: that the run is the rider’s (`NOT_ASSIGNED_RIDER`); the `ETag` of the run as it is now, stops included (`STATE_CONFLICT`, which a cancelled pickup or any other change to the run causes, and the rider reads the run again); that it is `DISPATCHED` (`STATE_CONFLICT`); and **that its day is today on the hub’s clock** (`STATE_CONFLICT`, `details.rule` `service_date_not_reached` or `service_date_passed`, with the run’s `service_date`). It writes the state and the moment of the start of the manifest **and nothing else**: “all member pickup work moves to the active context in one change” is that one row, because a stop is worked when its manifest is in progress and none is written. One event, `pickup.manifest.started`, in the rider’s name; no message. **`arriveAtPickupStop`** is judged under the stop’s lock: that the stop is the rider’s (`NOT_ASSIGNED_RIDER`); that its run has started (`MANIFEST_NOT_STARTED` for a run that is only dispatched, `STATE_CONFLICT` for one that is over); and that the stop is `PENDING` (`STATE_CONFLICT`). It writes the stop and nothing else, in any order of the stops, and one event, `pickup.stop.arrived`. Its `Idempotency-Key` replays the original answer for a retry; the same key for another stop is `IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_CONFLICT`; another key for a stop already arrived at is `STATE_CONFLICT`. **A rider who acts on work that is not its own is told `NOT_ASSIGNED_RIDER`, and the answer is one for a colleague’s run or stop, a draft, a run cancelled before it went out and an id that matches nothing** (errors-and-enums.md: a rider who *acts* on unassigned work is told so, and one who *looks it up* is told `NOT_FOUND`, as `getPickupManifest` does); the two routes declare no record scope, because the guard would answer a rider `NOT_FOUND`. **A run that crosses midnight** is untouched: its arrivals are today’s moments and its manifest keeps its date, its moments and its state.
**The correction (2 November 2026, on the Product Owner's review; the migration is edited in place because no persistent database has applied it).** *Decisions applied:* OB-S1-38 and OB-S1-39 as the review's table gives them; a rider who acts on work that is not its own is told `NOT_ASSIGNED_RIDER` for an unknown, a foreign and an otherwise inaccessible id alike, and `NOT_FOUND` stays for the reads; `getPickupStop` is built in this task, where the plan places it. *Built:* (1) **the end-of-transaction rule** (`pickup_request_cancelled_open_stop` and `pickup_stop_cancelled_request`: constraint triggers, deferrable and initially deferred, on the request and on the stop): a `CANCELLED` request and a stop that holds it (claim not released) and is `PENDING` or `ARRIVED` never coexist, whoever writes, and the cancellation, which writes the request and then skips the stop, is accepted whole in either order of its statements and refused whole if it leaves the stop behind; Ops is asked for no separate step. (2) **The cancelling key's branch in the stop's guard:** it may skip a stop and nothing else (the state `SKIPPED`, `resolved_at` and a `skip_snapshot` with the reason `REQUEST_CANCELLED`), and the stop keeps its `sequence`, its `released_at` and its `arrived_at` (the key did not hold `sequence` and `released_at`, which the grants of `0060` give the login, and the service never wrote them); the pin of the moment of an arrival now names the planning key alone, because the branch holds it for the cancelling one. (3) **The manifest's guard** refuses `COMPLETED` while a stop is `PENDING` or `ARRIVED`, whoever writes it and before any completion path exists, and refuses the rider's start while none is. (4) **`startRun` refuses a run with nothing left to work,** under the manifest's lock and after the day, with the declared `MANIFEST_EMPTY` (`422`); the run stays `DISPATCHED`, no event is written and nothing is sent. The stops are read after the lock was won, and a cancellation that commits after that read is a cancellation after the start: the two outcomes of a start that meets the cancellation of the last pickup are tested (the start refused with the run still dispatched, or the run going out with the pickup it had and the cancellation then skipping it). (5) **`getPickupStop`** (`PickupManifestsService.getStop`, `pickup-stops.controller.ts`): the stop of the manifest read, from the same function and so the same closed projection, `pickup.read`, with the stop's `ETag` (the token the arrival hands back for the same stop); staff by the hubs of the grant, with `HUB_SCOPE_VIOLATION` from a probe policy (`pickup_stop_probe`) and a record-scope entry for the stop, and an audited crossing of another hub; a rider through its assigned and dispatched run, every other stop answered to it as one that does not exist; a vendor `PERMISSION_DENIED` (OB-S1-23). (6) **Contract 5.84** (the new refusal on `startRun`; `getPickupStop` with its `400` and what a refusal reveals; the arrival's note that the cancellation and the skip are one transaction the database holds), the client regenerated, and the negative-access sweep and the route and OpenAPI inventories moved with it.
**The migration.** `0065` grants `UPDATE ("started_at")` on the manifest and `UPDATE ("arrived_at")` on the stop, adds a policy for each (`pickup_manifest_run`, `pickup_stop_run`: a rider arm only, under `pickup.run.execute`, on a run assigned to the rider and dispatched, with a check that holds the new state), replaces the two guards with a branch for the key (a dispatched manifest to `IN_PROGRESS` with the moment and nothing else; a pending stop to `ARRIVED` with the moment and nothing else, once its run is in progress and the rider’s), and extends the ledger’s rider arm by the two events, about the rider’s own run or stop and under the key of the act. **The independent review of the same day added three holds, in the same migration:** the grant of the moment of an arrival belongs to the runtime login and not to a key, so the stop’s guard refuses a change of `arrived_at` under the two staff keys that reach a stop and are not held to their own columns (the cancellation of a request, the planning), whatever else the same statement writes; each ledger event must carry **the facts of its act**, not only its author and object (the states it moved between, the hub of the object, and exactly the identifiers it relates, which the policy compares with the rows themselves); and both moments are taken with `clock_timestamp()` after the lock is won, as the siblings do (“the lock first, then the instant”), so that a start or an arrival that waited is stamped no earlier than whatever committed meanwhile. No request is read or written by either act, and no table is added. The registry, the state machines, the audit document, the feature and the rider surface say the same of the two acts, and the registry states the three holds. **The correction adds to the same migration:** the cancelling key’s branch of the stop’s guard, the completion and start rules of the manifest’s guard, the two constraint triggers and the stop’s probe policy; the pin of the moment of an arrival now names the planning key alone, because the cancelling key’s branch holds it.
**Checked before the correction (2 November 2026, the first build and the independent review).** Contract: `contract:check` compared the 65 implemented operations and found nothing; `api-client:check` says the client is current with 5.83; `db:check` found nothing. Build, typecheck (four workspaces), lint (`eslint . --max-warnings 0`) and format are clean. The API unit tests of the directories the change reaches (the pickup domain, contract, OpenAPI, routes, audit, authorization, tools, database code, architecture and application tests: 109 files): **3,737 passed, 4 platform skips**. The database files that the change reaches (every pickup file, the audit, migration, policy-structure, refusal-order, registry-grant and slice-grant files, the rider files, the notification files and `commitment-upgrade`: 50 files): **1,809 tests, of which 1,808 passed in the run of the 50 files**; the one failure, an expectation of `pickup-manifest-claim` that the new hold moved, was corrected, and that file passes (65 tests). Among them the three files of this task, **103 tests** (`pickup-run-start` 15, `pickup-stop-arrival` 21, `pickup-run-policies` 67); five files of the alone project (the catalogue, the technical identities, the runtime role, the seed, the provisioning): **317 passed**. A unit test of the stop’s token (13) passes. Two existing files moved with the change and say so: `negative-access-sweep` (the new record kind, the existence rule asked of the two operations, and the writes of `GBT-D2` and `GBT-D3` with the key of the act, with the scope as it is and forged) and `pickup-manifest-claim` (seven columns of a stop and five of a manifest may be updated; and, since the review, a write of the moment of an arrival under the cancelling key is refused by the stop’s guard, `23514`, before the policy’s check, `42501`, which that file expected). The Ops Portal: typecheck clean; its API, pickup and other wire-shape guards (`src/platform`: 29 files, **1,084 passed**) against the regenerated client. The root document and tooling tests: 1,481, **1,478 passed, 0 failed, 3 skipped**; one of them failed first, on the row of the security matrix that the S1.21 round had written with nested parentheses (the repository’s own check does not read them), and the row is rewritten, accurately for the rider’s new writes, with none. **Break checks of this task: 58 changes in two passes, 52 killed and 6 survive.** The first pass was 43 changes to the service, the migration, the ledger arm and the token (37 killed); the 6 survivors are each one layer of a pair that holds the same rule: the policy’s check and the guard on the manifest’s new state, the same on the stop’s, the rider’s own run in the manifest policy’s `USING` (the read policy and the check hold it), the guard’s rider and moment (the policy and the table’s constraint hold them), the stop policy’s own manifest (the manifest’s read policy holds it), and the service’s check of the principal (the route’s declared surface and key hold it). **The pairs that can be removed together inside this migration were, and each is killed** (`M4b`, `PP2`, `PP1`, `P7b`). One mutation I expected to survive was killed instead (`P7`, the guard’s `IN_PROGRESS` rule), because the test names the layer by its error code. **The second pass is the independent review’s: 15 changes, all killed** (the arrival pin removed, and removed for each of the two staff keys alone, and once more against the corrected expectation of `pickup-manifest-claim`; the start and the arrival stamped when the transaction began, which the two lock-wait tests that read the clock as the holder lets go catch; the ledger event’s hub, its states and the identifiers it relates removed for each event; the identifiers compared by containment, which would let one more through; and the hub and state pins of both events removed together). **The lock-wait test kills the removal of the stop’s lock** (`A9`: the stop is held, the arrival is seen waiting, the stop is skipped, and the arrival is a conflict and not a failure), the test of two arrivals at once the double tap, and a skipped stop among the stops of the run the count of those still to be worked (`S8`).
**Checked in the correction (2 November 2026, the final tree, not committed).** Contract: `contract:check` compared the 66 implemented operations and found nothing (`getPickupStop` is the new one); `api-client:check` says the client is current with 5.84; **`pnpm run precheck`, once on the final state, passed 8 of 8** (`db:check`, the client, format, typecheck, build, contract, lint, audit). **Red first:** before any code the new tests were run and 28 tests failed, 27 new ones and the changed expectation of `pickup-manifest-claim`, each for the reason it was written for (a run with every stop skipped was started with `200`, a cancellation that left its stop behind committed, a run with a stop to be worked was completed, the cancelling key's skip carried a changed place or claim, and the stop read answered `404` as an unrouted path); the others, the two orders of an arrival and a cancellation among them, pass on what the application already did and are held by the break checks below. **API unit tests** of the areas reached (109 files): **3,737 passed, 4 platform skips**. **Database files** the change reaches (every pickup file, the audit, migration, policy-structure, refusal-order, registry-grant and slice-grant files, the rider files, the notification files, `commitment-upgrade` and the negative-access sweep: 51 files): **1,865 passed**. The files of this correction: `pickup-run-policies` 100 tests (67 before), `pickup-run-start` 24 (15), `pickup-stop-arrival` 23 (21), `pickup-stop-read` 9 (new) and `pickup-manifest-claim` 65. Five files of the alone project: **317 passed**. The Ops Portal: typecheck clean; its wire-shape guards (`src/platform`: 37 files, **1,235 passed**) against the regenerated client. The root document and tooling tests: **1,478 passed, 0 failed, 3 skipped**; every Markdown link and anchor of 92 files resolves. **Three existing files moved with the change and say so:** `pickup-manifest-claim` (the columns the login holds for other acts, written by the cancelling key, are refused by the stop's guard, `23514`, where the policy's check refused them, `42501`, or accepted them when written together with the skip; each refusal now also shows that the row is whole afterwards), `pickup-manifest-cancellation` (the control of the dispatch's dead-stop recheck could only be arranged by a stop to be worked for a cancelled request, which the database now refuses at the end of the transaction; it is arranged with the constraint triggers off, as only an administrator could, and the same insert is asserted refused) and `negative-access-sweep` (the new addressed read). **Break checks of the correction: 34 changes in two passes, 33 killed and 1 survives.** The changes: the cancelling key's branch (the whole branch; the check that the columns are unchanged; the moment; the reason; the pin of the moment under the planning key), the completion rule of the manifest guard (removed; counting only pending stops; counting every stop), its start rule (removed; counting only pending stops), the end-of-transaction check (on the request side never firing, immediate and not deferred, counting a released stop, counting only pending stops; on the stop side never firing, immediate, and its trigger and function counting a released stop together), the start refusal (removed; counting only pending stops; counting every stop; judged before the day), the stop read (the crossing recorded with the wrong object type or not at all, an extra member, the Vendor surface, no record for the scope probe, the wrong token, the stop not hub-scoped, no probe policy) and the two orders of an arrival and a cancellation (the arrival without the stop's lock, the cancellation without it, the cancellation clearing the arrival with the guard's column check removed). **The one survivor, `C2` (the guard's check that the state becomes `SKIPPED`), is one layer of a pair:** the check that the skip carries its moment refuses the same statements with the same code, and removing both is killed (`C2b`). The changes to the start were run again on the final code after the refusal's code was changed to `MANIFEST_EMPTY`, and each is killed again.
**Findings for the Product Owner.** (1) **OB-S1-38 and OB-S1-39 are decided and closed** by the Product Owner's review of 2 November 2026, as built above and in [§11](#11-decisions-recorded). (2) **The declared error of the empty start.** The review asked for "the appropriate declared error". The catalogue has `MANIFEST_EMPTY`, which the dispatch already answers for the same condition (a manifest with nothing left to work) and which is a `422`, so `startRun` answers it too and its row in errors-and-enums.md now says "dispatch or start"; `STATE_CONFLICT` with a rule, the first choice, is withdrawn. Say if you prefer the other. (3) **The vendor's read of its own stop is not built (OB-S1-23).** The contract's text for `getPickupStop` promises "a vendor only the stop of its own request", and the registry defers that read until the operation that needs it adds its policy. Building it needs a vendor policy on `pickup_stop` through its own request and a decision that is yours: what a vendor may see of a stop (its `sequence` tells it how many pickups come before its own on a run that other vendors share, and the run's identifier names that run). Until then a vendor is `PERMISSION_DENIED` at the guard, as it is for the manifest reads, and the contract's text says so. (4) **The end-of-transaction rule checks what a transaction writes, not what is already there.** A database built by these migrations holds no row it would refuse, since `0059` skipped the stop in the cancellation's own transaction; a row made outside them would be refused at the end of the first transaction that updates it. [MIGRATION_AND_SEEDING.md](../../architecture/MIGRATION_AND_SEEDING.md#55-an-upgrade-check-data-the-migration-cannot-see) gives a read-only query to look first. (5) **A run can still come to have nothing left to work after it has started**, when the office cancels its last pickup: that is a stop turning terminal in a run in progress, and the system completion of S1.23 is evaluated for it; the manifest's guard already refuses any completion while a stop is to be worked. (6) **The stop-lock approach is not extended** to the collection and the custody acts (S1.24 and S1.26 carry the prerequisite to review their own locking). (7) **Source conflicts, resolved as follows; any may be reopened.** The plan's line "a manifest not theirs is `NOT_FOUND`" and `AC-SLICE-001-14` and `-80` with the error table (an act on work that is not the rider's is `NOT_ASSIGNED_RIDER`): the criteria and the table were followed, which the review confirmed, and `NOT_FOUND` stays for the reads. "The cancellation window closes here" (the state machine, the contract and the audit document) against the assignment closing it (MSC-DEC-195, `AC-SLICE-001-12`, built in S1.21): arrival writes nothing to the request, and the older statements are withdrawn. The contract gave `startRun` no `400` and `arriveAtPickupStop` neither a `400` nor a `422`, although their codes are those statuses: added, and `getPickupStop` gets its `400` the same way. (8) **Choices made to keep going, which the Product Owner may change.** Another key for a stop already arrived at is `STATE_CONFLICT` and not a second `200`; a stale or foreign `If-Match` of `startRun` is `STATE_CONFLICT` and a missing one `VALIDATION_FAILED` (as for the dispatch); `startRun` has no `Idempotency-Key` (the contract declares none), so a retry after a lost answer reads the run again and finds it started.
**Not run:** the gate (the owner’s); `pnpm run test:affected` (its selection is still the whole repository); the whole unit and database projects; the browser and end-to-end suites; the verifier; the `two-builds` file and the other alone files; Firefox and WebKit; the hosted and Linux runs. The Rider app halves complete in SLICE-016.
**Does not.** Record a count.
**Stop if.** None beyond the general conditions.

#### S1.23 — Skip a stop; the system completes a run with nothing collected

| | |
|---|---|
| **Needs**| [S1.22](#s122--start-the-run-and-arrive-at-a-stop), [S1.7](#s17--reason-domains-seeds-and-the-fixed-pickup-failure-categories), [S1.12](#s112--the-notification-service-outbox-sms-through-hubtel-receipts-and-the-ops-log-gate-2) |
| **Run**| B D |
| **Operations**| `skipPickupStop` |
| **Criteria**| 74 |

**Builds on.** S1.22; the `PICKUP_STOP_SKIP` reasons (S1.7); the outbox (S1.12).
**Newly builds.** `skipPickupStop` (Ops only; a `PICKUP_STOP_SKIP` code; `STATE_CONFLICT` on a terminal stop; `REASON_NOT_ACTIVE` for another domain's code; the attempt chain unchanged; the vendor notified with the request, the reason and the requested action). The System transition `IN_PROGRESS → COMPLETED` when every stop is terminal and none was collected and no custody handover is `PENDING`, evaluated when a stop turns terminal and when a handover resolves: no handover and no intake.
**Files.** `apps/api/src/pickup/stop-skip.service.ts`, `run-completion.ts`, controllers, tests.
**Evidence.** AC-74 (skip, then the run may complete; a rider is `PERMISSION_DENIED`; a `DELIVERY_FAILURE` code is `REASON_NOT_ACTIVE`); the system completion in both orders against a pending custody handover. *Real-operation verified; AC-78 completes at S1.31.*
**Prerequisites from the S1.20 closeout (2 November 2026).** The skip that the cancellation of a request makes is a stop turning terminal: the system completion is evaluated for it too, and the cancellation's transaction calls the same evaluation once it exists (a run whose last stop to be worked is skipped by a cancellation, with nothing collected, completes by System).
**Prerequisites from S1.22 (2 November 2026; OB-S1-39, decided by the Product Owner’s review).** `startRun` now refuses a run with nothing left to work (`MANIFEST_EMPTY`), so no run goes out empty and the system completion of this task need not be evaluated at the start. A run can still come to have nothing left to work after its start (the office cancels its last pickup), and that is a stop turning terminal in a run in progress: the system completion is evaluated for it, as the prerequisite above says. **The manifest’s guard now refuses `COMPLETED` while any stop is `PENDING` or `ARRIVED`** (migration `0065`), whoever writes it; this task’s completion path relies on that rule and must keep it, because `arriveAtPickupStop` reads its run without locking it and is correct only because a run in progress cannot leave that state while one of its stops is still to be worked.
**Obligations carried.** OB-S1-31 (OB-S1-39 was closed by S1.22’s correction); OB-S1-40 and OB-S1-41 (opened by this task, below).
**Built (2 November 2026).** `skipPickupStop` (`src/pickup/pickup-stop-skip.service.ts`, `.controller.ts`, `-schemas.ts`), `completeRunIfSettled` (`pickup-run-completion.ts`), migration `0066`, contract 5.85, and the Vendor's notice (below). **The act** is judged in this order after the guard has found the stop in a hub of the grant: the retry (`Idempotency-Key`); the note (the ledger must be able to keep it: `VALIDATION_FAILED`); the reason (an `ACTIVE` `PICKUP_STOP_SKIP` code with its note where it requires one: `REASON_REQUIRED`, `REASON_NOT_ACTIVE`, the system's `REQUEST_CANCELLED` and `MANIFEST_CANCELLED` being no catalogue code); then, under the stop's lock, the `ETag` (`STATE_CONFLICT`) and that the stop is still to be worked (`STATE_CONFLICT`: a stop collected, failed or skipped, the one a cancellation skipped among them, is left as it is). It is allowed on a stop of a draft, a dispatched or a started manifest alike (a draft whose request was cancelled cannot be dispatched, and Ops resolves it with this act). **The stop** becomes `SKIPPED` with its moment and the snapshot of the reason and nothing else changes: its place, its arrival and its claim are kept, the request is not written and no attempt is consumed. **The note is Ops's own**: `STOP_COLUMNS` returns a skip without its note to every principal but staff, so no representation a rider reads, and no `ETag` a rider holds, carries it. **The database holds the skip for every writer** (the policy of the key, the guard's rules on the columns, the catalogue reason and the moment of the skip). **The completion** is described in [state-machines.md §4](../../contracts/state-machines.md#4-pickupmanifest): `startRun` locks the stops after the manifest, a stop turning terminal takes the manifest's lock after the stop's and only for a run in progress, and the stops are counted after the lock. **The cancellation of a request** calls the same completion, so the case the S1.20 closeout left open (a run whose last pickup is cancelled) closes. **The Vendor's notice:** for a request with a registered vendor, an envelope for `PICKUP_SKIPPED_VENDOR_FEED` (a feed entry) in the same transaction, and `vendor.pickup_skip.notified` with the reason and `requested_action`: `CORRECT_PICKUP_LOCATION` for a location the rider could not reach and `NONE` for the other three (domain-model.md §3.9, the column "the next action that differs"). The lane copies the declared members of the envelope's payload into the notification's closed `parameters` (migration `0066`: at most four members, a lower-case name and an upper-case token, in jsonpath `strict` mode, since the default mode unwraps a list and let `["TOKEN"]` through; a test found it).
**Evidence.** AC-74: the skip, the run completing, a rider and the second rider who holds custody refused (`PERMISSION_DENIED`), a failure code refused (`REASON_NOT_ACTIVE`), the attempt chain unchanged, the Vendor notified with the request, the reason and the action. AC-78 for the cases without a custody handover. **The "both orders against a pending custody handover" evidence needs the custody table, so it is S1.24's** (`run-custody-handover.db.test.ts`: a skip against the resolution of a pending handover, in either order and at the same moment). Real-operation verified against a real PostgreSQL (see S1.23's bullet in the current position for the files and the break checks).
**Findings for the Product Owner.** (1) **The Vendor's notice wording is a draft** (OB-S1-40): the feed entry names the reason and the action as tokens that the Vendor's screens (SLICE-015) turn into words; the template in `templates.ts` is unapproved. (2) **An individual sender's pickup has no Vendor, so no notice** (OB-S1-41): no document asks for a text to an ad-hoc sender on a skip, and the event of the skip says `NO_VENDOR`. (3) **The skip's note is staff-only**, by the same rule as the note of a cancellation; say if a rider should read it. (4) **The master specification's §31.2 gained the row** for an event `MSC-DEC-412` had already signed, because the list of messages is held to that table. (5) A skip is allowed on a draft or a dispatched manifest's stop; say if it should be refused there (a draft's stop could instead be dropped by cancelling the draft).
**Does not.** Let a rider skip.
**Stop if.** None beyond the general conditions.

#### S1.24 — Run custody handover

| | |
|---|---|
| **Needs**| [S1.23](#s123--skip-a-stop-the-system-completes-a-run-with-nothing-collected) |
| **Run**| B D |
| **Operations**| `initiateRunCustodyHandover`, `acceptRunCustodyHandover`, `listRunCustodyHandovers` |
| **Criteria**| 67, 68 |

**Builds on.** S1.23; the fleet permission keys and the scope row (registry line 131).
**Prerequisite from the S1.22 correction (2 November 2026).** The arrival’s locking (a rider’s act locks the stop alone and relies on the database to keep a cancelled request from coexisting with a stop to be worked) is **not** extended to the custody acts by analogy: this task reviews and states the locks it takes (the run, the stops’ custody, and a second rider’s context for the receiver) and tests them against the acts they meet.
**Newly builds.** The `run_custody_handover` table (domain-model.md §6.8.1). Initiate (Ops, naming another active rider or the hub; custody stays with the first rider until acceptance); accept (the named receiver, recording the count taken, which seeds nothing); the receiver may then complete the run and the first rider is `NOT_CUSTODY_HOLDER`; completed stops stay attributed to the first rider. `failRunCustodyHandover` (provisional name; Ops; `REASON_REQUIRED`; custody unchanged, the run `IN_PROGRESS`). Who may skip afterwards is D-07's.
**Files.** `apps/api/src/fleet/run-custody-handover.*` (the `fleet` domain holds the keys, so the placement rule puts the acts there; an existing domain, not a new one), one migration, tests.
**Evidence.** AC-67 and AC-68 (a pending handover leaves custody and the run unchanged; a failed one leaves custody unchanged; the count taken does not seed the hub count); matrix D rows for the receiving rider's reach. *Real-operation verified.*
**Obligations carried.** OB-S1-42, OB-S1-43 and OB-S1-44 (opened by this task, below).
**Built (2 November 2026).** Migration `0067`, `src/fleet/` (`run-custody-handover.schema.ts`, `-view.ts`, `-schemas.ts`, `.service.ts`, two controllers, `fleet-providers.ts`, `fleet.module.ts`), contract 5.86, `getRunCustodyHandover`, the platform's `record.surfaces`, and the changes to the manifest's guard and the ledger. **The locks, reviewed and stated** (the prerequisite of the S1.22 correction): the custody acts lock the run, then its handover, and the completion by the system takes the run's lock after the stop it has resolved, only for a run in progress ([state-machines.md §4](../../contracts/state-machines.md#4-pickupmanifest) says why none waits in a circle, and tests meet it). **Opening** locks the run first so a handover is never opened on a run being completed, nor a run completed under a handover being opened; the unique index is the backstop. **A rider's act** (the receiving rider's acceptance) cannot read the run by any read operation, so the database shows the receiver the run and its stops under the accepting key alone, while a handover to it stands. **The notes** are Ops's own and in no representation a rider reads; the representation has no note. **The count taken** is written on the handover and nowhere else (the stops and the run are the rows they were, asserted to the last column).
**Evidence.** AC-67 (the stranded run: the transfer, Ops skipping the rest, the receiver refused the skip, the stops worked before kept with their rider, the sender no longer the holder) and AC-68 (a pending handover leaves custody and the run unchanged, a failed one leaves custody unchanged and a further one opens, the count does not seed anything). The completion on a resolution (AC-78's custody clause) in the receiving rider's own transaction, by Ops's failure and by Ops's acceptance for the hub, and the races. Matrix rows `GBT-D13` to `GBT-D16`. Real-operation verified.
**The port (3 November 2026, OB-S1-42 closed).** The build first went on past this block's own "Stop if" with `fleet` importing seven files of `pickup` and `pickup` reading the custody table by name, which I called "the one place" a domain crosses another; both were wrong, and the Product Owner kept F-32. Now: `platform/domain-ports/` defines `RunAccess` (the run for `fleet`: read, lock, version, completion by the system) and `RunCustody` (for `pickup`: whether a handover is pending); `pickup/pickup-run-access.adapter.ts` and `fleet/fleet-run-custody.adapter.ts` implement them; `src/domain-ports.module.ts` wires them; **neither domain imports the other, neither names the other's tables in a statement**, and the generic pieces they shared moved to the platform (`platform/http/contract-headers`, `pagination`, `page-token`; `platform/audit/required-note`). **A port takes the caller's transaction handle and opens none**: the completion still runs in the transaction of the act, after the run's lock, so the order of locks and the atomicity are those of before (shown by the lock tests, the races and the failure tests below). The one thing of `pickup` that `fleet` still names is the run's table schema, for the foreign key of its own table: the data layer's convention (`pickup` does it for riders and vendors, `vendor` for riders). **(1) Other crossings predate this and are not changed (OB-S1-47).** (2) **Three clauses are unmet until S1.31** (OB-S1-44): AC-67's refusal of the first rider's completion as `NOT_CUSTODY_HOLDER`, AC-74's `STOPS_UNRESOLVED` and AC-78's handover of a run the system completed. **What the rider who handed custody over may still do** (OB-S1-43): it still reads the run and **can still arrive at a stop of it**, because arrival is judged on the assignment alone, which a handover leaves as it was. The Product Owner's recommendation of 3 November 2026 is that custody transfer does not silently transfer run execution; the mapping against the state machine, the proposed rules and the conflict they uncover about a run the hub holds (OB-S1-48) are in the findings of this task's handoff, **proposed and not built**, and `NOT_CUSTODY_HOLDER` has no emitter until they are decided. (3) **A handover to the hub is final in this slice:** once Ops has accepted custody for the hub, no rider holds the run and none can be handed it. **The text I wrote here, "the run is closed by Ops (S1.31)", had no basis in the permission catalogue** (`hub.handover.submit` is "R only"): see OB-S1-48. (4) **The destination hub must be the run's own** (`VALIDATION_FAILED` otherwise). (5) **`getRunCustodyHandover` and the list's two filters were added** to the contract; the contract's third principle owes the read an act's `If-Match` comes from. (6) A rider's accepted handover that completes the run is audited by the rider (the ledger's rider arm takes that one event), with `completed_by` `SYSTEM`.
**Does not.** Move cash (the matrix: parcels move, cash does not).
**Stop if.** D-07 leaves the skip authority after a handover unresolved; the acts must change a manifest's completion authority and no document allows a domain to import another (F-32): surface the port. **Met, surfaced and decided (3 November 2026): the Product Owner kept F-32 and asked for the port, which is built (below).**

**Built (3 November 2026).** `apps/ops-web/src/app/pages/manifests/*` (the list, the builder, the detail, the skip page, the sections and dialogs, the rider picker), `components/ui/reorder-list/*`, row selection in `QueueTable`, `src/platform/manifest/*`, `platform/problem/manifest-copy.ts`, the registry, the showcase, and the specs. **The list's filters** (`state` and `service_date`, OB-S1-25) are asked of the server and nothing is filtered in the browser; they live in the address as the pickup queue's do (`?state=…&date=…`), a changed filter starts at the first page, a value in the address that is not one is no filter, and the request never carries an empty or a repeated parameter. An empty result under a filter says what was filtered and offers to clear it; a failed read, a refused filter and a refused page each have their own words. **The custody half** is written from the contract and verified against the stand-in only.
**Findings for the Product Owner.** (1) **The manifest list keeps its place on the pickup queue's terms** (OB-S1-46, closed 3 November 2026): the filters, the pages visited, the scroll position and the focus on the manifest opened, by one mechanism of `platform/pickup/queue-continuity*` that takes the list as a parameter; the pickup queue's page and tests are unchanged (the unit tests of the mechanism and of both lists, and the browser specs of both lists on Chromium, built and in dev (166 manifest and 222 pickup specs, all passing)). The page wiring of the two lists (about 50 lines) is duplicated, and one extracted hook would touch the pickup page (OB-S1-58). (2) **The refusal of a query parameter is spelled two ways** (OB-S1-45), below. (3) The words of the manifest screens are proposed (`manifest-copy.ts`), as the earlier screens' were, for review with the composed screens. (4) The Chromium runs were Chromium only; Firefox and WebKit were not run.
**Does not.** Offer a rider screen.
**Stop if.** None beyond the general conditions.

### Stage 5 — Collection, the handshake and failure

What happens at the door. The order of the count and the handshake is D-04's; the blocks are written for option 1. The stage ends at **Gate 5** (S1.30).

#### S1.26 — `recordCollection`

| | |
|---|---|
| **Needs**| [S1.22](#s122--start-the-run-and-arrive-at-a-stop), [S1.7](#s17--reason-domains-seeds-and-the-fixed-pickup-failure-categories), [S1.12](#s112--the-notification-service-outbox-sms-through-hubtel-receipts-and-the-ops-log-gate-2), [D-04](SLICE-001-READINESS-CHECK.md#d-04-the-order-of-the-count-and-the-handshake-and-the-ad-hoc-direction) |
| **Run**| B D |
| **Operations**| `recordCollection` |
| **Criteria**| 20, 21, 28, 29 |

**Builds on.** S1.22; the idempotency store; the reasons (S1.7); the outbox (S1.12).
**Prerequisite from the S1.22 correction (2 November 2026).** `recordCollection` is the first act of the rider that writes beyond the stop (the collection record, the sender set, the office notification), and it does **not** inherit the arrival’s locking by analogy. This task reviews and states the locks it takes (the stop, the collection record, and what the cancellation of the request does to a stop that has been collected), and tests a collection against a cancellation in both orders, as S1.22 does for the arrival.
**Newly builds.** The `collection_record` table. `recordCollection` (the assigned rider, an `ARRIVED` stop, `Idempotency-Key`, `If-Match`), written for D-04 option 1: the actual count, never the declared one; **zero is `ZERO_COLLECTION_IS_FAILURE`** and creates no record, sender set or handover; a short count needs a variance reason (`REASON_REQUIRED`); `variance` `MATCH` or `SHORT` is recorded on every collection; the shortfall sets `office_notified_at` and writes the office notification in the same transaction; a `PROVISIONAL` ad-hoc sender becomes `REUSABLE` when the count is recorded (AC-28's "physical completion" under option 1), without waiting for the handshake; `captured_at`; a replay returns the original and another body under the same key is `IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_CONFLICT`. The count never seeds the intake's declaration. Under option 1 the stop stays `ARRIVED` until S1.27 closes it, so AC-21's "the stop becomes `PARTIALLY_COLLECTED`" is amended in S1.1 to say when (the office flag still rises at the count); under option 2 the stop closes here. Under option 3 (the overlay) the stop closes here too and S1.27 gates nothing: the state machine's two `handshake VERIFIED` guards and `HANDSHAKE_NOT_VERIFIED` leave in S1.3b, and S1.27 records the confirmation (`pending_vendor`, `confirmed`, `disputed`, `unconfirmed`) beside the closed stop.
**Files.** `apps/api/src/pickup/collection-record.schema.ts`, `collection.controller.ts`, `.service.ts`, one migration (`0068`; the closeout of 4 November 2026 is the forward migration `0069`), tests.
**Evidence.** AC-20, 21, 28, 29; audit `pickup.collection.recorded` (the count's own event, OB-S1-54; `pickup.stop.collected` and `pickup.stop.partially_collected` are written by S1.27 when the handshake closes the stop); matrix D6 (a rider reads only its own collection records). *Real-operation verified; AC-22 completes at S1.31.*
**Obligations carried.** OB-S1-50 to OB-S1-52, OB-S1-55 and OB-S1-59 (opened by this task, by the journey and by the second review, below; OB-S1-53, OB-S1-54 and OB-S1-60 are closed).
**Built (3 November 2026).** As the current position says. **The judging order** (`collection.service.ts`): the principal; a count of zero; the note and the moment (malformed bodies, before anything is read); then in the transaction the retry, the stop under its lock (`NOT_ASSIGNED_RIDER`, one answer), the run (`MANIFEST_NOT_STARTED`, `STATE_CONFLICT`), the stop's `ETag`, that the stop was arrived at and has no record, the evidence, and the count against what the stop declared. **The locks this task takes (the plan's prerequisite):** the stop alone, as the arrival does; no request is read or written by this act; a count that commits first is kept, because the cancellation of the request (which locks the request and then the stop) and Ops's skip look for a record under the stop's lock and are refused; a cancellation that commits first has skipped the stop, which the count then finds is not `ARRIVED`. Both orders are tests (`pickup-collection.db.test.ts`), with the lock-wait helper, and the database refuses the rest (`pickup_stop_collection_kept`). **This act reads no request and no sender** (OB-S1-53, resolved on the independent review of 4 November 2026): the sender's kind fixes the handshake's direction and the individual sender is the request's, and a rider's context reads no request, so the database answers the direction (`melarc.collection_handshake_type`) and makes the promotion (`melarc.promote_collected_sender`, which refuses a stop this rider has not counted) as two narrow operations, and the key's own queries find no request and no sender at any time. The declared count is the stop's own summary. **The record's guard takes the stop's lock before it judges the stop** (the same review): a foreign-key check takes only a share lock on the stop, which an update of the stop's state does not conflict with, so a direct insert and a skip or a failure could each have found the stop eligible and both committed; now they meet in either order, and a record and a skipped or failed stop never stand together. **Corrected again on the second independent review of 4 November 2026:** the promotion operation writes the promotion's event in the change it records, and the ledger admits that event to a statement that runs as the owner alone (the key could set the transaction setting that the first arm trusted, and so write the event for a sender it never promoted, cite another counted stop in it, or write it twice); and the record's guard compares the record's direction with the one `melarc.collection_handshake_type` answers, for a writer under the counting key (the table accepted either word, so a direct insert could store the wrong flow for the request's sender and freeze it).

**Closeout (4 November 2026, on the Product Owner's approval; migration `0069`).** *Approved by the Product Owner on 4 November 2026:* OB-S1-60, OB-S1-54, counted-stop protection (a stop with a positive record is never skipped, failed or cancelled away) and the counted-stop authority of S1.27 (an S1.27 requirement, in [the feature, §5.4](../../features/pickup/pickup-collection.md#54-a-stop-that-has-been-counted)); OB-S1-59 stays an operational-release blocker. **Migration `0069`, forward (not an edit of `0068`).** The record's guard holds the reason of a short count to the `PICKUP_SHORT_COUNT` catalogue at the insert (active; its label and attribution as the catalogue holds them; a note where it asks for one; no photo asked for until S1.28 stores evidence; no member of the writer's own) and refuses with `REASON_NOT_ACTIVE` or `REASON_REQUIRED`, which the act answers as the contract's errors: a catalogue that changed between the act's reading and its insert is a controlled refusal that undoes the act and frees the key, and the same request is then a retry. `recorded_at` and a short count's `office_notified_at` are the database's own instants, taken once the stop's lock is held (a writer that waited for the lock is stamped after it); a flag on a clean count is refused by the table's own check; the device's `captured_at` is kept apart. The ledger's arm for the count admits one event, **`pickup.collection.recorded`** (the same for a clean count and a short one: its object the record; the stop, its run and request, the rider and the hub named; the counts, the variance, whether the flag was raised and the direction of the handshake; `ARRIVED` before and after; no note), with every member bound to the stored record and its stop and every other free-form member absent, and admits `pickup.stop.collected` and `pickup.stop.partially_collected` to the counting key never: S1.27 writes them when the handshake closes the stop. The catalogue is 200 codes (its prose counts of the tiers, 78 and 111, were stale and are corrected to 79, 5 and 116). The act writes the count's event, then the promotion (whose event the database writes), then completes the idempotency record, in one transaction. **The migration history was read first**: every database of the local server was looked at read-only (a read-only transaction each, through the repository's own `checkAgainstApplied`, nothing applied). The owner's `melarc_dev` holds `0000` to `0063` (`0064` to `0068` pending, no history problem), four leftover test databases stop at `0041` or `0057`, one unrelated database is unreadable by the migration login, and **no database holds `0068`**; the forward migration rests on the text of `0068` having been given to two reviewers in archives, whose use cannot be established from here. `melarc_dev` and the running stack were not touched. **Evidence.** The probe of 4 November 2026 is now permanent: every case is the runtime login as the assigned rider under the counting key at an arrived stop of its own run, each refusal is shown to be the guard's stated reason, and the same writer is then accepted on the same stop with the genuine facts. They cover an invented, retired and wrong-catalogue reason, a substituted label or attribution, a fact of the writer's own, a note or photo requirement unmet, a snapshot that is not an object, a label that is the text of a number, a catalogue that changed between the reading and the insert (as a database fact and through the real operation, held between the two by a lock), a reason kept intact when the catalogue is edited later, past and future instants given for the recording and the flag, the instants taken after a real lock wait, and the device's moment kept apart. The ledger is tested member by member for a clean and a short count (each member changed on its own with the rest right, the genuine event then accepted by the same writer), for the writer, the key and the other kinds of caller, for the free-form members, for the correlation id, for a stop that has left `ARRIVED`, and for the two events of the close. The route tests cover the events, the note kept out, a ledger that refuses either of the two events (the whole act, the promotion included, is undone, the same key then makes it all, and a replay after that makes nothing more). **Break checks of the closeout.** The first pass over the migration (36 mutants) killed 30; of the 6 that survived, two were checks the table already makes (removed), three were gaps in my tests (closed) and one is a layered pair. The final run over the migration (35 mutants) killed 34, and its one survivor is that layered pair: the recording rider inside the arm's own `EXISTS`, behind the record's read policy, which shows a rider only its own records and has its own test (the same pair as Q11 of the second review). The 4 mutants of the service and the 3 of the reason mapping were all killed. **Not changed, recorded as OB-S1-61:** the ledger holds no uniqueness for the count's event (a rider with a real record could write the same true event again from direct SQL), and `occurred_at` and `recorded_at` of `audit_event` are writer-supplied for every arm of the insert policy. **Checks on the final tree.** `pnpm run precheck`: 8 of 8. The two collection files (78 tests of the policies, the ledger and the instants, and 32 of the real operation), a wider set of 79 database files (2,792 tests: the pickup, custody, notification, audit, migration, policy, registry, rider, vendor, sweep and slice files) and 4 files that run alone (168 tests: the catalogue invariants, the seed's initialization, the runtime role, provisioning), all passed. The API unit and process projects: 271 files, 7,846 passed, 13 skipped (the platform skips that were already there), none failed. The root document and tooling tests: 1,481, with 1,478 passed, 3 skipped and none failed. The first unit run found one failure that was mine, the security matrix's coverage table (it counts its rows, and the new row `GBT-D20` made Family D 20 and the total 305); it is fixed and the file passes. **Not run:** the rest of the database suite, the browsers, the integrated journeys (none reaches the count's event), the gate and `pnpm run test:affected` (whose selection is the whole repository while its baselines are old: the owner's). **Not built, by the Product Owner's order:** the reconciliation of OB-S1-59.
**Findings for the Product Owner.** (1) **A count above the declared one is refused** (OB-S1-50): the variance is `MATCH` or `SHORT` and no document names an over-count. (2) **No office message is queued for a shortfall** (OB-S1-51): the flag is `office_notified_at` and the event; no message kind, audience or wording is defined. (3) **A photo a reason asks for cannot be given yet** (OB-S1-52). (4) **The rider's key reads no request and no sender** (OB-S1-53, closed 4 November 2026: the first build had widened "a rider is shown no request" to the whole row of the stop it had arrived at; two narrow operations replaced it). (5) **The count has its own event, `pickup.collection.recorded`** (OB-S1-54, **decided 4 November 2026**): `pickup.stop.collected` and `pickup.stop.partially_collected` are reserved for S1.27, when the handshake closes the stop; this block's first assignment of them to the count is superseded. (6) **The count is judged on assignment, as the arrival is**: a rider that has handed custody over can still count at a remaining stop (OB-S1-55, with OB-S1-43). (7) **Refusing the skip, the failure and the cancellation of a counted stop is approved** (the Product Owner, 4 November 2026): a counted stop on a skipped stop would be parcels with no stop, so the count is kept and the stop leaves `ARRIVED` through the handshake. **The independent review of 4 November 2026 approves refusing the ordinary skip, failure and cancellation of a counted stop** (the parcels have been counted and need a traceable resolution) and asks that the separate recovery gap for skipped requests stay visible; **the second review asks the Product Owner to confirm that refusal.** (8) **A custody count and a run that completes as empty** (OB-S1-59): see the row; it **stays an operational-release blocker** (the Product Owner, 4 November 2026).
**Does not.** Run the handshake (S1.27); set a declared count.
**Stop if.** D-04 is not decided.

#### S1.27 — The handshake: code, delivery, verification, fallback and override

| | |
|---|---|
| **Needs**| [S1.26](#s126--recordcollection), [S1.12](#s112--the-notification-service-outbox-sms-through-hubtel-receipts-and-the-ops-log-gate-2), [D-04](SLICE-001-READINESS-CHECK.md#d-04-the-order-of-the-count-and-the-handshake-and-the-ad-hoc-direction), [D-10](SLICE-001-READINESS-CHECK.md#d-10-the-recipient-channel-bar-and-the-heads-up-sms) |
| **Run**| B D |
| **Operations**| `initiateHandshake`, `verifyHandshake`, `requestSmsHandshakeFallback`, `authoriseHandshakeOverride` |
| **Criteria**| 23, 24, 26, 73 |

**Builds on.** S1.26; the linkless SMS kind, the code store, the cap and the cooldown (S1.12); the handshake settings.
**Newly builds.** The handshake record and the states S1.3 fixed. The code (length, lifetime and attempts from the settings, all provisional) is generated by the system when a collection is recorded, stored hashed and never logged. The direction is fixed: the registered vendor enters the rider-displayed code in the Vendor PWA; an ad-hoc sender receives an SMS and the rider enters it. Wrong codes count to `ATTEMPTS_EXHAUSTED`; expiry is `CODE_EXPIRED`, routes to Ops and leaves the physical state unchanged. The SMS fallback goes to the registered number only, capped at three and spaced 60 seconds, enforced from the database per stop (`SMS_FALLBACK_LIMIT_REACHED`); busy is not a trigger. Every operation of the slice that causes an outbound SMS also meets the sub-ceiling `provider_initiating_operation_ceiling_per_minute` (20 a minute per session, inside the authenticated-API bucket), which is seeded and enforced nowhere today. The Ops override takes a reason and records `OPS_OVERRIDE` with its actor. `resolveHandshake` (provisional name; Senior Ops). The channel is recorded on every handshake. The stop closes when the handshake is verified or overridden (option 1; under option 3 the stop is already closed and the handshake only records its confirmation).

**Requirements from the S1.26 closeout (approved by the Product Owner, 4 November 2026).** (1) **The events of the close are this task's.** `pickup.stop.collected` and `pickup.stop.partially_collected` are written when a verified handshake or an authorized override actually moves the stop `ARRIVED → COLLECTED` or `PARTIALLY_COLLECTED`, with their own arm of the ledger, bound to the stored record and handshake (the count's own event is `pickup.collection.recorded`, already built; no arm of the counting key admits the two). An abandoned handshake writes neither. (2) **A counted stop's rider-side handshake belongs to the rider who recorded the count** ([the feature, §5.4](../../features/pickup/pickup-collection.md#54-a-stop-that-has-been-counted)): after a custody transfer that rider may finish it only while its assignment and authorization are still valid; custody alone gives the receiving rider no handshake authority and no run-execution authority; if the recording rider is no longer eligible, the specified recovery or the Ops override applies, with its existing prerequisites and audit; the vendor's confirmation and the individual sender's code are unchanged; **no authority to collect at additional stops is granted** (OB-S1-43 and OB-S1-55 stay open for that). The database holds it for every writer, as the record's other rules are held, and a test names each of the former holder, the receiving rider and Ops separately. (3) **The facts a handshake sets are the database's own**, as the record's are: the instant of `handshake_confirmed_at` is taken from the database's clock once the stop's lock is held, and the channel, the override officer and the instant are written together or not at all (OB-S1-60's rule for the columns the handshake adds).
**Files.** `apps/api/src/pickup/handshake.*`, `handshake.schema.ts`, the SMS kind in `apps/api/src/platform/notifications/`, one migration, tests.
**Evidence.** AC-23 in both directions, AC-24 (every rung logged with its channel), AC-26, AC-73; the code appears in no log, metric, audit event or response but the rider's (sentinel); the cap holds across two processes. *Real-operation verified for the API; the client halves complete in SLICE-015 and SLICE-016.*
**Obligations carried.** OB-S1-06 (a real provider, G3); `OQ-048` (security review of the parameters).
**Does not.** Reverse the direction by configuration; send to any number but the registered one.
**Stop if.** The registered number is not a defined field (F-23).

**Built (4 November 2026, not committed).** *What was built:* as the position bullet says. *The stop condition was met and a value was chosen to keep going, which the owner decides* ([OB-S1-62](#7-deferred-obligations--what-is-owed-and-not-yet-run)): the registered number of the SMS fallback was chosen as the vendor organisation's business contact number (`vendor_organization.contact_phone`), and was replaced in the review closeout of 5 November 2026 by the phone of the vendor's credential (OB-S1-62). *Verified against a real PostgreSQL:* the database level (`collection-handshake.db.test.ts`: the operations as the real rider, vendor, officer and notification lane, the table's guard, privileges and the ledger's refusals, races), the routes (`collection-handshake-routes.db.test.ts`: the primary rung, the ladder, an individual sender's code, the override and the resolution, and a sentinel that the code is stored nowhere), and the real lane end to end (`notification-handshake-lane.db.test.ts`: the envelope the API writes is relayed, the code is made and handed to a capture sender, the handshake is told, a code that events overtook is not sent, a lane that cannot make the code sends nothing, a real provider is held back until the owner approves the words). *Mutation:* a break-check of the migration (see the checks line of the position). *Not built then, built since:* the sub-ceiling for operations that cause a text (OB-S1-65, closed in the review closeout of 5 November 2026); `disputeCollection` (S1.29). *Still not built:* evidence on a manual resolution (S1.28).

|---|---|

**Builds on.** S1.26, S1.18; the evidence store (S1.11); the outbox (S1.12); the worker process.
**Newly builds.** `reportPickupFailure` (the assigned rider, an `ARRIVED` stop): one of the four fixed categories, a required note, optional contact attempts and a **stored** evidence photo, the requirements read from the reason and never hard-coded; the stop becomes `FAILED`, which is terminal and cannot move back; the request enters the Ops decision context; `REFUSED_COLLECTION` is escalation-only; a zero collection arrives here. A busy vendor is no fallback: after `rider_door_wait_minutes` the rider may fail the stop. The assisted Ops review of AC-35 as an audited note on escalation (D-07). **The backstop:** a scheduled lane (D-11) under a `pickup_backstop` capability that escalates a failure undecided beyond `stale_failure_backstop_hours` and records its trigger, leaving no failure silently undecided.
