**Evidence.** AC-25, 30 to 36; the backstop with an aged row (SQL ages it): the request is escalated with its trigger; two workers escalate it once; audit `pickup.stop.failed`, `pickup.request.escalated` (enhanced). *Real-operation verified; the Linux run of the worker lane is owed (L).*
**Obligations carried.** OB-S1-07: the backstop's cadence is the owner's (F-27); a development cadence is recorded as such.
**Does not.** Reschedule a refused collection by ordinary action.
**Stop if.** The cadence or the process of the lane is not decided.

**Built in part (4 November 2026, not committed).** *What was built:* as the position bullet says. *The stop condition was met for the backstop*, and S1.11, which this task was to build with its first user, was not built, so those parts were left whole rather than built to a guess ([OB-S1-66](#7-deferred-obligations--what-is-owed-and-not-yet-run), [OB-S1-07](#7-deferred-obligations--what-is-owed-and-not-yet-run), [OB-S1-05](#7-deferred-obligations--what-is-owed-and-not-yet-run)). *Verified against a real PostgreSQL:* `pickup-failure.db.test.ts` (45 tests: the report and its bookkeeping, each refusal making nothing, whose stop and which run and which state and which version, the retry, terminality, the refused collection through reschedule, extension and escalation, zero collection) and `pickup-failure-policies.db.test.ts` (89 tests: the one writer, the snapshot held to the catalogue rule by rule, the frozen stop, the completion of a run under the key, the ledger's arms row by row); the helpers of the ceiling are unit-tested. *Mutation:* A break-check of the migration (71 mutants: 55 killed; the 16 that survive are checks that stand behind a policy or another check — the stop policy's assigned-rider and state terms and the guard's run terms, held by the policy's check and the rider's read policy; the guard's state move, held by the state machine; the label type, held by the comparison with the catalogue; four checks of a contact attempt that cover one another; the domain filter of the catalogue lookup, held by the reasons policy; the not-found term; the ledger arm's rider term and the completion arm's, held by what a rider can read) and of the code (36 mutants: 33 killed; the 3 that survive are the category list, held by the schema, the attempt bound, held by the schema's maxItems, and the principal check, held by the route's guard). Eleven mutants that the first run found alive each have a test that kills them now. *Acceptance:* AC-SLICE-001-30, -31, -32, -33 and -36 are met at the API; **-34 is not met** (no backstop) and -35 rests on `escalatePickupRequest`'s note as before. *Not built:* [OB-S1-66](#7-deferred-obligations--what-is-owed-and-not-yet-run).

### Stage 6 — Hub handover and receiving

The custody boundary and the blind count. The stage ends at **Gate 6** (S1.35).

#### S1.31 — Hub handover and intake creation

| | |
|---|---|
| **Needs**| [S1.28](#s128--pickup-failure-the-decision-flow-and-the-backstop), [S1.24](#s124--run-custody-handover), [D-03](SLICE-001-READINESS-CHECK.md#d-03-the-two-clients-scope-and-the-riders-technology) |
| **Run**| B D |
| **Operations**| `submitHubHandover` |
| **Criteria**| 17, 18, 22, 39, 44, 45, 75, 78 |

**Builds on.** S1.20 to S1.28; the idempotency store; the outbox.
**Newly builds.** The `pickup_intake` table (domain-model.md §6.6). `submitHubHandover` (the custody holder only, `Idempotency-Key`, `If-Match` on the manifest, required until SLICE-016 decides the offline rule of a queued replay: D-03, F-16): every stop terminal else `STOPS_UNRESOLVED`; **rows exactly the run's collected requests, each once**, else `VALIDATION_FAILED` naming the row; counts pre-filled and correctable, each correction recorded; the declared count stored as given and **never derived from `collected_count`**; the device's `captured_at` kept apart from the receipt time; one intake per row at `AWAITING_COUNT` including a deliberate zero row, none for a request with nothing collected; the run `IN_PROGRESS → COMPLETED` by the handover; `NOT_CUSTODY_HOLDER`; the same key with other rows `IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_CONFLICT`; a handover on a run the system completed `STATE_CONFLICT`. Receiving is a distinct act: nothing is counted or received by this operation. The handover notification to the receive queue.
**Files.** `apps/api/src/hub/hub-handover.*`, `pickup-intake.schema.ts`, `src/hub/hub.module.ts`, `apps/api/src/app.module.ts`, one migration, tests.
**Evidence.** AC-17, 22, 39, 44, 45, 75, 78 and the server half of AC-18 (a replay returns the original, one handover results; the capture time survives); a skewed device clock is arranged and its effect on `captured_at` recorded (F-35). *Real-operation verified; the Rider's queue clauses of AC-18 complete in SLICE-016.*
**Obligations carried.** The bound on a device clock's `captured_at` is an open value (F-35). **OB-S1-44:** the manifest's guard refuses a completion while a custody handover is pending only for a writer whose context can read `run_custody_handover`, so this operation's key must be given that read (the table's read policy lists the keys by name) and a test must show a completion by it refused while a handover is pending; and the three clauses of AC-67, AC-74 and AC-78 that need this operation are unmet until it exists (`NOT_CUSTODY_HOLDER`, `STOPS_UNRESOLVED`, the handover of a run the system completed as `STATE_CONFLICT`).
**Does not.** Count or receive an intake.
**Stop if.** The handover's refusal for a run that is not `IN_PROGRESS` has no declared code.

#### S1.32 — Intake reads and the receive lock

| | |
|---|---|
| **Needs**| [S1.31](#s131--hub-handover-and-intake-creation), [D-13](SLICE-001-READINESS-CHECK.md#d-13-values) |
| **Run**| B D |
| **Operations**| `listHubIntakes`, `getHubIntake`, `acquireIntakeLock` |
| **Criteria**| 37, 77 |

**Builds on.** S1.31; the existence-rule probe for `pickup_intake`.
**Newly builds.** `HubIntakePreCount` and `HubIntakePostCount` as separate response shapes: the pre-count view **has no `rider_declared_count` property**, not even for a zero declaration. `listHubIntakes` filtered to the awaiting-receive queue, hub-scoped, with the run, the rider who handed over, the submission time and the active receiver. `getHubIntake` sets the `ETag`. `acquireIntakeLock`: advisory, taken when no receiver holds it or the holder has been idle past `intake_lock_idle_ttl_minutes` (D-13), refreshed by the holder; a vendor and a rider read no intake.
**Files.** `apps/api/src/hub/hub-intakes.*`, `hub-intake-view.ts`, `hub-intake-etag.ts`, tests.
**Evidence.** AC-37 and AC-77: the declaration is absent from the read, the queue and a captured notification (a script reads every response of the two reads for the property); another hub's intake is not returned; a user without `hub.read` is `PERMISSION_DENIED`. *Real-operation verified.*
**Obligations carried.** None.
**Does not.** Accept a count.
**Stop if.** A read would carry the declaration in any form.

#### S1.33 — The blind count

| | |
|---|---|
| **Needs**| [S1.32](#s132--intake-reads-and-the-receive-lock) |
| **Run**| B D |
| **Operations**| `submitBlindCount` |
| **Criteria**| 38, 43 |

**Builds on.** S1.32; the lock and compare-`If-Match` pattern.
**Newly builds.** `submitBlindCount` (`hub.intake.count`): the body is `physical_count` alone, `additionalProperties: false`, and a declaration in the body is refused (the code F-07 fixes); `Idempotency-Key` and `If-Match`; `AWAITING_COUNT → COUNTED`; the comparison is revealed for the first time in the response (`count_variance` `MATCH`, `SHORT` or `OVER` and `count_difference`); the intake becomes `READY_FOR_ITEMIZATION`, or `RECONCILIATION_REQUIRED` for a variance, a condition finding or **a zero declaration whatever the variance**. Two receivers: one succeeds and the other `STATE_CONFLICT`; the lock does not substitute for the guards.
**Files.** `apps/api/src/hub/blind-count.service.ts`, controllers, tests.
**Evidence.** AC-38 (a count carrying the declaration is refused and nothing is committed) and AC-43 (receiver B sees A; takeover after the idle time; both submit and one wins); audit `hub.intake.counted`, the first moment both counts may legally appear together. *Real-operation verified.*
**Obligations carried.** `intake_parcel_cap` (100) has no refusal code in any document (F-37e): surface it before enforcing.
**Does not.** Record a condition (S1.34).
**Stop if.** The refusal for an intake over `intake_parcel_cap` is wanted and has no code.

A zero declaration is resolved by Senior Ops with a reason; an Ops Staff user, who does not hold the key, is `PERMISSION_DENIED`.

### Stage 7 — Itemization and pricing

Where price first becomes authoritative, and the intake closes. The stage ends at **Gate 7** (S1.40), which is the milestone at which the API and the Ops Portal half of the slice is complete.

#### S1.36 — `itemizeOrder`

| | |
|---|---|
| **Needs**| [S1.34](#s134--condition-osd-and-adjudication), [S1.6](#s16--pricing-calculation-and-the-booking-estimate-pure), [S1.5](#s15--launch-seed-for-the-accra-hub-and-the-address-to-zone-resolver), [S1.11](#s111--evidence-tables-the-object-store-port-and-the-three-operations) |
| **Run**| B D |
| **Operations**| `itemizeOrder` |
| **Criteria**| 47 to 50, 53, 54, 56, 58, 59 |

**Builds on.** S1.6 (the calculation), S1.5 (the zone), S1.11 (evidence), S1.34; the idempotency store; the audit writer.
**Newly builds.** The `order` and `delivery_commitment` tables. `itemizeOrder` (`hub.intake.itemize`, `Idempotency-Key`, `If-Match`): one parcel, one Order; `READY_FOR_ITEMIZATION → ITEMIZING` at the first. The body is closed: no price, total, margin, surcharge or zone; `carrier_cost` is the only money and is accepted only in the third-party mode, stored as an observed value with its waybill evidence. The destination zone is resolved by the server (S1.5), and pricing is blocked when it does not resolve (`SETTING_MISSING`), when the hub has no margin (`MARGIN_NOT_CONFIGURED`), when no size class was chosen (`SIZE_CLASS_REQUIRED`: no default, no derivation; the officer is recorded and an enhanced event names them), when a carrier cost is missing (`CARRIER_COST_REQUIRED`) and when a declared value above the threshold was not declared at booking (`VALUE_DECLARATION_REQUIRED`). Serialized goods need a serial or IMEI; a declared value above the first tier sets `is_high_value`, and a high-value parcel needs stored evidence (`REASON_REQUIRED`). The price of S1.6 is frozen with its components and their hub, key, version and value; the origin zone is inherited from the request once. **The order's initial `DeliveryCommitment` is written atomically at `sequence = 1`**, its SLA clock starting at the pickup-custody time read through the collection record, stop and manifest (`COMMITMENT_SOURCE_UNRESOLVED` and no Order when it cannot be read), and the delivery date is derived. The estimate and the frozen price both stay readable and distinct (D-01).
**Files.** `apps/api/src/hub/itemization.*`, `apps/api/src/pricing/order-pricing.service.ts`, `order.schema.ts`, `delivery-commitment.schema.ts`, one migration, tests.
**Evidence.** AC-47 to 50, 53, 54, 56, 58, 59; a retried call under the same key mints no second Order and no second commitment; audit `hub.intake.itemized`, `pricing.price.calculated`, `pricing.price.locked`, `pricing.size_class.selected` and `pricing.carrier_cost.captured` (both enhanced). *Real-operation verified.*
**Obligations carried.** The delivery commitment's date rules have no criterion (F-37e); this task carries their tests and surfaces the gap.
**Does not.** Gate the order commercially (S1.37); close the intake.
**Stop if.** D-06 or D-02 is undecided; a fee row has no rule.

#### S1.37 — The commercial gate

| | |
|---|---|
| **Needs**| [S1.36](#s136--itemizeorder), [D-12](SLICE-001-READINESS-CHECK.md#d-12-credit-reservation) |
| **Run**| B D |
| **Operations**| none |
| **Criteria**| 65, 66 |

**Builds on.** S1.36; `Order.commercial_state`.
**Newly builds.** At the freeze, the resolved vendor portion is tested **first**: `sender_minor == 0` takes the order to `NO_VENDOR_CHARGE` and the account's overdue status is not consulted (`order.commercial.cleared_no_charge` is recorded); a non-zero portion takes it to `PAYMENT_REQUIRED` while the vendor's allowance is disabled. `CREDIT_RESERVED` and the ledger are SLICE-007's and are not built (D-12). The test reads the resolved amount, never the request's intent: a split that resolves to zero and a parcel-level override that moves the whole fee both reach `NO_VENDOR_CHARGE`.
**Files.** `apps/api/src/pricing/commercial-gate.ts`, tests.
**Evidence.** AC-65 (a recipient-pays order from an `OVERDUE` vendor clears with no reservation and no payment), AC-66; an order in `PAYMENT_REQUIRED` for S1.38. *Real-operation verified.*
**Obligations carried.** OB-S1-08: `CREDIT_RESERVED` and the reservation uniqueness are owed to SLICE-007 (`OQ-005`).
**Does not.** Reserve credit.
**Stop if.** D-12 chooses to build the reservation and the artifact is not supplied.

#### S1.38 — Close the intake, the exception close and the vendor summary

| | |
|---|---|
| **Needs**| [S1.37](#s137--the-commercial-gate) |
| **Run**| B D |
| **Operations**| `closeHubIntake` |
| **Criteria**| 40, 46, 51, 52 |

**Builds on.** S1.37; the registry's vendor-visibility rules.
**Newly builds.** `closeHubIntake`: parity is the itemized order count against the **hub-received** count; `PAYMENT_REQUIRED` orders count; `ORDER_UNPRICED`; `PARITY_NOT_MET`; the approved exception close (Senior Ops, a substantive reason, `parity_exception_id`) as the body D-07 adds; `ITEMIZING → CLOSED`. The closure writes `hub.intake.closed`, emits `intake.completed` as D-07 names it, and produces the vendor-facing summary: a read (`getIntakeSummary`, provisional name) that is field-filtered, so that no blind count, declaration, discrepancy evidence or adjudication can be in it, whatever the code does. A read of an Order (`getOrder`, provisional name) serves the Vendor's `/orders/{id}` route, AC-56's visibly distinct estimate and price, and `pricing.read` for the vendor's own orders, field-filtered the same way.
**Files.** `apps/api/src/hub/intake-closure.*`, `intake-summary.*`, tests.
**Evidence.** AC-40 (declared 10, received 9: closure needs 9 orders and the 10 survives as history), AC-46 (the vendor sees the summary and none of the four; negative tests at the read, a notification and any export), AC-51, AC-52. *Real-operation verified.*
**Obligations carried.** None.
**Does not.** Send the recipient heads-up SMS (D-10).
**Stop if.** The event's consumer is undefined.

**Builds on.** S1.46; the offline design of [rider-android.md](../../surfaces/rider-android.md) lines 72 to 91.
**Newly builds.** The handover screen: one editable row per collected request, pre-filled and correctable, a read-only total, a deliberate zero row. The encrypted local queue under a Keystore AES-256-GCM key, `captured_at` stamped at enqueue, the `Idempotency-Key` minted at enqueue and replayed unchanged, first in first out with one in flight, backoff, three sync states (queued, rejected and actionable, reconciled), a wipe on device loss, a queue lifetime inside the idempotency window. The queued submit carries or omits `If-Match` as SLICE-016 decides ([D-03](#11-decisions-recorded); the recommendation omits it, rider-android.md line 78), and the reconcile path reads `getPickupManifest`, never `listHubIntakes`, which the Rider does not hold. The storage library is chosen here and recorded.
**Files.** `apps/rider-android/app/src/main/.../handover/*`, tests.
**Evidence.** **Gate 9.** AC-18 end to end with the network off and then on: one handover results however often it is retried, the capture time is preserved, a rejected submission is shown and never dropped, and a stop outcome attempted offline shows the no-connectivity state and is not queued; instrumented tests with the network disabled. *Client evidence completing AC-18.*
**Obligations carried.** The criteria for the reconcile and rejected-sync paths are owed (F-35); this task writes them into the feature through S1.1's mechanism or surfaces the gap.
**Does not.** Queue any command but the handover.
**Stop if.** The storage library needs a dependency the repository has not approved.

| | |
|---|---|
| **Needs**| SLICE-001 accepted at API and Ops level, [S1.45](#s145--rider-app-foundation-enrolment-and-sign-in), [S1.47](#s147--hub-handover-and-the-offline-queue-gate-9) |
| **Run**| M |
| **Operations**| none |
| **Criteria**| none |

**Builds on.** S1.45 to S1.47; the owner's hardware-backed handset.
**Newly builds.** Nothing is built. The owner enrols a handset from the QR, signs in, works a run and submits a handover with the network off, and records what happened. It certifies no task and may be produced at any time; if it is not, S1.56 lists it as *deferred*, never as passed (as S0.61).
**Files.** The record in `delivery/planning/SLICE-001-ACCEPTANCE.md`.
**Evidence.** The owner's record. *Client/manual evidence.*
**Obligations carried.** G3 holds the accepted attestation roots, the package name and the signing digest.
**Does not.** Stand in for a handset.
**Stop if.** No eligible handset.

**Newly builds.** One journey for each of the twelve steps of the normal path and one for each row of the exception table, on the real stack, in order: the Ops legs through the Ops Portal, and the Vendor and Rider legs at API level with that actor's own session, their clients being SLICE-015 and SLICE-016. Closed-list entries for hidden state, each with a test.

**Newly builds.** The sweep over every operation of the slice at query, export, notification, file and API: a vendor reaches only its own requests and orders and is told nothing of another's (the answer equals that for an id that matches nothing, in status, code, body and headers); a rider only assigned work; the blind count and the declaration absent from every read, queue, notification payload, export, log, audit event and metric; evidence retrieval refused to the wrong principal; the matrix rows of Families C, D, G, L, M, N, R, SB, U and X that this slice makes real, listed in a table with the test that runs each.

**Does not.** Add a new rule.

**Builds on.** The race harness of SLICE-000 (`concurrency.db.test.ts`, seeds, the server's deadlock count).
**Newly builds.** Races on the real routes: two confirmations of one request; assignment against a vendor's cancellation; two dispatches; a lock takeover against a count; two counts; a handover replayed against a concurrent one; an itemization retried under one key (one Order, one commitment); a close against an itemize; a cancellation with a replacement against the attempt cap; a randomized interleaving that must not deadlock. The instruments are shown to see (a deadlock made on purpose is recognised).
**Files.** `apps/api/test/database/alone/pickup-concurrency.db.test.ts`.
**Evidence.** Recorded seeds and counts; no deadlock; no advisory lock left; every race has exactly one winner where the criterion says so. *Real-operation verified on Windows; Linux is CI's.*
**Obligations carried.** A deadlock is a specification defect for the owner.
**Does not.** Measure capacity (S1.54).
**Stop if.** A deadlock appears.

## 6. Traceability

Generated from the task blocks and verified: each of the inventory's operations that this plan places, and each criterion, appears **exactly once**, at the **parent** task where its real-operation evidence completes. Fixture and client evidence are named in the blocks and in [§7](#7-deferred-obligations--what-is-owed-and-not-yet-run). A criterion is not fully accepted until every mandatory clause has its evidence.

### 6.1 Operations

Placement follows [SOLUTION_ARCHITECTURE.md](../../architecture/SOLUTION_ARCHITECTURE.md) §5: a pickup act lives in `src/pickup/`, receiving and itemization in `src/hub/`, price and corrections in `src/pricing/`, the run custody handover (fleet keys) in `src/fleet/`, evidence and notifications in `src/platform/` behind ports (D-09). All five are existing permission domains or platform folders; none is a new domain.

### 6.2 Criteria

| AC | Criterion | Surfaces | Completes at |
|---|---|---|---|
| 01 | Standard request confirms with an indicative estimate and no charge | Melarc Ops, Melarc Vendor | [S1.15](#s115--confirmpickuprequest); client half: SLICE-015 |
| 02 | Single package is refused | Melarc Vendor | [S1.14](#s114--createpickuprequest-the-ad-hoc-lookup-and-the-saved-location-read); client half: SLICE-015 |
| 03 | One-package exception needs the permission and recipient details | Melarc Ops | [S1.16](#s116--the-one-package-exception) |
| 04 | A late booking queues, it does not fail | Melarc Ops | [S1.15](#s115--confirmpickuprequest) |
| 05 | Vendor cannot self-cancel a confirmed request | Melarc Vendor | [S1.17](#s117--cancellation-and-the-cancellation-charge); client half: SLICE-015 |
| 06 | Reschedule creates a linked clone, never an edit | Melarc Ops | [S1.18](#s118--the-reschedule-chain-the-attempt-cap-and-escalation) |
| 07 | Attempt cap holds without Senior Ops | Melarc Ops | [S1.18](#s118--the-reschedule-chain-the-attempt-cap-and-escalation) |

## 7. Deferred obligations — what is owed and not yet run

**Each row says in its State what has been run and what has not.** A row is an obligation the specifications state; the task that builds the behaviour owes the check, and it is closed only by a recorded run in the environment named, never by a green unit test. Nothing has been run for this plan.

Owed by the owner: the account and bucket, **the first contact of the adapter with a non-production bucket** (a restricted token; never a production one), the bucket's own rules (CORS for a handset's direct upload, a lifecycle rule for objects that were never completed), the malicious-content mechanism (a release gate), the retention period, the launch ceiling and types, and the two enum values defined for the first owner (`SUBJECT_READERS`, `PICKUP_STOP_EVIDENCE`: [domain-model.md §3.6](../../contracts/domain-model.md)) |

Open the Melarc app to see your stops.*, recorded in `templates.ts` with its digest and the Product Owner’s identity as the first two are. | OB-S1-33 | Dependency | `reassignPickupRequestHub` (SLICE-014) must deal with a request that a stop holds. A stop keeps the hub it was made in, and the stop resolution of a cancellation reaches only the stops of the hubs of the actor’s grant, so a request moved to another hub while claimed would leave its stop out of the new hub’s reach. **This blocks `reassignPickupRequestHub`, and any other change of the hub of a request that a stop holds: it is not built until the claim, the scope of the stop, the commitment mark (OB-S1-35) and the guard of migration `0064` have been reconciled in the same change.** Today the database refuses any change of a request’s hub for every writer (the guard of a request) and any change of a stop’s hub (the guard of a stop), and a stop’s hub is its manifest’s by foreign key, so the case cannot arise; the operation that changes the first rule does it in a new migration, and that migration carries the reconciliation. The operation refuses a claimed request, or resolves its stop, when it is built. **The guard of a pickup request (migration `0064`) reads the stops through row-level security, which is complete only while a request and its stops share a hub, so a request that can change hub while claimed must have its stop resolved or be refused first.** A composite foreign key from the stop to the request’s (id, hub) would hold the same-hub rule structurally; it is proposed and not built. **The same holds for the summary of a stop:** it is made once at dispatch and not changed, so any operation that changes the pickup facts of a request while a stop holds it (this one, or an amendment) must decide what it does to the summary | S1.20 second closeout; S1.21; data-scope-registry.md | SLICE-014 | D | **Open, and a blocker of the hub reassignment of SLICE-014**; recorded here |

So the test fails when the browser has too little CPU to run the module graph of a second tab, which is what the configuration says of the dev server. **What is not shown:** what else was running during the original failure (the load was not recorded). **Not done:** the 5 s is not raised (no inflated timeouts) and the test is not skipped. | OB-S1-62 | Decision | **The registered number of the SMS fallback is the phone of the vendor's credential (F-23); resolved by the build on the review of 4 November 2026, awaiting the Product Owner's confirmation.** No retained document said which stored number 'the registered number' is, the plan's stop condition for S1.27 was met on 4 November, and the vendor organisation's business contact number was used to keep going; the review asked for the field, its ownership, its verification and its change to be settled and for delivery to be bound to it. Built (migration `0073`): the number is `VendorCredential.recovery_phone` where the credential's `delivery_channel` is `PHONE`, the number the vendor's own setup and recovery messages go to. **Owned** by the vendor organisation's credential (entered at registration, chosen as the delivery channel by the approver); **verified** by possession (the vendor set its secret from the grant that was texted to it: a credential whose secret was never set has no registered number); **changed** by no operation in this slice (a change is a later slice's authorised, verified act: a grant to the new number); **delivery is bound to it**: the lane reads no table, it executes `melarc.notification_handshake_number()` (the notification capability only, for the request its envelope names, only while an unexpired fallback code is live), the owner-run reads of the account and the credential are bound to the organisation concerned, a vendor with none is `NO_REGISTERED_NUMBER`, and the message log keeps no hint of it. A purpose of its own would need its own custody and rotation; the existing purpose is where a secret that is sent to a person is kept ([SECURITY_DESIGN.md §14.9](../../architecture/SECURITY_DESIGN.md)). Rotation overlaps: a digest is checked under every version held. Consequence: the worker, which holds this key already, can make any handshake code. The review of 4 November 2026 asked for this trust boundary to be decided explicitly before any real send; the closeout of 5 November does not change it | S1.27 | S1.27 | D | Open: the Product Owner's to accept or to ask for a purpose of its own, before any real send |

| OB-S1-65 | Gap | **The sub-ceiling is enforced (closed on 5 November 2026).** The five operations that write a notification envelope declare `providerInitiating: true` (`confirmPickupRequest`, `dispatchManifest`, `skipPickupStop`, `initiateHandshake`, `requestSmsHandshakeFallback`); the authentication step admits the counter right after the session's bucket, on the session, in the limiter's own transaction and never inside the request's (the limiter's rule); the counter is the seventh name of the limiter's closed set and not a bucket (its limit is its own setting, read on every admission; its window is the bucket's). It counts what is admitted and stays counted whatever the operation then answers: a missing token, a stale `If-Match`, a validation failure, a refusal for state and the replay of an idempotent request each take a place, and a request the ceiling refuses takes none and changes nothing. A structural test requires the operations that write an envelope (the TypeScript writer read as a syntax tree, the one SQL function) to be exactly the ones that declare; the routes are proven through the real application (each of the five, one counter for the session, a read and another session untouched, a burst of fourteen concurrent skips of which exactly the limit are done). | OB-S1-68 | Decision | **An extension permits exactly one explicitly authorised next attempt, and says what it did.** For a refused collection (escalation-only, AC-SLICE-001-33) it releases the restriction for that attempt and records that it did (`releases_refused_collection`); for a request at the hub's cap it lifts the ceiling by one and records the ceiling before and after; it may do both. The ceilings are recorded only where one was lifted, and the audit event never presents the request's own attempt number as the ceiling that was in force. The release belongs to the request it was raised for: the replacement that carries the extension so that its attempt can be confirmed is not itself released and needs its own. Limit, for the owner: a request extended before its stop failed as a refused collection cannot be extended a second time (one extension a request, OB-S1-15), so it can only be escalated or cancelled | S1.28; AC-SLICE-001-33; OB-S1-15 | S1.28, review closeout | D | Open: the Product Owner confirms the reach and decides whether the second act is allowed |

It is not a refusal for every reason. | OB-S1-77 | Decision | **The Platform Admin's maker-checker message waits for a server fact.** The interim, accepted by the Product Owner on 5 November 2026: the act is offered to the maker of a profile with a note that only a Platform Admin can use it ([the Ops surface §7](../../surfaces/ops-portal.md#7-page-inventory), the staff-profile row; [§10](../../surfaces/ops-portal.md#10-what-this-surface-must-never-do)), because the session lists permission keys and no tiers and the record does not say whether the exception applies. An exact message ("you may decide this") needs the session or the record to carry that fact: a contract change, specified in a task of its own. **Nothing is changed meanwhile** | the Product Owner's decision of 5 November 2026; [permissions.md §6](../../contracts/permissions.md#6-maker-checker) | to be named | D | Open: waits for a server fact |
| OB-S1-78 | Gap | **Cancelling a request does not close its cut-off decision.** A late booking waiting for the cut-off decision (`cutoff_exception_state` `PENDING_OPS_REVIEW`) that is then cancelled keeps `PENDING_OPS_REVIEW`; nothing clears it. The queue is right and one page is not: the queue counts and lists only confirmed requests (`PICKUP_CUTOFF_REVIEW` is `CONFIRMED` with `PENDING_OPS_REVIEW`, `AC-QSUM-10`), and the Ops Portal reads the late-bookings filter as a refinement of State = Confirmed, so a cancelled request is never offered for the decision; but the request’s own page draws its cut-off fact for any request that has one, so a cancelled late booking still reads “Booked after the hub’s cut-off: waiting for an Ops decision” (the decision itself is not offered: it needs a confirmed request). **The rule owed, for a later task of the pickup domain: cancelling a request should close its cut-off decision.** No data change now | the Product Owner's decision of 6 November 2026; [pickup-request.md](../../features/pickup/pickup-request.md) | to be named | D | Open: a pickup-domain rule for a later task |
| OB-S1-79 | Gap | **The moment a pickup run was cancelled is not stored.** `PickupManifest.created_at` (the table’s own column, contract 5.100.0: run 5, Stage 1.7) lets a run’s history say when it was made, and `assigned_at`, `dispatched_at`, `started_at` and `completed_at` say the other moments; a cancellation leaves no moment of its own on the row (the state `CANCELLED` and the version change, and nothing else), and the audit event `pickup.manifest.cancelled` is not a read the pickup API gives an Ops session. So the history of a cancelled run cannot say when it was cancelled, and the Ops Portal draws no moment it was not given. **Owed, for a later contract task:** either a `cancelled_at` on `pickup_manifest` (a migration, the guard of the state, the view, the ETag and the contract, set in the transaction of `cancelPickupManifest`) or a read of the run’s events; neither is invented now | the Product Owner, 9 November 2026 (run 5, Stage 1.7); [domain-model.md](../../contracts/domain-model.md) §6.3 | to be named | D | Open: recorded here |

### 7.2 Release gates — not acceptance of this slice's code

Each gate must exist before the exposure it names. **None blocks the local walk** (S1.56), and the local walk **waives none of them**. G1 to G5 and the Staging walk are those of [the SLICE-000 plan's §7.2](SLICE-000-IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md#72-release-gates--not-acceptance-of-this-slices-code); this slice adds to **G3** (a real SMS provider and its templates, the evidence store and its malicious-content check and retention, the recipient-channel questions `OQ-048`, `OQ-028`, `OQ-109` as D-10 decides) and to **G5** (the Vendor PWA and Rider app journeys for the clauses of this slice that need a client: owed by SLICE-015 and SLICE-016, not by this plan). The deferrals are only what an owner decision names: the two client slices (with S1.48, the handset), and the release gates.

### 7.3 Inherited from SLICE-000

The owner's open items of [SLICE-000 §1.2](SLICE-000-IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md#12-current-position) stay the owner's and are not this plan's: the full gate on a quiet machine, the hosted evidence, the real handset, the accessibility note, the signature of the acceptance record, the look of the `DatePicker`, and findings 1 to 10 of §7.49 and §7.50 — **finding 1** (`VALIDATION_FAILED` on operations that take a body or a path id) and **finding 4 to 6** (alert definitions) bear directly on S1.2 and S1.53.

### 7.4 The proposed record of the office's contact with a rider (OB-S1-34)

**Status.** The requirement is approved (the Product Owner, 2 November 2026): a separate, append-only record of the office’s contact with the rider after a cancellation. **The design decisions below were approved on the same day (the Product Owner approved the reviewer’s recommendations); the facility itself is not built and not authorized.** No table, operation, permission or grant exists for it, and it stays **a separate bounded implementation task**. It is a prerequisite of *operating* the contact procedure ([pickup-manifest.md](../../features/pickup/pickup-manifest.md) §5.2), not of local backend development and not of S1.22. **The cancellation stays immediate and never waits for the record, for a notification or for an acknowledgement.**

**Who owns unresolved follow-up today.** By the procedure (OB-S1-30): the officer who handles the cancellation owns the contact with the assigned rider when the run is dispatched or in progress; if the rider cannot be reached, that officer escalates to the duty Senior Ops, who owns it from then until the rider acknowledges or someone takes it over. **The platform cannot show who that is, or whether anything is unresolved:** the ownership exists only in the procedure and in the officers’ own working outside the system. The record exists to make it visible.

**Reuse, inspected.** None serves.
- `ContactAttempt` (a rider’s attempt to reach a vendor, inside a pickup failure) holds a time and an outcome word, belongs to a failure, and has the wrong parties.
- `RecipientContactAttempt` (the three recipient checkpoints of an order, [domain-model.md](../../contracts/domain-model.md) §6.12) is the contact of a rider or of Ops with a *recipient*, under a recipient reason catalogue, with an order or a delivery stop as its parent; the counterpart here is a rider and the parents a cancelled request and a pickup run.
- The audit trail records acts and is immutable, but it is no work list: it cannot say what is unresolved or be handed over, and its notes are bounded.
- The message log records what the platform sent, not what an officer did by telephone. The note of the cancellation is one note, written at the cancellation, and later events are not written into it.

**Approved design: one entry per event, `RiderContactEntry`, append-only.**

| What | Decision |
|---|---|
| Linkage | `pickup_request_id` (the cancelled request), `pickup_manifest_id` (the run), `rider_id` (the run’s assigned rider *at the moment of the entry*, copied so that a later reassignment rewrites nothing) and `responsible_hub_id` (the request’s) |
| Who and when | `recorded_by` (the session’s staff identity) and `recorded_at` (the database’s clock): neither is the client’s to send |
| Channel | closed list: `PHONE_CALL`, `SMS`, `WHATSAPP`, `IN_PERSON`. The actual channel is recorded. **Sending is outside this facility:** the record sends nothing, and an `SMS` entry is the officer’s statement that they sent one, never a delivery receipt (the message log holds what the platform sends) |
| Outcome | closed list: `ATTEMPTED`, `ACKNOWLEDGED`, `ESCALATED`. **"SMS sent" and "rider acknowledged" are different facts and are never merged:** an `SMS` entry that is `ATTEMPTED` says the office tried; a provider’s receipt says a text arrived; only an `ACKNOWLEDGED` entry, or later the rider’s own authenticated acknowledgement, says the rider has it |
| Note | a brief note under the ledger’s note rules (no number, address or secret) |
| Escalation recipient | `escalated_to_staff_id`, **a staff identity** (active, whose grant reaches the hub), required for `ESCALATED` and absent otherwise, **with a snapshot of the display name as it was** (`escalated_to_display_name`) so that the history survives a rename or a departure. A free-text name is never the authoritative recipient. The officer picks the recipient from a bounded read of the hub’s officers (an identifier and a display name, nothing else), which belongs to the same task |
| Acknowledgement | `acknowledgement_source` is `OFFICER_REPORTED` for every entry a member of staff makes (the officer reports what the rider said). An acknowledgement by the rider’s **own authenticated session** is another act, made in the rider app (SLICE-016) with its own source (`RIDER_AUTHENTICATED`); no staff operation can write it, and the two are never merged or inferred from each other |
| Correction | an additional entry that names the one it corrects (`corrects_entry_id`) and says why; no update and no delete (no grant for either, and a trigger for every writer); a read shows the entry and its correction |
| Unresolved, handover | not a stored flag: the read derives it from the entries of a request. It is *open* until an uncorrected `ACKNOWLEDGED` entry exists; the officer responsible is the one who made the last uncorrected entry, or the `escalated_to` person of an `ESCALATED` entry, who takes it over by making the next entry in their own name. An open request is listable for the hub |
| Before dispatch | **no mandatory rider-contact workflow before the run is dispatched.** A voluntary entry is permitted where the office did contact the rider the manifest was assigned |

**Permissions, scope, privacy and retention (no grant is added here).**
- **Write: a dedicated permission** (proposed name `pickup.rider_contact.record`), so that follow-up staff need not hold the authority to cancel. *It is a proposed new key: it is not in [permissions.md](../../contracts/permissions.md), in no bundle and granted to nobody.* **Which bundles hold it, at which tier and scope, is an explicit decision still owed**, and the build task does not start without it.
- **Read: `pickup.read`, at the scope of the hub, after the projection is documented explicitly.** When the facility is built, the `pickup.read` row of permissions.md and the entity’s row of the data-scope registry say that the entries are read under it, by whom, and that a vendor and a rider read none. That documentation is part of the build task and is not done here.
- The table is hub-scoped with forced row-level security as the stops are; a vendor reads none, and a rider does not read the officer’s entries (the rider’s own acknowledgement is the rider app’s operation); the runtime login holds `SELECT` and `INSERT` only. No telephone number is stored (the rider’s is on the rider’s record), the note is screened as an audit note is, and the entries appear in no vendor view and not in the message log; the trail gets one event for each entry (`pickup.rider_contact.recorded`, proposed).
- **Retention:** the entries inherit the operational-record retention policy of [domain-model.md](../../contracts/domain-model.md) §8 (per category, configurable, with a legal-hold exemption; the periods are `OQ-028` and are not set). **No duration is invented here.** **Age never makes an entry more widely readable:** an old entry keeps the authorization controls of a new one.

**The minimum build, for a later task the owner authorizes.** API: `recordRiderContact` (`POST`, `Idempotency-Key`; for a request cancelled while a manifest with a rider held it, dispatched or not), `listRiderContacts` (by request or by run, newest first, with the derived status) and the bounded read of the hub’s officers; one migration (the table, its forced policy, the append-only trigger, the new permission, its registry classification); the contract, the regenerated client and the event. Ops: a "Contact with the rider" section on the cancelled request’s page (and on the manifest’s when S1.25 builds it) to list, add and correct entries; the cancellation dialog is unchanged. **Targeted acceptance tests:** an attempt, an acknowledgement and an escalation are each recorded with the server’s time and the session’s officer, the recipient with a snapshot of the name; an `SMS` entry is never an acknowledgement and no staff operation writes `RIDER_AUTHENTICATED`; a holder of `pickup.request.cancel` alone cannot record, and a holder of the new key alone cannot cancel; refused for a vendor, a rider, another key and another hub, and for a request that no manifest with a rider ever held; an update or a delete is refused to the runtime login and by the trigger to every writer; a correction leaves the first entry as it was; the open status and the responsible officer follow the entries; the cancellation succeeds identically with no entry, and its answer, its event and its charge do not depend on the record; nothing is sent to the rider.

**Decisions still owed.** (1) The bundle assignment of the new write key: which bundles, at which tier and scope. (2) The category and period of retention for these entries, when the periods of `OQ-028` are set. Nothing else of the design is open.

## 9. Stop conditions and the task handoff

**Stop conditions** are those of the [runbook](BOOTSTRAP_B0_RUNBOOK.md#7-stop-conditions) and of [the SLICE-000 plan's §9](SLICE-000-IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md#9-stop-conditions-and-the-task-handoff), and also stop the task when: a decision of the readiness check that the task needs is not recorded; a document and the contract disagree and S1.1 has not recorded which governs; a reason code, a zone alias, a batch day or any product value is needed and unset (surface it, never default it); a fee row, a component or a refusal has no stated rule or code; a new tool, package, store, process or capability the repository has not approved is needed; a vendor-visible or rider-visible shape would carry another party's data or the declared count; a deadlock appears in a concurrency run.

**Handoff.** End each task in the runbook's [format](BOOTSTRAP_B0_RUNBOOK.md#8-task-handoff-format), extended as SLICE-000's was:

```text
Task: S1.NN, title
Git: HEAD, branch, and the scope of uncommitted change
Files changed:
Decisions implemented (document and section):
Commands run (exact) and exit codes:
Result: the affected run's closing lines as printed (passed, failed, owed); counts; every skipped test with its reason and the environment it needs
Evidence reached, per criterion: fixture/platform verified, real-operation verified, or client/manual evidence pending
Obligations closed, and obligations carried (OB-S1-nn)
Checks not run, and why (services, browser, Android, platform; the gate, unless the owner asked for it)
Problem or blocker, if any:
Findings the Product Owner must decide: conflicts, provisional values, suggestions
```

| S1.20, the manifest and its claim (1 November 2026) | Approved with changes. (1) Every request of a manifest belongs to one hub in the grant's scope; an all-hub grant never makes a manifest span hubs. (2) Stops are numbered 1 to n in the submitted order. (3) Authorized visibility is established before `ALREADY_MANIFESTED` is revealed: an unknown id and a foreign one are indistinguishable even when the foreign one is claimed, and creation is atomic. (4) A stop keeps its request claimed until an **explicit release** (a manifest's authorized cancellation, or a recovery transition no document specifies yet): a skipped stop does not release it, and no uniqueness rule assumes otherwise. (5) The pool excludes a request awaiting cutoff review, which stays visible in the request queues. (6) The manifest's `service_date` equals every included request's scheduled date and is an operating day; moving work to another day is a cancellation and rebooking or the failed-attempt reschedule, never an edit (**qualified by the closeout row below: the equality became “on or after the request’s promised day”**). (7) A rider sees a manifest only once it was dispatched and only while assigned; a cancelled-after-dispatch or completed run stays visible to that rider, and reassignment moves access at once. (8) A vendor's access to both tables is denied for now as a deferred capability; the registry's vendor read of its own stop (through its request) is unchanged. (9) `responsible_hub_id` is the domain model's `hub_id` (domain-model.md §6.3). (10) Audit follows the request reads: an event for each foreign hub a result actually represents, none for a scoped result that holds only the actor's own | S1.20 as its block now says; OB-S1-21 to OB-S1-23 |
| S1.20, `pickup_summary` and the checks (1 November 2026) | `pickup_summary` is deferred from S1.20 but is a **required dependency of dispatch**: S1.21 resolves the field mapping, `location_note` included, and verifies that the summary gives a rider the authorized information needed to perform the pickup, before any dispatch exposes a stop. `pnpm run test:affected` may be skipped for S1.20. The break checks target the invariants named in the block's evidence, not a count | S1.21's `Stop if`; S1.20's evidence; OB-S1-21 |
| S1.20 contract and workflow closeout (1 November 2026) | Approved. (1) Contract: the missing `403` of `listPickupManifests` and the missing `VALIDATION_FAILED` and `400` of both manifest reads are added, the described schemas follow, and the temporary declaration exceptions are removed. (2) **Version 1 manifest limit: 64 stops**, stated as `maxItems: 64` in the contract and in a constant of its own, independent of the audit writer, tested at 64 and at 65; its approval is recorded once, in OB-S1-28 (closed 2 November 2026). (3) The claim rule is reconciled in the domain model (`released_at`) and the stop and manifest state machines: a skip, a failure and a collection do not release a claim, and a release needs an explicit authorized transition. (4) Rider scope is “assigned and dispatched”, in the registry and the contract, with a manifest cancelled after its dispatch and a completed one staying readable to its rider. (5) The initial stop order is the submitted order. (6) The manifest list stays oldest-first with no filter for this API task; operational filtering and presentation are settled before the manifest UI. (7) The refusal of `manifested` to a non-staff caller is documented in the query contract. (8) **A past service date is not approved by the silence of the specification.** A manifest’s `service_date` is the day the run is **worked**; the request’s `scheduled_service_date` stays the promise; the existing model already holds both, so no field is added. A manifest cannot be dated in the past on the hub’s clock. A request may go on a manifest for its promised day or a later one (carried over, late against its promise, counted in the creation’s audit event) and not on an earlier one. Cancelling and rebooking is the answer to a new promise, not to Melarc’s own delay. (9) **Cancellation after the claim:** the office’s right is not withdrawn, the cancellation does not touch the stop, and dispatch must refuse (`STATE_CONFLICT`) while a stop still to be worked is for a request that is not `CONFIRMED`; the case after dispatch is open (OB-S1-26). (10) The `window-counter` failure of the full unit run is recorded as “passed in isolation; failure in the full run unresolved”. No full gate for this closeout | S1.20’s block; OB-S1-24 to OB-S1-28; S1.21’s prerequisites |

(4) **The commands are run in the documented order, from the build**, with their exit codes and no credential printed.
